The Group Determines the Curve: \(p^k\) torsion in the Lubin-Tate action

The Lubin-Tate action at height \(\class{gk-h}{h}=p^{k-1}(p-1)\), from a curve with its symmetries

Rin Ray · early version, 13 November 2026

In the HTML version of this page every symbol shows its definition on hover.

Abstract
At height \(\class{gk-h}{h}=p^{k-1}(p-1)\), the maximal finite subgroup \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) of the Morava stabilizer group with an element \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) of order \(p^k\) acts on Lubin-Tate space. We model this action by curves: \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) alone produces a curve whose deformations map \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-equivariantly, smoothly and onto Lubin-Tate space. We also describe the action without any curve, from the fixed points of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\), which carry the formal group of local class field theory. One identity, Frobenius \(=(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\cdot\text{unit}\), appears in both.
Let \(p\) be odd and \(\class{gk-h}{h}=p^{k-1}(p-1)\). The Morava stabilizer group at height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\) has a maximal finite subgroup \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\cong\mathbb Z/p^k\rtimes\mathbb Z/(p-1)^2\), containing an element \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) of order \(p^k\), and \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) acts on Lubin-Tate space. We ask two questions about this action, and give two answers.

Can the action be modeled by curves, starting from the group alone? Yes. From \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) we build a curve \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\), and we show \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) implies that \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) has a one dimensional formal group of height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\) as a quotient of the Jacobian. Some deformations of \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) lift this quotient, and we call them block-preserving. Sending such a decorated deformation of curves to that formal group gives a \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-equivariant, formally smooth functor onto the Lubin-Tate stack.

At \(k=1\) this is an equivalence of categories, as in the prequel. At \(k\ge2\) it has fibers: the deformations of the curve that the formal group does not notice.

Can the Lubin-Tate action of the maximal finite subgroup be described without any curve? Yes. The element \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) of order \(p^k\) makes the formal group a module over \(\mathbb Z_p[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\), and the fixed points of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) on Lubin-Tate space carry the formal group of local class field theory. From these fixed points we obtain a presentation of the Lubin-Tate ring with its \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-action.

One identity, Frobenius \(=(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\cdot\text{unit}\), appears in both answers.

What is new, and what is not

We have two aims. The first is a formula for the Lubin-Tate action itself (Main Theorem 1). The second is a model of the Lubin-Tate action of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) by curves: for \(k\ge2\) it is a fibered model (Main Theorem 2). The formula we want is the formula of Hill, Hopkins and Ravenel below (conj:action), in the formulation of Ray in Belmont and Ray.

Unpublished theorem (Hill, Hopkins and Ravenel; the HHR formula, in the formulation of Ray in Belmont and Ray, conj:action).

Let \(\mathbb W=W(\mathbb F_{p^{\class{gk-h}{h}}})\), let \(C_p=\langle g\rangle\) with \(g=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^{p^{k-1}}\), and \(\bar\rho=\mathrm{coker}\big(\mathbb W\to\mathbb W[C_p]\big)\) for the map \(1\mapsto[1]+[g]+\dots+[g^{p-1}]\), with the generators of \(\mathbb W[C_p]\) in degree \(-2\). The element \(x_0\) is the image of \([1]\), \(\Delta=\prod_{i=0}^{p^k-1}\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^ix_0\), and \(I=(p,\ x_0-\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma} x_0,\ \dots,\ \class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^{p^k-2}x_0-\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^{p^k-1}x_0)\).

Then there is a \(\mathbb W[C_{p^k}]\)-module isomorphism

\[\pi_*E_{\class{gk-h}{h}}\;\cong\;\Big(\mathrm{Sym}\big(\mathrm{Ind}_{C_p}^{C_{p^k}}\bar\rho\big)[\Delta^{-1}]\Big)^{\wedge}_{I},\]
Unpublished theorem (HHR) we do not know of a written proof at odd primes; at \(\class{gk-h}{h}=p-1\) the Lubin-Tate action is linear on homogeneous coordinates (Theorem D of Tiny and Unique)
ResultAboutStatus of the idea
Main Theorem 1the ring, without curvesWe describe \(\pi_0E_{\class{gk-h}{h}}\) itself, integrally, not only its linear terms: one orbit of generators, one orbit of relations forming a regular sequence, and the fixed locus \(\mathrm{Spf}\,W[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\). At \(\class{gk-h}{h}=p-1\) the Lubin-Tate action is linear on homogeneous coordinates, so it is projective linear on \(\pi_0\) (Theorem D of Tiny and Unique); for \(k\ge2\) this formula is conj:action.
Main Theorem 2the ring, through curvesWe show that the deformations of an explicit curve determined by \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) map \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-equivariantly and formally smoothly onto Lubin-Tate space, as formal moduli problems. At \(k=1\) this map is an equivalence (the prequel).
\[\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\ \circlearrowright\ \class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\ \xrightarrow{\ \ \class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\ \ }\ \class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\ \circlearrowleft\ \class{gk-Gp}{G'}\]

a deformed curve whose block lifts \(\longmapsto\) its block, a formal group of height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\)
\(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) is formally smooth

Hill, Hopkins and Ravenel have an unpublished argument for the action, and we do not know of a written proof at odd primes.

Main Theorem 1 presents \(\pi_0E_{\class{gk-h}{h}}\) with its \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-action by one orbit of generators and one relation \(\class{gk-frel}{f}\) together with its \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-translates. Our theorem is currently stated in degree \(0\) and neither uses nor proves this formula. We know the constant term of \(\class{gk-frel}{f}\) (it is \(p\) times a unit), the linear term of \(\class{gk-frel}{f}\) modulo \(p\) (a unit multiple of \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{\class{gk-h}{h}-1}e\), up to \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{\class{gk-h}{h}}\class{gk-rhoW}{\rho_W}\)), and the restriction of \(\class{gk-frel}{f}\) to the fixed locus (an Eisenstein polynomial in \(t^{p-1}\), times a unit). What we do not give, for \(k\ge2\), is a closed formula for \(\class{gk-frel}{f}\), and so we do not give an explicit formula for the Lubin-Tate action of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) on \(\pi_0E_{\class{gk-h}{h}}\) or on \(\pi_*E_{\class{gk-h}{h}}\).

We prove Main Theorem 1 using only Lubin-Tate ramification theory and formal \(A\)-modules. I could not have come up with this proof without the spiritual understanding of Main Theorem 2, and of how function field representations and number field representations relate and reflect each other (see Memetic Context: After Lubin).

Main Theorem 2 carries the curve model of Tiny and Unique, constructed solely from \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\), from \(k=1\) to every \(k\ge2\). What changes is told step by step in the first case, \(k=1\), next to general \(k\): four things break when we push past \(k=1\), and the main theorems repair them.

1The questions

Let \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) be a one-dimensional formal group of height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\) over \(\overline{\mathbb F}_p\), a field of characteristic \(p\).

Can the Lubin-Tate action of the maximal finite subgroup be described without any curve? Main Theorem 1 →

For which heights \(\class{gk-h}{h}\) and primes \(p\) does the action of the maximal finite subgroup \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\subseteq\class{gk-Gh}{\mathbb G_h}\) on \(\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\) admit a geometric model? And can it be built uniformly, for all \(\class{gk-h}{h}\) and \(p\) at once? Main Theorem 2 →From the dream: "it would be cool to be able to compute the Lubin-Tate action for any maximal finite subgroup you want"

* Why these heights

From Modeling Group Actions on Stacks, §0.3, Which Finite Subgroups Matter, and Why. Three claims of different strengths; the distinction is the honest advertisement for Theorems D and E [of Tiny and Unique].

  • Claim 1 (group theory). \(\mathbb{G}_h\) contains \(p\)-torsion if and only if \(p-1\mid h\): an element of order \(p\) generates \(\mathbb{Q}_p(\zeta_p)\) inside the division algebra \(D\), and \([\mathbb{Q}_p(\zeta_p):\mathbb{Q}_p]=p-1\). The maximal finite subgroups are classified:

    Lemma 0.1 (Bujard, Hewett). For \(p>2\) and \(h=(p-1)p^{k-1}m\) with \(p\nmid m\), \(\mathbb{G}_h\) has exactly \(k+1\) conjugacy classes of maximal finite subgroups, \[\mathbb{Z}/(p^h-1)\qquad\text{and}\qquad \mathbb{Z}/p^i\rtimes\mathbb{Z}/(p^{h_i}-1)(p-1)\quad(1\le i\le k),\] where \(h_i=h/\varphi(p^i)\). For \(i=k\) the subgroup is \(\mathbb{Z}/p^k\rtimes\mathbb{Z}/(p-1)^2\), in a single conjugacy class.

  • Claim 2 (cohomology). Torsion becomes cohomological structure. Subgroups containing \(\mathbb{Z}/p\) have periodic Tate cohomology, which feeds periodicity classes into every descent spectral sequence \(H^*(G^{\prime},\pi_*E_h)\Rightarrow\pi_*E_h^{hG^{\prime}}\). The shape of Theorem E, a polynomial generator \(\beta\) and an invertible \(\Delta\), is this periodicity at height \(p-1\).
  • Claim 3 (homotopy). For \(2p-2>h^2+h\) the \(K(h)\)-local category is algebraic (Pstrągowski; Barthel, Schlank, Stapleton): no exotic phenomena. So subgroups with \(p\)-torsion are the only possible carriers of non-algebraic behavior. That they always carry something is not a theorem; it is the verdict of every computed case, most directly Nave’s theorem, which is Claim 2 for the wild subgroup \(C_p\) alone. Theorems D and E supply the Claim 2 output for the full \(G^{\prime}\). This is the input any Claim 3 statement at height \(p-1\) must consume.

At \(h=p-1\), the first height with \(p\)-torsion, the maximal finite subgroup is \(G^{\prime}=\mathbb{Z}/p\rtimes\mathbb{Z}/(p-1)^2\), and \(\pi_*(E_{p-1}^{hG^{\prime}})\) is the goal of Tiny and Unique. Heights \(p^{k-1}(p-1)\) for \(k\ge2\) are this paper.

Let a group \(\class{gk-Ggen}{G}\) act on an object \(Y\), so \(\class{gk-Ggen}{G}\subseteq\mathrm{Aut}_\kappa(Y)\). A lift of \(\class{gk-Ggen}{G}\) is an object \(\class{gk-Xgen}{X}\) of a moduli problem \(\class{gk-M}{\mathcal M}\), with \(\class{gk-F}{\mathcal F}(\class{gk-Xgen}{X})\cong Y\), on which \(\class{gk-Ggen}{G}\) acts compatibly. A geometric model asks the induced map \(\class{gk-Ftil}{\widetilde{\mathcal F}}\) on deformations to be a bijection; a fibered model asks only that \(\class{gk-Ftil}{\widetilde{\mathcal F}}\) be formally smooth.

the data: a lift of \(\class{gk-Ggen}{G}\)

\[\begin{array}{ccc}\class{gk-Ggen}{G} & \xrightarrow{\ \ g\,\mapsto\, g_X\ \ } & \mathrm{Aut}_\kappa(\class{gk-Xgen}{X})\\ & \underset{\textstyle\subseteq}{\searrow} & \Big\downarrow{\scriptstyle \class{gk-Fstar}{\mathcal F_*}}\\ & & \mathrm{Aut}_\kappa(Y)\end{array}\]

geometric model

\[\class{gk-Ggen}{G}\ \circlearrowright\ \mathrm{Def}^{\star,\mathcal M}_{\class{gk-Xgen}{X}}\ \xrightarrow[\ \text{a bijection for every }A\ ]{\ \ \class{gk-Ftil}{\widetilde{\mathcal F}}\ \ }\ \class{gk-Dstar}{\mathrm{Def}^{\star,\mathcal N}_Y}\ \circlearrowleft\ \class{gk-Ggen}{G}\]

fibered model

\[\class{gk-Ggen}{G}\ \circlearrowright\ \mathrm{Def}^{\star,\mathcal M}_{\class{gk-Xgen}{X}}\ \xrightarrow[\ \text{formally smooth}\ ]{\ \ \class{gk-Ftil}{\widetilde{\mathcal F}}\ \ }\ \class{gk-Dstar}{\mathrm{Def}^{\star,\mathcal N}_Y}\ \circlearrowleft\ \class{gk-Ggen}{G}\]

(After Modeling Group Actions on Stacks, §Geometric Modelling.) Let \(\class{gk-M}{\mathcal M}\) and \(\class{gk-N}{\mathcal N}\) be prestacks, and let \(\class{gk-F}{\mathcal F}\colon\mathcal M\to\mathcal N\) be a natural transformation. Let \(Y\in\mathcal N(\kappa)\), and let \(\class{gk-Ggen}{G}\subseteq\mathrm{Aut}_\kappa(Y)\) be a subgroup. A lift of \(\class{gk-Ggen}{G}\) to \(\class{gk-M}{\mathcal M}\) is an object \(\class{gk-Xgen}{X}\in\mathcal M(\kappa)\), an isomorphism \(\class{gk-F}{\mathcal F}(\class{gk-Xgen}{X})\cong Y\), which we use to identify \(\mathrm{Aut}_\kappa(\mathcal F(X))\) with \(\mathrm{Aut}_\kappa(Y)\), and a homomorphism \(\class{gk-Ggen}{G}\to\mathrm{Aut}_\kappa(\class{gk-Xgen}{X})\), \(g\mapsto g_X\), such that \(\class{gk-Fstar}{\mathcal F_*}(g_X)=g\) for every \(g\in\class{gk-Ggen}{G}\). Here \(\class{gk-Fstar}{\mathcal F_*}\colon\mathrm{Aut}_\kappa(\class{gk-Xgen}{X})\to\mathrm{Aut}_\kappa(Y)\) is the homomorphism induced by \(\class{gk-F}{\mathcal F}\). Since \(\class{gk-Fstar}{\mathcal F_*}(g_X)=g\), the homomorphism \(g\mapsto g_X\) is injective.

The group \(\class{gk-Ggen}{G}\) acts on \(\class{gk-Dstar}{\mathrm{Def}_{Y}^{\star,\mathcal N}}\) by \(g\cdot(\mathfrak Y,\iota)=(\mathfrak Y,g\circ\iota)\), and on \(\mathrm{Def}_{\class{gk-Xgen}{X}}^{\star,\mathcal M}\) by \(g\cdot(\mathfrak X,\iota)=(\mathfrak X,g_X\circ\iota)\). We write \[ \class{gk-Ftil}{\widetilde{\mathcal F}}\colon \mathrm{Def}_{\class{gk-Xgen}{X}}^{\star,\mathcal M}\longrightarrow \class{gk-Dstar}{\mathrm{Def}_{Y}^{\star,\mathcal N}},\qquad(\mathfrak X,\iota)\mapsto(\mathcal F(\mathfrak X),\mathcal F(\iota)),\] for the induced map of rigidified deformation functors. Both take values in sets of isomorphism classes, on Artinian local \(W\)-algebras \(A\) with residue field \(\kappa\). By functoriality of \(\class{gk-F}{\mathcal F}\), we have \(\class{gk-Ftil}{\widetilde{\mathcal F}}(g\cdot\xi)=g\cdot\class{gk-Ftil}{\widetilde{\mathcal F}}(\xi)\) for every \(g\in\class{gk-Ggen}{G}\) (Modeling, Lemma 3.2). So \(\class{gk-Ftil}{\widetilde{\mathcal F}}\) respects the two actions automatically, and this is not a condition.

A lift \(\class{gk-Xgen}{X}\) of \(\class{gk-Ggen}{G}\) is a fibered model of the action of \(\class{gk-Ggen}{G}\) on \(\class{gk-Dstar}{\mathrm{Def}_{Y}^{\star,\mathcal N}}\) if \(\class{gk-Ftil}{\widetilde{\mathcal F}}\) is formally smooth. That is, for every surjective map \(A\to A_0\) of Artinian local \(W\)-algebras with residue field \(\kappa\), every point of \(\mathrm{Def}_{\class{gk-Xgen}{X}}^{\star,\mathcal M}(A_0)\) and every point of \(\class{gk-Dstar}{\mathrm{Def}_{Y}^{\star,\mathcal N}}(A)\) with the same image in \(\class{gk-Dstar}{\mathrm{Def}_{Y}^{\star,\mathcal N}}(A_0)\) come from one point of \(\mathrm{Def}_{\class{gk-Xgen}{X}}^{\star,\mathcal M}(A)\).

A lift \(\class{gk-Xgen}{X}\) of \(\class{gk-Ggen}{G}\) is a geometric model of the action of \(\class{gk-Ggen}{G}\) on \(\class{gk-Dstar}{\mathrm{Def}_{Y}^{\star,\mathcal N}}\) if \(\class{gk-Ftil}{\widetilde{\mathcal F}}(A)\) is a bijection for every \(A\).

When the fibers are points, a fibered model is a geometric model.

Applying formal smoothness to \(A\to\kappa\) shows that \(\class{gk-Ftil}{\widetilde{\mathcal F}}(A)\) is onto for every \(A\). A geometric model is a fibered model, since a bijection is formally smooth. A fibered model is a geometric model exactly when every fiber \(\class{gk-Ftil}{\widetilde{\mathcal F}}(A)^{-1}(\eta)\), \(\eta\in\class{gk-Dstar}{\mathrm{Def}_{Y}^{\star,\mathcal N}}(A)\), is a single point. When both functors are pro-representable, this holds if and only if \(\class{gk-Ftil}{\widetilde{\mathcal F}}\) is injective on tangent spaces.

Behrens and Hopkins also model the element \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) of order \(p^k\) at the height \(\class{gk-h}{h}=p^{k-1}(p-1)\): as an automorphism of a CM abelian variety at a point of a \(U(1,\class{gk-h}{h}-1)\) Shimura variety, whose \(p\)-divisible group is \(\class{gk-h}{h}\) copies of \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\). Their moduli problem and ours are not related, for dimension reasons: the abelian varieties on the Shimura variety have dimension \(\class{gk-h}{h}^2\), while our curves have Jacobians of dimension \(g_X\). Where our curves do sit: Shimura variety subsection.

At \(k=1\) this is \((p-1)(p-2)/2\) against \((p-1)^2\) (Tiny and Unique, Remark 0.7); at \((p,k)=(3,2)\) it is \(16\) against \(36\).

  • \(p\) is an odd prime, \(k\ge1\), and \(\class{gk-h}{h}=p^{k-1}(p-1)\).
  • \(\class{gk-N}{\mathcal N}\) is formal groups, and \(\mathcal F(\class{gk-Xgen}{X})=\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\), the one-dimensional formal group of height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\) over \(\overline{\mathbb F}_p\).
  • \(\class{gk-Dstar}{\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal F(X)}^{\star,\mathcal N}}=\mathrm{Spf}\,\pi_0\class{gk-E}{E_h}\) is Lubin-Tate space.
  • The group is \(\class{gk-Gp}{G^{\prime}}\), the maximal finite subgroup of the Morava stabilizer group containing an element \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) of order \(p^k\).

Remark (the graded case). Throughout we work in degree 0, with the ring \(\pi_0E_{\class{gk-h}{h}}\) and its Lubin-Tate action. The graded case, the Lubin-Tate action on all of \(\pi_*E_{\class{gk-h}{h}}\), is left for later work.

Throughout, \(p\) is an odd prime. Each theorem states its own range of \(k\).

Theorems: · show as Each proof can also be switched on its own. Full proofs are the text of the tex files.

2The first case, \(k=1\), next to general \(k\)

This is my earlier paper, Tiny and Unique: The Lubin-Tate Action at Height \(p-1\) from the Minimal Curve with Its Symmetries (arXiv 2509.23428). The reader does not need it; here is the story.

  • The curve. Among curves with a faithful action of \(\mathbb Z/p\rtimes\mathbb Z/(p-1)^2\), the least genus is \((p-1)(p-2)/2\), attained only by \(\class{gk-X1}{X_1}\colon y^p-y=z^{p-1}\).
  • Decorate the center. Deform the curve together with the action of the center \(\class{gk-Gc}{G}\cong\mathbb Z/(p-1)\).

    These deformations are the configurations for the map \(f\colon \class{gk-X1}{X_1}\to \class{gk-X1}{X_1}/(\mathbb Z/(p-1))\simeq\mathbb P^1\) with \(p+1\) branch points on \(\mathbb P^1\):

    \(\class{gk-DefX1}{\mathrm{Def}(X_1,G)}\simeq\mathrm{Def}(\class{gk-X1}{X_1}/\class{gk-Gc}{G}=\mathbb P^1,\,p+1)\), \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-equivariantly.

    \(\class{gk-DefX1}{\mathrm{Def}(X_1,G)}\) is pro-represented by \(\class{gk-Wring}{W}[[t_1,\dots,t_{p-2}]]\).
  • The Frobenius eigenvalues. The Frobenius eigenvalues of the curve \(\class{gk-X1}{X_1}\) are Gauss sums, and Stickelberger's theorem gives their slopes.
  • The block splits off. The center splits the \(p\)-divisible group of the Jacobian into summands by characters; \(H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathrm{Jac}(\class{gk-X1}{X_1})[p^\infty])^{\chi}\), for \(\chi\) a multiplicative character of \(\mathbb Z/(p-1)\), is the one of dimension one, and defines a formal group \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_{p-1}}\) over the field \(\kappa\) of height \(p-1\).
  • The equivalence. Sending a deformed curve to that summand is a \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-equivariant equivalence \(\class{gk-DefX1}{\mathrm{Def}(X_1,G)}\simeq\mathrm{Def}(H_{p-1})\), on the nose.

    So the Lubin-Tate action of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) is the action of the automorphisms of the curve on its deformations, and in fact includes into the action of \(\Sigma_p\) on \(\mathrm{Def}(\mathbb P^1,p+1)\) fixing the point at infinity.
    \[\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\ \circlearrowright\ \class{gk-DefX1}{\mathrm{Def}(X_1,G)}\ \xrightarrow[\ \simeq\ ]{\ \ \widehat{\mathcal F}^1\ \ }\ \mathrm{Def}(H_{p-1})\ \circlearrowleft\ \class{gk-Gp}{G'}\]
  • The reduced regular representation. The action of \(\Sigma_p\) on \(\mathrm{Def}(\mathbb A^1,p)\) gives a representation of the Lubin-Tate ring as the reduced regular representation.
  • The one identity. \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) acts on the summand through \(\mathbb Z_p[\zeta_p]\), and \(\pi=\zeta_p-1\) satisfies \(\pi^{p-1}=-p\cdot(\text{unit})\): Frobenius is \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\) times a unit.
    The case \(k=1\) controls the tower. → The identity at \(k=1\), \(\pi^{p-1}=-p\cdot(\text{unit})\), carries up to every \(k\) as \(F=(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}-1)\cdot\text{unit}\) on the block (lem:F2), in the same way that Liu and Wan's \(T\)-adic sums carry the \(k=1\) slopes of Gauss sums up to every \(k\) (lem:Dw).
  • Computations. From the model: \(\pi_*E_{p-1}\) as a \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-module, its Tate cohomology, and the detection of Ravenel's elements.

What changes at \(k\ge2\). Four things break, and this paper is about how to repair them. 1 The group no longer picks one curve: it picks a connected family of curves of least genus, and \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) is its simplest member. 2 The Frobenius eigenvalues of the curve are no longer Gauss sums. 3 The formal group no longer splits off the Jacobian: it comes as a quotient. 4 The curve has more deformations than the formal group: the functor is onto but not injective, and the extra directions are the ghosts \(\class{gk-ghost}{\mathfrak g}=\ker d\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) (just the tangent space of the fiber of the functor).

The same story at \(k\ge2\), step by step, with each repair and where to find it.

  • 1 The curve. This is the first thing that breaks. Among curves with a faithful action of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\cong\mathbb Z/p^k\rtimes\mathbb Z/(p-1)^2\), the curves of least genus form a connected family, not one curve. The repair: \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\colon\wp(y_0,\dots,y_{k-1})=(z^{p-1},0,\dots,0)\) is the simplest member of the family, and its first coordinate \(y_0^p-y_0=z^{p-1}\) is \(\class{gk-X1}{X_1}\) (Theorem thm:curve).
  • Decorate the center. Nothing breaks here. Deform the curve together with the action of the center \(\class{gk-Gc}{G}\cong\mathbb Z/(p-1)\). These deformations are the configurations for the map \(f\colon \class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\to \class{gk-Xk}{X_k}/\class{gk-Gc}{G}=\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k}\) with branch points \(\class{gk-B}{B}=\class{gk-B0}{B_0}\cup\{\class{gk-Qinf}{Q_\infty}\}\): the \(p^k\) points of \(\class{gk-B0}{B_0}\cong \class{gk-WkFp}{W_k(\mathbb F_p)}\), one orbit of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\), and the point \(\class{gk-Qinf}{Q_\infty}\). So \(\class{gk-DefXG}{\mathrm{Def}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\class{gk-Gc}{G})}\simeq \class{gk-DefYB}{\mathrm{Def}(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k},\class{gk-B}{B})}\), \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-equivariantly (the setup of Main Theorem 2).
  • 2 The Frobenius eigenvalues. This is the second thing that breaks. The Frobenius eigenvalues of the curve \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) are no longer Gauss sums. The repair: a \(T\)-adic argument, after Liu and Wan, carries the \(k=1\) slopes up to every \(k\) (lem:Dw).
  • 3 The block does not split off. This is the third thing that breaks. The center no longer cuts out the formal group. The repair: it comes as a quotient, \(\mathrm{Jac}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k})[p^\infty]\twoheadrightarrow \class{gk-HX}{H_X}\), of height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\), not as a summand (the setup of Main Theorem 2).
  • 4 The equivalence becomes a map with fibers. This is the fourth thing that breaks. The curve has more deformations than the formal group. The repair: keep the deformations along which the block lifts, \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\subsetneq \class{gk-DefYB}{\mathrm{Def}(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k},\class{gk-B}{B})}\). Sending a deformed curve to its block is a \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-equivariant, formally smooth map \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\colon \class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\to \class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\) onto Lubin-Tate space, and the ghosts \(\class{gk-ghost}{\mathfrak g}=\ker d\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) are just the tangent space of a fiber (Main Theorem 2).
    \[\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\ \circlearrowright\ \class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\ \xrightarrow[\ \text{formally smooth}\ ]{\ \ \class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\ \ }\ \class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\ \circlearrowleft\ \class{gk-Gp}{G'}\]
  • The induced representation. The Lubin-Tate ring \(\pi_0E_{\class{gk-h}{h}}\) has a presentation from the induced representation \(\class{gk-rhoW}{\rho_W}=\mathrm{Ind}_{\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle}^{\class{gk-Gp}{G'}}\tilde{\class{gk-chic}{\chi}}\): one orbit of \(p^k\) generators, and one relation with its \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-translates. The proof uses no curve: it starts from the fixed points of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) on Lubin-Tate space, \(\mathrm{Spf}\,W[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\) (Main Theorem 1).
  • The one identity. \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) acts on the block through \(\mathbb Z_p[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\), and \(\pi=\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}-1\) satisfies \(\pi^{\class{gk-h}{h}}=p\cdot(\text{unit})\): Frobenius is \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\) times a unit on the block.
  • Computations. For \(k\ge2\) the formula for the Lubin-Tate action on homogeneous coordinates is conj:action. Assuming conj:action at \((p,k)=(3,2)\), Eva Belmont and I computed the \(E_2\)-page of the homotopy fixed point spectral sequence for \(\pi_*(E_6^{hC_9})\), toward the Kervaire invariant problem at \(p=3\) (Belmont and Ray, arXiv 2507.10157).

This paper does not replace the prequel at \(k=1\): there the curve is unique, the equivalence is on the nose, the moduli are global, and the homotopy is computed.

Main Theorem 1 · The Lubin-Tate \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-action, on the nose

Question 1Can the Lubin-Tate action of the maximal finite subgroup be described without any curve?

Answer: Main Theorem 1Yes. The element \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) of order \(p^k\) makes the formal group a module over \(\mathbb Z_p[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\), and the fixed points of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) on Lubin-Tate space carry the formal group of local class field theory. From these fixed points we obtain a presentation of the Lubin-Tate ring with its \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-action: one induced representation of generators, one relation and its \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-translates.

Main Theorem 1 (thm:onthenose). Let \(p\) be odd and \(k\ge1\). Let \(\class{gk-Wring}{W}=W(\overline{\mathbb F}_p)\) be the ring of Witt vectors of \(\overline{\mathbb F}_p\), and let \(\class{gk-rhoW}{\rho_W}=\mathrm{Ind}_{\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle}^{\class{gk-Gp}{G'}}\tilde{\class{gk-chic}{\chi}}\) be the representation of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) induced from the Teichmüller lift \(\tilde{\class{gk-chic}{\chi}}\) of the conjugation character \(\class{gk-chic}{\chi}\) (given by \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\class{gk-tau}{\tau}^{-1}=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^{a}\), \(\class{gk-chic}{\chi}(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})=a\bmod p\)). As a \(\class{gk-Wring}{W}\)-module \(\class{gk-rhoW}{\rho_W}\) is free of rank \(p^k\) with basis \(e,\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}e,\dots,\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^{p^k-1}e\), so \(\class{gk-Symh}{\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_W}(\class{gk-rhoW}{\rho_W})=\class{gk-Wring}{W}[[x_0,\dots,x_{p^k-1}]]\) with \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}x_i=x_{i+1}\). Then there is a \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-equivariant isomorphism \[\pi_0\class{gk-E}{E_h}\ \cong\ \class{gk-Symh}{\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_W}(\class{gk-rhoW}{\rho_W})\big/\big(\class{gk-frel}{f},\ \class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\class{gk-frel}{f},\ \dots,\ \class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^{\class{gk-nn}{n}}\class{gk-frel}{f}\big),\qquad \class{gk-nn}{n}=p^{k-1}.\] Moreover:

  1. (i) the generators are one orbit: \(x_i\mapsto\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^i\class{gk-xtop}{x}\) for a generator \(\class{gk-xtop}{x}\), a \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\)-eigenvector at the top of the chain \(\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}/\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}^2\),
  2. (ii) the relations are one orbit: \(\class{gk-frel}{f},\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\class{gk-frel}{f},\dots,\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^{\class{gk-nn}{n}}\class{gk-frel}{f}\) is a regular sequence, with \(\class{gk-frel}{f}(0)\in p\class{gk-Wring}{W}^\times\) and \(\class{gk-frel}{f}\) invariant under \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\),
  3. (iii) on the fixed locus, \(\class{gk-xtop}{x}\) becomes a uniformizer of \(\class{gk-Wring}{W}[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\) and \(\class{gk-frel}{f}(t,\dots,t)\) is an Eisenstein polynomial in \(t^{p-1}\) times a unit.
♡ every odd \(p\), every \(k\ge1\)

From one small, rigid piece, the fixed points of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) on Lubin-Tate space (a Lubin-Tate formal module over \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\)), the whole ring of Lubin-Tate space gets a \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-equivariant presentation: one induced representation of generators, one relation and its \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-translates. This result does not use the curve.

The setup, step by step

From the group to the ring. Click a step.

→→→→

The group

Fix an odd prime \(p\) and \(k\ge1\). Put \(\class{gk-h}{h}=p^{k-1}(p-1)\) and \(\class{gk-nn}{n}=p^{k-1}\), so \(p^k=\class{gk-h}{h}+\class{gk-nn}{n}\). Let \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) be the one-dimensional formal group of height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\) over \(\kappa\), and let \(\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\cong\class{gk-OD}{\mathcal O_D}^\times\) be its group of automorphisms. The group \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\subseteq\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\) is the maximal finite subgroup containing an element \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) of order \(p^k\). The element \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) generates \(\class{gk-Cs}{C}\cong\mathbb Z/p^k\), and \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) with \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\class{gk-tau}{\tau}^{-1}=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^a\); the character \(\class{gk-chic}{\chi}(\tau)=a\bmod p\) records this.

Fact (cited: Hewett, Bujard). Such a group \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) exists, and \(p^k\) is the largest \(p\)-power order of an element at height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\).

The element \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) is the new \(p^k\)-torsion at this height; everything below is built from it.

Lubin-Tate space

Definition (deformations, Lubin-Tate space). For an Artinian local \(W\)-algebra \(A\) with residue field \(\kappa\), a deformation of \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) to \(A\) is a pair \((H,\class{gk-iota}{\iota})\), where \(H\) is a formal group over \(A\) and \(\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\colon H\otimes_A\kappa\xrightarrow{\sim}\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\). An isomorphism of deformations is an isomorphism of formal groups compatible with the identifications \(\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\). Let \(\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}(A)\) be the set of isomorphism classes of deformations of \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) to \(A\).

Definition (the Lubin-Tate action). An element \(g\in\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\) sends a deformation \((H,\class{gk-iota}{\iota})\) to \((H,g\circ\class{gk-iota}{\iota})\). This is the Lubin-Tate action of \(\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\) on \(\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\), and by restriction the Lubin-Tate action of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\).

Let \(R=\pi_0\class{gk-E}{E_h}=\class{gk-Wring}{W}[[u_1,\dots,u_{h-1}]]\), with maximal ideal \(\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}=(p,u_1,\dots,u_{h-1})\). The cotangent space \(\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}/\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}^2\) has dimension \(\class{gk-h}{h}\): one line for each deformation parameter \(u_i\), and one for \(p\).

Fact (cited: Lubin and Tate). The functor \(\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\) is represented by \(\mathrm{Spf}\,R\). So the Lubin-Tate action of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) on \(\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\) is an action on the ring \(R\).

This is the ring Main Theorem 1 describes, with its \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-action.

The fixed locus of σ

Let \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}=R/\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)R\bigr)\), where \(\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)R\bigr)\) is the ideal generated by all \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}r-r\).

Fact (lem:fixedfunctor). \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}\) represents the deformations fixed by \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\).

This is the one small, rigid piece the whole ring is built from.

Formal \(\mathcal O_L\)-modules

Definition (formal \(\mathcal O_L\)-module). Let \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}=\mathbb Z_p[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\) and \(\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O}=\class{gk-Wring}{W}[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\). A formal \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\)-module over an \(\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O}\)-algebra \(A\) is a one-dimensional formal group \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\) over \(A\) together with a ring map \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\to\mathrm{End}(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G})\), \(b\mapsto[b]\), such that \([b](X)=bX+\cdots\), where \(b\) also denotes its image in \(A\). Its \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\)-height is the height of \([\class{gk-unif}{\zeta_{p^k}-1}]\), so that the \(\mathbb Z_p\)-height is \(\class{gk-h}{h}\) times the \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\)-height.

Definition (Lubin-Tate formal group). Let \(K\) be a local field with ring of integers \(\mathcal O_K\), uniformizer \(\pi\), and residue field of size \(q\). A Lubin-Tate formal group for \(\pi\) is a formal \(\mathcal O_K\)-module \(F\) over \(\mathcal O_K\) with \([\pi]_F(X)\equiv\pi X\) modulo degree 2 and \([\pi]_F(X)\equiv X^q\) modulo \(\pi\). Example: for \(K=\mathbb Q_p\) and \(\pi=p\), the multiplicative formal group \(X+Y+XY\), with \([p](X)=(1+X)^p-1\).

Fact (cited: Drinfeld, Prop. 4.2). Deformations of a formal \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\)-module of \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\)-height \(N\) are represented by \(\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O}[[t_1,\dots,t_{N-1}]]\). At height one there are no deformations.
Fact (cited: Lubin and Tate). For each uniformizer a Lubin-Tate formal group exists and is unique up to isomorphism; adjoining its \(\pi^j\)-torsion gives a totally ramified abelian extension.

Where \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) acts, \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\) acts, and the height measured over \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\) drops from \(\class{gk-h}{h}\) to one.

The generators

Definition (the representation \(\rho_W\)). Let \(\tilde{\class{gk-chic}{\chi}}\) be the Teichmüller lift of the conjugation character \(\class{gk-chic}{\chi}\). Let \(\class{gk-rhoW}{\rho_W}=\mathrm{Ind}_{\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle}^{\class{gk-Gp}{G'}}\tilde{\class{gk-chic}{\chi}}\), with generating vector \(e\) on which \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) acts by \(\tilde{\class{gk-chic}{\chi}}(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})\). As a \(\class{gk-Wring}{W}\)-module it is free of rank \(p^k\) with basis \(e_i=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^ie\), \(i\in\mathbb Z/p^k\), and \(\class{gk-Symh}{\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_W}(\class{gk-rhoW}{\rho_W})=\class{gk-Wring}{W}[[x_0,\dots,x_{p^k-1}]]\) with \(x_i\) the variable for \(e_i\); here \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}x_i=x_{i+1}\) and \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}x_i=\tilde{\class{gk-chic}{\chi}}(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})x_{ai}\).

Definition (a generator). A generator of \(R\) is an element \(\class{gk-xtop}{x}\in\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}\) such that \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\class{gk-xtop}{x}=\tilde{\class{gk-chic}{\chi}}(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})\class{gk-xtop}{x}\) and the class of \(\class{gk-xtop}{x}\) in \(\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}/\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}^2\) is not in \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)(\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}/\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}^2)\), that is, it is the top of the chain. Given a generator \(\class{gk-xtop}{x}\), its translates \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^i\class{gk-xtop}{x}\), \(i\in\mathbb Z/p^k\), are the generators, and \(\varphi\colon\class{gk-Symh}{\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_W}(\class{gk-rhoW}{\rho_W})\to R\) is the \(\class{gk-Wring}{W}\)-algebra map with \(\varphi(x_i)=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^i\class{gk-xtop}{x}\).

Fact (thm:onthenose (1), (2)). A generator exists: average any lift of the top of the chain over \(\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle\). For every generator, \(\varphi\) is a \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-equivariant surjection.

The source of the presentation: a power series ring with a linear action.

Definition 1 (The group). Fix an odd prime \(p\) and \(k\ge1\). Put \(\class{gk-h}{h}=p^{k-1}(p-1)\) and \(\class{gk-nn}{n}=p^{k-1}\), so \(p^k=\class{gk-h}{h}+\class{gk-nn}{n}\). Let \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) be the one-dimensional formal group of height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\) over \(\kappa\), and let \(\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\cong\class{gk-OD}{\mathcal O_D}^\times\) be its group of automorphisms. The group \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\subseteq\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\) is the maximal finite subgroup containing an element \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) of order \(p^k\). The element \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) generates \(\class{gk-Cs}{C}\cong\mathbb Z/p^k\), and \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) with \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\class{gk-tau}{\tau}^{-1}=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^a\); the character \(\class{gk-chic}{\chi}(\tau)=a\bmod p\) records this.

Fact (cited: Hewett, Bujard). Such a group \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) exists, and \(p^k\) is the largest \(p\)-power order of an element at height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\).

Definition (deformations, Lubin-Tate space). For an Artinian local \(W\)-algebra \(A\) with residue field \(\kappa\), a deformation of \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) to \(A\) is a pair \((H,\class{gk-iota}{\iota})\), where \(H\) is a formal group over \(A\) and \(\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\colon H\otimes_A\kappa\xrightarrow{\sim}\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\). An isomorphism of deformations is an isomorphism of formal groups compatible with the identifications \(\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\). Let \(\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}(A)\) be the set of isomorphism classes of deformations of \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) to \(A\).

Definition (the Lubin-Tate action). An element \(g\in\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\) sends a deformation \((H,\class{gk-iota}{\iota})\) to \((H,g\circ\class{gk-iota}{\iota})\). This is the Lubin-Tate action of \(\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\) on \(\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\), and by restriction the Lubin-Tate action of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\).

Definition 2 (Lubin-Tate space). Let \(R=\pi_0\class{gk-E}{E_h}=\class{gk-Wring}{W}[[u_1,\dots,u_{h-1}]]\), with maximal ideal \(\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}=(p,u_1,\dots,u_{h-1})\). The cotangent space \(\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}/\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}^2\) has dimension \(\class{gk-h}{h}\): one line for each deformation parameter \(u_i\), and one for \(p\).

Fact (cited: Lubin and Tate). The functor \(\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\) is represented by \(\mathrm{Spf}\,R\). So the Lubin-Tate action of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) on \(\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\) is an action on the ring \(R\).

Definition 3 (The fixed locus of σ). Let \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}=R/\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)R\bigr)\), where \(\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)R\bigr)\) is the ideal generated by all \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}r-r\).

Fact (lem:fixedfunctor). \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}\) represents the deformations fixed by \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\).

Definition 4 (formal \(\mathcal O_L\)-module). Let \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}=\mathbb Z_p[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\) and \(\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O}=\class{gk-Wring}{W}[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\). A formal \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\)-module over an \(\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O}\)-algebra \(A\) is a one-dimensional formal group \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\) over \(A\) together with a ring map \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\to\mathrm{End}(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G})\), \(b\mapsto[b]\), such that \([b](X)=bX+\cdots\), where \(b\) also denotes its image in \(A\). Its \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\)-height is the height of \([\class{gk-unif}{\zeta_{p^k}-1}]\), so that the \(\mathbb Z_p\)-height is \(\class{gk-h}{h}\) times the \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\)-height.

Definition 5 (Lubin-Tate formal group). Let \(K\) be a local field with ring of integers \(\mathcal O_K\), uniformizer \(\pi\), and residue field of size \(q\). A Lubin-Tate formal group for \(\pi\) is a formal \(\mathcal O_K\)-module \(F\) over \(\mathcal O_K\) with \([\pi]_F(X)\equiv\pi X\) modulo degree 2 and \([\pi]_F(X)\equiv X^q\) modulo \(\pi\). Example: for \(K=\mathbb Q_p\) and \(\pi=p\), the multiplicative formal group \(X+Y+XY\), with \([p](X)=(1+X)^p-1\).

Fact (cited: Drinfeld, Prop. 4.2). Deformations of a formal \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\)-module of \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\)-height \(N\) are represented by \(\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O}[[t_1,\dots,t_{N-1}]]\). At height one there are no deformations.

Fact (cited: Lubin and Tate). For each uniformizer a Lubin-Tate formal group exists and is unique up to isomorphism; adjoining its \(\pi^j\)-torsion gives a totally ramified abelian extension.

Definition 6 (the representation \(\rho_W\)). Let \(\tilde{\class{gk-chic}{\chi}}\) be the Teichmüller lift of the conjugation character \(\class{gk-chic}{\chi}\). Let \(\class{gk-rhoW}{\rho_W}=\mathrm{Ind}_{\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle}^{\class{gk-Gp}{G'}}\tilde{\class{gk-chic}{\chi}}\), with generating vector \(e\) on which \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) acts by \(\tilde{\class{gk-chic}{\chi}}(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})\). As a \(\class{gk-Wring}{W}\)-module it is free of rank \(p^k\) with basis \(e_i=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^ie\), \(i\in\mathbb Z/p^k\), and \(\class{gk-Symh}{\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_W}(\class{gk-rhoW}{\rho_W})=\class{gk-Wring}{W}[[x_0,\dots,x_{p^k-1}]]\) with \(x_i\) the variable for \(e_i\); here \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}x_i=x_{i+1}\) and \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}x_i=\tilde{\class{gk-chic}{\chi}}(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})x_{ai}\).

Definition 7 (a generator). A generator of \(R\) is an element \(\class{gk-xtop}{x}\in\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}\) such that \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\class{gk-xtop}{x}=\tilde{\class{gk-chic}{\chi}}(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})\class{gk-xtop}{x}\) and the class of \(\class{gk-xtop}{x}\) in \(\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}/\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}^2\) is not in \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)(\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}/\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}^2)\), that is, it is the top of the chain. Given a generator \(\class{gk-xtop}{x}\), its translates \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^i\class{gk-xtop}{x}\), \(i\in\mathbb Z/p^k\), are the generators, and \(\varphi\colon\class{gk-Symh}{\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_W}(\class{gk-rhoW}{\rho_W})\to R\) is the \(\class{gk-Wring}{W}\)-algebra map with \(\varphi(x_i)=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^i\class{gk-xtop}{x}\).

Fact (thm:onthenose (1), (2)). A generator exists: average any lift of the top of the chain over \(\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle\). For every generator, \(\varphi\) is a \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-equivariant surjection.

Remark (the relation \(f\)). For \(k\ge2\) the relation \(f\) is not given by a closed formula. We know its constant term \(f(0)\in pW^\times\), its linear term, and its restriction \(f(t,\dots,t)=E(t)\cdot\)unit to the fixed locus. At \(k=1\) the ring is already given by Theorem D of Tiny and Unique, so the case \(k=1\) is not new.

The proof in three steps

Step 1. The fixed locus is uniformized by a Lubin-Tate torsion point

Key ideas:

the coinvariant ring

Definition (Coinvariant ring). For a ring \(R\) with an action of \(\class{gk-Cs}{C}=\langle\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\rangle\), the coinvariant ring is \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}=R/\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)R\bigr)\), where \(\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)R\bigr)\) is the ideal generated by all \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}r-r\), \(r\in R\): the largest quotient of \(R\) on which \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) acts trivially.

formal A-modules

Definition (Formal \(A\)-module). Let \(A\) be the ring of integers of a finite extension of \(\mathbb Q_p\) (for us \(A=\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\)) and \(B\) an \(A\)-algebra. A formal \(A\)-module over \(B\) is a one-dimensional formal group \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\) over \(B\) together with a ring map \(A\to\mathrm{End}(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G})\), \(b\mapsto[b]\), such that \([b](X)=bX+\cdots\), where \(bX\) uses the image of \(b\) in \(B\).

Definition (\(A\)-height). Let \(\pi_A\) be a uniformizer of \(A\) and \(q\) the size of its residue field. Over a field of characteristic \(p\), \([\pi_A](X)=uX^{q^N}+\cdots\) with \(u\neq0\); the number \(N\) is the \(A\)-height.

Fact (height and degree) Hazewinkel; see Salch, arXiv:1004.4941, Prop. 2.4

If \(A\) has degree \(d\) over \(\mathbb Z_p\), a formal \(A\)-module of \(A\)-height \(N\) has \(\mathbb Z_p\)-height \(dN\).

In words. Measuring the height over the bigger ring divides it by the degree.

Used in thm:fixedlocus: \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) has \(\mathbb Z_p\)-height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\) and \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\) has degree \(\class{gk-h}{h}\), so as a formal \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\)-module it has \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\)-height one (lem:Omodule (c)).

Drinfeld’s lemmas

Lemma 1 (rigidity) Drinfeld, Prop. 4.1; on this page lem:drinfeldrigid, proof recalled

Let \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G},\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}^{\prime}\) be one-dimensional formal groups over \(A\) whose reductions to \(\kappa\) have finite height. If a homomorphism \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\to\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}^{\prime}\) reduces to \(0\) over \(\kappa\), then it is \(0\).

In words. A map between deformations is determined by what it does over \(\kappa\). So \(\mathrm{End}(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G})\to\mathrm{End}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\) is injective.

Used in lem:lift: the lift \(\class{gk-sigG}{\sigma_{\mathcal G}}\) of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) is unique.

Lemma 2 (deformations of formal modules) Drinfeld, Prop. 4.2; cited

Let \((\class{gk-Hh}{H_h},\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma})\) be a formal \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\)-module over \(\kappa\) of \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\)-height \(N\). Its deformations to \(\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O}\)-algebras \(A\) are represented by \(\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O}[[t_1,\dots,t_{N-1}]]\).

In words. Height \(N\) means \(N-1\) deformation parameters: the same count as Lubin-Tate space, which has \(\class{gk-h}{h}-1\) for height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\). At height one there are none: over each \(\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O}\)-algebra \(A\) there is exactly one deformation, up to a unique isomorphism.

Used in thm:fixedlocus, with \(N=1\) by lem:Omodule.

Source: V. G. Drinfeld, Elliptic modules (1974).

the Lubin-Tate formal group

Definition (Lubin-Tate series). Let \(K\) be a local field with ring of integers \(\mathcal O_K\), uniformizer \(\pi\), and residue field of size \(q\). Let \(\mathcal F_\pi\) be the set of power series \(f\in\mathcal O_K[[X]]\) with \(f(X)\equiv\pi X\) modulo degree 2 and \(f(X)\equiv X^q\) modulo \(\pi\). Example: \(f(X)=\pi X+X^q\).

Theorem (Lubin and Tate) Lubin and Tate 1965; Riehl, senior thesis, Prop. 4.1, 4.3, 4.4

For each \(f\in\mathcal F_\pi\) there is a unique formal group law \(F_f\) over \(\mathcal O_K\) with \(f\in\mathrm{End}(F_f)\). Moreover \(a\mapsto[a]_f\) is an injective ring map \(\mathcal O_K\to\mathrm{End}(F_f)\) with \([\pi]_f=f\), so \(F_f\) is a formal \(\mathcal O_K\)-module, and \(F_f\cong F_g\) for all \(f,g\in\mathcal F_\pi\).

In words. Each uniformizer \(\pi\) has one Lubin-Tate formal group, up to isomorphism; on it, multiplication by \(\pi\) is \(\pi X\) to first order and Frobenius modulo \(\pi\).

Used in thm:fixedlocus: for \(K=\mathbb Q_p\) and \(\pi=p\) it is \(\widehat{\mathbb G}_m\).

Example (the multiplicative formal group). For \(K=\mathbb Q_p\), \(\pi=p\), and \(f(X)=(1+X)^p-1\in\mathcal F_p\), the Lubin-Tate formal group is \(\widehat{\mathbb G}_m\), with group law \(X+Y+XY\) and \([a](X)=(1+X)^a-1\).

Lubin-Tate torsion

Definition (Torsion points). For \(f\in\mathcal F_\pi\), let \(\text{И}_{f,j}\) be the roots of \([\pi^j]_f=f\circ\cdots\circ f\) (\(j\) times) in the maximal ideal of an algebraic closure of \(K\), and \(K_{\pi,j}=K(\text{И}_{f,j})\).

Theorem (Lubin and Tate) Riehl, senior thesis, Prop. 5.2 and Thm. 5.3

\(\text{И}_{f,j}\cong\mathcal O_K/\pi^j\) as \(\mathcal O_K\)-modules, and \(K_{\pi,j}/K\) is totally ramified and abelian of degree \((q-1)q^{j-1}\), with \(\mathrm{Gal}(K_{\pi,j}/K)\cong(\mathcal O_K/\pi^j)^\times\).

In words. Adjoining the \(\pi^j\)-torsion of the Lubin-Tate formal group gives a totally ramified abelian extension.

Used in thm:fixedlocus.

Example (our case). For \(K=\mathbb Q_p\), \(\pi=p\), \(f(X)=(1+X)^p-1\): \([p^k]_f(X)=(1+X)^{p^k}-1\), so \(\text{И}_{f,k}=\{\zeta-1:\zeta^{p^k}=1\}\) and \(K_{p,k}=\mathbb Q_p(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}})\), of degree \((p-1)p^{k-1}=\class{gk-h}{h}\). So the uniformizer \(\class{gk-unif}{\zeta_{p^k}-1}\) of the fixed locus is a primitive \(p^k\)-torsion point of \(\widehat{\mathbb G}_m\), and \(\class{gk-Wring}{W}[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\) is \(\class{gk-Wring}{W}\) with it adjoined. (The thesis does not work out \(k\ge2\); this is the standard cyclotomic computation.)

Two formal groups, not to be confused. \(\widehat{\mathbb G}_m\) has height one over \(\mathbb Z_p\); its torsion point \(\class{gk-unif}{\zeta_{p^k}-1}\) is the coordinate of the fixed locus. The universal \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-fixed deformation, the formal group over \(\mathrm{Spf}\,\class{gk-Wring}{W}[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\) classified by the fixed locus, has height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\) over \(\mathbb Z_p\) and \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\)-height one.

picture of the fixed locus

We consider the fixed locus of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) on \(\mathrm{Spf}\,\class{gk-Wring}{W}[[u_1,\dots,u_{h-1}]]\). It represents the \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-fixed deformations of \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\), and it is \(\mathrm{Spf}\,\class{gk-Wring}{W}[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\). Its uniformizer \(\class{gk-unif}{\zeta_{p^k}-1}\) is a primitive \(p^k\)-torsion point of \(\widehat{\mathbb G}_m\), the Lubin-Tate formal group of \(\mathbb Q_p\) for \(\pi=p\).

Proofs

Lemma: fixedfunctor · Part (a) of thm:fixedlocus: the coinvariant ring \(R_C\) represents the \(\sigma\)-fixed deformations · Proved

Lemma (Part (a) of thm:fixedlocus). Let \(R=\pi_0\class{gk-E}{E_h}\cong\class{gk-Wring}{W}[[u_1,\dots,u_{h-1}]]\) be the Lubin-Tate ring, with \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) acting on \(R\) through its action on \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\). For \(A\in\mathrm{Test}_W\) let \(\mathrm{Def}^\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}(A)\subset\mathrm{Def}(A)\) be the set of points fixed by \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\), and let \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}=R/\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)R\bigr)\) be the coinvariant ring, where \(\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)R\bigr)\) is the ideal of \(R\) generated by all \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}r-r\). Then \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}\in\mathrm{Test}_W\) and \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}\) represents \(\mathrm{Def}^\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\).

A point of Lubin-Tate space with values in \(A\) is a map out of \(R\), and \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) moves the point by precomposition. So a point is fixed exactly when the point cannot tell \(r\) from \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma} r\), and the fixed points are the points of the coinvariant ring \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}\).

The proof in steps

1
A point is a map out of the ring.
2
A fixed point cannot tell \(r\) from \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma} r\).
3
The quotient \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}\) is again a ring in \(\mathrm{Test}_W\).
Proof.

Here \(\kappa=\overline{\mathbb F}_p\), \(\class{gk-Wring}{W}=W(\kappa)\) is its ring of Witt vectors, \(\class{gk-h}{h}=p^{k-1}(p-1)\), \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) is the one-dimensional formal group of height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\) over \(\kappa\), \(\mathfrak m\) is the maximal ideal of \(R\), \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) is the element of order \(p^k\) in \(\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\), and a deformation of \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) over \(A\) is a pair \((\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G},\class{gk-iota}{\iota})\) of a formal group \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\) over \(A\) and an isomorphism \(\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\colon\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\otimes_A\kappa\to\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\).

Step 1 · A point is a map out of the ring. Recall that \(\mathrm{Test}_W\) is the category of complete noetherian local \(W\)-algebras with residue field \(\kappa\). Lubin-Tate space is \(\mathrm{Spf}\,R\), so for \(A\in\mathrm{Test}_W\) a point \(y\in\mathrm{Def}(A)\), that is, a deformation of \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) over \(A\) up to isomorphism compatible with \(\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\), is a local \(W\)-algebra map \(y\colon R\to A\). The group acts on the space by acting on the ring: \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\cdot y\) is \(y\) composed with the automorphism of \(R\) induced by \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\).

Step 2 · A fixed point cannot tell \(r\) from \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma} r\). The point \(y\) is fixed when \(y(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma} r)=y(r)\) for every \(r\in R\). So \(y\) is fixed if and only if \(y\) kills every \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma} r-r\), that is, if and only if \(y\) factors through \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}=R/\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)R\bigr)\).

Step 3 · The quotient \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}\) is again a ring in \(\mathrm{Test}_W\). The ideal \(\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)R\bigr)\) is proper: since \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) fixes \(W\) and preserves the maximal ideal \(\mathfrak m\) of \(R\), and \(R=W+\mathfrak m\), every \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma} r-r\) lies in \(\mathfrak m\), so \(\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)R\bigr)\subset\mathfrak m\). A quotient of \(R\) by a proper ideal is again a complete noetherian local \(W\)-algebra with residue field \(\kappa\), so \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}\in\mathrm{Test}_W\), and since a fixed point cannot tell \(r\) from \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}r\), the ring \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}\) represents \(\mathrm{Def}^{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}\).

Lemma: lift · Part (b) of thm:fixedlocus: a deformation is \(\sigma\)-fixed exactly when \(\sigma\) lifts to it, and the lift is unique · Proved

Lemma (Part (b) of thm:fixedlocus). A class \([(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G},\iota)]\in\mathrm{Def}(A)\) is fixed by \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) if and only if there is an automorphism \(\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}\) of \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\) with \(\iota\circ\overline{\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}}=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\circ\iota\). Such a \(\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}\) is unique, and it satisfies \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}(\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}})=0\) in \(\mathrm{End}(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G})\).

A deformation is fixed by \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) exactly when \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) can be carried along to the formal group over \(A\). A deformation remembers its endomorphisms from the special fiber (rigidity), so \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) can be carried along in only one way, and the lift satisfies every polynomial identity that \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) satisfies in \(\mathrm{End}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\), in particular \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma})=0\).

The proof in steps

1
The class is fixed by \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) exactly when \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) lifts to an automorphism of the deformation: an isomorphism from the deformation to its \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-twist is the inverse of a lift.
2
Two lifts differ by an endomorphism that reduces to \(0\) over \(\kappa\), and such an endomorphism vanishes by rigidity (Lemma lem:drinfeldrigid). So the lift is unique.
3
Since \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) is a root of \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}\) in \(\mathcal O_D\), the polynomial \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}\) evaluated at the lift reduces to \(0\) over \(\kappa\), and rigidity again makes it vanish.
Proof.

Here \(A\in\mathrm{Test}_W\) has residue field \(\kappa=\overline{\mathbb F}_p\), \((\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G},\class{gk-iota}{\iota})\) is a deformation of \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) over \(A\), so that \(\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\colon\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\otimes_A\kappa\to\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) is an isomorphism, \(\bar f\) is the reduction over \(\kappa\) of an endomorphism \(f\) of \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\), \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) is the element of order \(p^k\) in \(\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\), and \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}\) is the \(p^k\)-th cyclotomic polynomial. A lift of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) is an automorphism \(\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}\) of \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\) with \(\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\circ\overline{\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}}=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\circ\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\).

Since \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) sends \([(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G},\class{gk-iota}{\iota})]\) to \([(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G},\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\circ\class{gk-iota}{\iota})]\), the class is fixed exactly when there is an isomorphism \(f\colon(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G},\class{gk-iota}{\iota})\to(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G},\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\circ\class{gk-iota}{\iota})\), that is, an automorphism \(f\) of \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\) with \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\circ\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\circ\bar f=\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\). Putting \(\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}=f^{-1}\) gives \(\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\circ\overline{\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}}=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\circ\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\), and conversely any such \(\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}\) gives the isomorphism \(f=\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}^{-1}\).

For uniqueness, let \(\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}_1\) and \(\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}_2\) be two lifts of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\). Over \(\kappa\) both reduce to \(\class{gk-iota}{\iota}^{-1}\circ\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\circ\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\), so their difference \(\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}_1-_{\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}}\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}_2\), an endomorphism of \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\) because \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\) is commutative, reduces to \(0\) over \(\kappa\), and it vanishes by rigidity (Lemma lem:drinfeldrigid).

It remains to see that the lift is a root of \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}\). In \(\mathrm{End}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})=\mathcal O_D\), the maximal order of the central division algebra \(D\) over \(\mathbb Q_p\) of invariant \(1/h\), the element \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) has order exactly \(p^k\), and \(\mathbb Q_p(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma})\subset D\) is a field, being a commutative subalgebra of a division algebra; so \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) is a primitive \(p^k\)-th root of unity and \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma})=0\). The endomorphism \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}(\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}})\) of \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\) therefore reduces to \(0\) over \(\kappa\), and rigidity again gives \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}(\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}})=0\).

Lemma: drinfeldrigid · Rigidity: a map of deformations that vanishes over \(\kappa\) is zero (Drinfeld) · Proved

Lemma (Rigidity). Let \(A\in\mathrm{Test}_W\) and let \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\), \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G'}\) be one dimensional formal groups over \(A\), such that the reduction of \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\) over \(\kappa\) has finite height. If a homomorphism \(f\colon \class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\to \class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G'}\) reduces to \(0\) over \(\kappa\), then \(f=0\).

A homomorphism of formal groups commutes with multiplication by \(p\). We go up one power of \(\mathfrak m_A\) at a time: if \(f\) already vanishes modulo \(\mathfrak m_A^n\), then on the next layer \([p]_{\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G'}}\circ f\) is zero, while \([p]_{\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}}\) keeps a nonzero leading term because the height is finite, so \(f\circ[p]_{\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}}\) can vanish only if \(f\) does.

The proof in steps

1
It is enough to go from \(\mathfrak m_A^n\) to \(\mathfrak m_A^{n+1}\).
2
The composite \([p]_{\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G'}}\circ f\) is zero.
3
The composite \(f\circ[p]_{\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}}\) is zero only if \(f\) is zero.
Proof.

Here \(\mathfrak m_A\) is the maximal ideal of \(A\), \(\kappa=\overline{\mathbb F}_p\) is its residue field, and \([p]_{\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}}\) is multiplication by \(p\) on \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\), the power series obtained by adding \(x\) to itself \(p\) times with the group law of \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\).

Step 1 · It is enough to go from \(\mathfrak m_A^n\) to \(\mathfrak m_A^{n+1}\). The statement is Drinfeld's rigidity [Dri74, Proposition 4.1], and we recall the argument. Since \(A=\varprojlim A/\mathfrak m_A^n\), it suffices to show that \(f\equiv0\) modulo \(\mathfrak m_A^n\) implies \(f\equiv0\) modulo \(\mathfrak m_A^{n+1}\), for \(n\ge1\). We replace \(A\) by \(A/\mathfrak m_A^{n+1}\) and put \(I=\mathfrak m_A^n\), so that \(\mathfrak m_AI=0\) and \(I^2=0\); in particular \(pI=0\), because \(p\in\mathfrak m_A\). We write \(f(x)=\sum_{m\ge1}f_mx^m\) with \(f_m\in I\) and \([p]_{\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G'}}(T)=pT+\sum_{j\ge2}b_jT^j\).

Step 2 · The composite \([p]_{\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G'}}\circ f\) is zero. We have \([p]_{\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G'}}(f(x))=pf(x)+\sum_{j\ge2}b_jf(x)^j=0\), because \(pI=0\) and \(I^2=0\). Since \(f\) is a homomorphism, \(f\circ[p]_{\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}}=[p]_{\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G'}}\circ f=0\).

Step 3 · The composite \(f\circ[p]_{\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}}\) is zero only if \(f\) is zero. Since \(\mathfrak m_AI=0\), the series \(f([p]_{\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}}(x))\) depends only on \([p]_{\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}}\) modulo \(\mathfrak m_A\), which is \(\bar ux^{q}+(\text{higher terms})\) with \(\bar{u}\in \kappa^\times\) and \(q\) a power of \(p\), because the reduction of \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\) over \(\kappa\) has finite height. If \(f\ne0\), let \(m\) be minimal with \(f_m\ne0\); then the coefficient of \(x^{mq}\) in \(f([p]_{\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}}(x))\) is \(f_m\bar{u}^{\,m}\ne0\), which contradicts \([p]_{\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G'}}\circ f=0\). So \(f=0\) over \(A/\mathfrak m_A^{n+1}\), which is the inductive step.

Lemma: Omodule · Part (c) of thm:fixedlocus: the lift makes the deformation a formal \(\mathcal O_L\)-module of height one · Proved

Lemma (Part (c) of thm:fixedlocus). Let \([(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G},\iota)]\in\mathrm{Def}^\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}(A)\) with lift \(\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}\), and let \(\class{gk-alead}{c}\in A\) be the scalar by which \(\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}\) acts on the free rank one \(A\)-module \(\mathrm{Lie}(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G})\).

(i) \(\class{gk-alead}{c}\) depends only on the class \([(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G},\iota)]\), and \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}(\class{gk-alead}{c})=0\). Hence \(x\mapsto \class{gk-alead}{c}\) defines a local \(W\)-algebra map \(\mathcal O=W[x]/\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}(x)\to A\).

(ii) With \(A\) regarded as an \(\mathcal O\)-algebra through (i), the rule \(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}\mapsto\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}\) makes \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\) a formal \(\mathcal O_L\)-module over \(A\) in the sense of [Dri74, §1], and \((\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G},\iota)\) a deformation of the formal \(\mathcal O_L\)-module \((\class{gk-Hh}{H_h},\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma})\) over \(\kappa\).

(iii) The formal \(\mathcal O_L\)-module \((\class{gk-Hh}{H_h},\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma})\) has height one.

In a coordinate \(X\) of the formal group \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\), the lift of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) reads \(\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}(X)=\class{gk-alead}{c} X+(\text{higher terms})\), so \(\class{gk-alead}{c}\) is how the lift acts on the tangent line of the formal group \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\) (the tangent line of the formal group, never of Lubin-Tate space). Sending \(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}\) to the lift makes \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\) a formal module over \(\mathcal O_L=\mathbb Z_p[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\), and since \(\mathcal O_L\) has degree \(h\) over \(\mathbb Z_p\), the height \(h\) measured over \(\mathbb Z_p\) becomes \(h/h=1\) measured over \(\mathcal O_L\).

The proof in steps

1
The scalar \(\class{gk-alead}{c}\) is a root of \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}\) that depends only on the class, which is item (i).
2
Sending \(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}\) to the lift makes \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\) a formal \(\mathcal O_L\)-module that deforms \((\class{gk-Hh}{H_h},\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma})\), which is item (ii).
3
The height of \((\class{gk-Hh}{H_h},\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma})\) measured over \(\mathcal O_L\) is one, which is item (iii).
Proof.

Here \(A\in\mathrm{Test}_W\) has maximal ideal \(\mathfrak m_A\) and residue field \(\kappa=\overline{\mathbb F}_p\), and \((\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G},\class{gk-iota}{\iota})\) is a \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-fixed deformation of \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) over \(A\) with its unique lift \(\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}\) (Lemma lem:lift). We write \(\mathrm{Lie}(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G})\) for the tangent line of \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\) at the origin, a free \(A\)-module of rank one on which an endomorphism \(e\) acts by its derivative \(e'(0)\); \(\bar f\) for the reduction over \(\kappa\) of a map \(f\); \(L=\mathbb Q_p(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}})\) and \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}=\mathbb Z_p[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\) for its ring of integers; and \([b]_{\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}}\) for the action of \(b\) on \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\).

Step 1 · The scalar \(\class{gk-alead}{c}\) is a root of \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}\) that depends only on the class, which is item (i). Recall that \(h=p^{k-1}(p-1)\) is the degree of \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}\) and that \(\mathcal O=W[x]/\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}(x)\). The derivative at \(0\), \(e\mapsto e'(0)\), is a ring homomorphism \(\mathrm{End}(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G})\to\mathrm{End}_A(\mathrm{Lie}\,\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G})=A\), and \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}(\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}})=0\) by Lemma lem:lift, so \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}(\class{gk-alead}{c})=0\). The scalar \(\class{gk-alead}{c}\) depends only on the class: if \(f\colon(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G},\iota)\to(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G'},\iota')\) is an isomorphism, then \(f\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}} f^{-1}\) lifts \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) on \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G'}\) since \(\class{gk-iota}{\iota}'\circ\bar f\circ\overline{\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}}\circ\bar f^{-1}=\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\circ\overline{\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}}\circ\bar f^{-1}=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\circ\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\circ\bar f^{-1}=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\circ\class{gk-iota}{\iota}'\), so \(f\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}} f^{-1}\) is the lift \(\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}'\) by uniqueness, and the derivative of \(f\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}} f^{-1}\) is \(f'(0)\,\class{gk-alead}{c}\,f'(0)^{-1}=\class{gk-alead}{c}\). The map \(\mathcal O\to A\), \(x\mapsto \class{gk-alead}{c}\), is local because \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}(x)\equiv(x-1)^h\) modulo \(p\), which gives \((\class{gk-alead}{c}-1)^h\in pA\subset\mathfrak m_A\).

Step 2 · Sending \(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}\) to the lift makes \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\) a formal \(\mathcal O_L\)-module that deforms \((\class{gk-Hh}{H_h},\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma})\), which is item (ii). A formal \(\mathcal O_L\)-module over an \(\mathcal O_L\)-algebra \(A\) is a one dimensional formal group over \(A\) together with a ring homomorphism from \(\mathcal O_L\) to its endomorphisms, such that each \(b\in\mathcal O_L\) acts on the Lie algebra by the image of \(b\) in \(A\) [Dri74, §1]. The ring \(\mathrm{End}(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G})\) is a \(\mathbb Z_p\)-algebra: since \(p\in\mathfrak m_A\), we have \([p]_{\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}}(x)\in(\mathfrak m_A,x^2)\), so \([p^j]_{\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}}\to0\) in the \((\mathfrak m_A,x)\)-adic topology of \(A[[x]]\), and \(j\mapsto[j]_{\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}}\) extends continuously to \(\mathbb Z_p\). Because \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}(\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}})=0\), we obtain a ring homomorphism \(\mathcal O_L=\mathbb Z_p[x]/\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}(x)\to\mathrm{End}(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G})\), \(x\mapsto\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}\). The induced action on \(\mathrm{Lie}(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G})\) is through \(\mathcal O_L\to A\), \(x\mapsto \class{gk-alead}{c}\), which is the structure map of \(A\) as an \(\mathcal O_L\)-algebra from (i), so \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\) is a formal \(\mathcal O_L\)-module over \(A\).

The isomorphism \(\iota\) intertwines \(\overline{\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}}\) and \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\), so \(\iota\) is an isomorphism of formal \(\mathcal O_L\)-modules over \(\kappa\); here \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) acts on \(\mathrm{Lie}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\) by \(1\), the image of \(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}\) in the residue field, by (i) applied to \(A=\kappa\).

Step 3 · The height of \((\class{gk-Hh}{H_h},\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma})\) measured over \(\mathcal O_L\) is one, which is item (iii). The extension \(L=\mathbb Q_p(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}})\) of \(\mathbb Q_p\) is totally ramified of degree \(h\), with uniformizer \(\varpi=\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}-1\) (a generator of the maximal ideal of \(\mathcal O_L\)) and residue field \(\mathbb F_p\), and \(p=v\varpi^h\) with \(v\in\mathcal O_L^\times\). Hence \([p]_{H_h}=[v]_{H_h}\circ[\varpi]_{H_h}^{\circ h}\). The endomorphism \([\varpi]_{\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}}=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1\) of \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) is nonzero, since \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\ne1\), so it has a leading term. Let \(d\) be the \(\mathcal O_L\)-height, so that \([\varpi]_{H_h}(x)=\bar ux^{p^{d}}+\cdots\) with \(\bar{u}\ne0\). The series \([v]_{H_h}\) has leading term of degree \(1\), since \(v\) is a unit, and \([p]_{H_h}\) has leading term of degree \(p^h\), since \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) has height \(h\); comparing leading terms gives \(p^h=p^{dh}\), so the \(\mathcal O_L\)-height \(d\) equals \(1\).

Thm: fixedlocus · Theorem (the fixed locus): the fixed points of \(\sigma\) on Lubin-Tate space are \(\mathrm{Spf}\,W[\zeta_{p^k}]\) · ♡

Theorem (the fixed locus). Let \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}=R/\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)R\bigr)\) be the coinvariant ring, where \(\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)R\bigr)\) is the ideal of \(R\) generated by all \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}r-r\). There is a unique isomorphism of \(W\)-algebras \[\class{gk-nu}{\nu}\colon W[x]/\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}(x)=W[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\;\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\;\class{gk-RC}{R_C}\] sending \(x\) to the scalar by which the lift of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) acts on the Lie algebra of the universal \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-fixed deformation. The hypothesis that \(p\) is odd is not used.

At a fixed point the lift of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) reads \(\class{gk-alead}{c} X+(\text{higher terms})\) in a coordinate \(X\) of the formal group, and \(\class{gk-alead}{c}\) is a root of \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}\) that reduces to \(1\). The lift makes the formal group a formal \(\mathcal O_L\)-module of \(\mathcal O_L\)-height one, and height one has no deformations, so a fixed deformation is the same thing as its leading coefficient \(\class{gk-alead}{c}\). The fixed locus is therefore the space of such roots, \(\mathrm{Spf}\,W[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\).

The proof in steps

1
Sending a fixed deformation to its leading coefficient \(\class{gk-alead}{c}\) is a natural map \(\alpha\).
2
The fiber of \(\alpha_A\) over a root \(\lambda\) is the set of deformations of the formal \(\mathcal O_L\)-module \((\class{gk-Hh}{H_h},\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma})\).
3
A formal \(\mathcal O_L\)-module of height one has no deformations, so each fiber is one point.
4
The bijection \(\alpha\) is an isomorphism \(\class{gk-nu}{\nu}\) from \(W[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\) to \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}\).
Proof. Proof of Theorem thm:fixedlocus

Here \(R\) is the Lubin-Tate ring, \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}=R/\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)R\bigr)\) is its coinvariant ring, \(\mathrm{Def}^{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}(A)\) is the set of classes of deformations \((\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G},\class{gk-iota}{\iota})\) of \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) over \(A\) fixed by \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\), \(\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}\) is the unique lift of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) to such a deformation, \(L=\mathbb Q_p(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}})\), and \(\kappa=\overline{\mathbb F}_p\).

Parts (a), (b) and (c) of the theorem are Lemma lem:fixedfunctor, Lemma lem:lift and Lemma lem:Omodule, proved above. What remains is the isomorphism \(\class{gk-nu}{\nu}\).

Step 1 · Sending a fixed deformation to its leading coefficient \(\class{gk-alead}{c}\) is a natural map \(\alpha\). Recall that \(\mathrm{Test}_W\) is the category of complete noetherian local \(W\)-algebras with residue field \(\kappa\), and that \(\mathcal O=W[x]/\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}(x)=W[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\). By Lemma lem:Omodule(i) there is, for every \(A\in\mathrm{Test}_W\), a map \[\alpha_A\colon\mathrm{Def}^{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}(A)\to\mathrm{Hom}_{W}(\mathcal O,A),\qquad [(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G},\iota)]\mapsto(x\mapsto \class{gk-alead}{c}),\] where \(\mathrm{Hom}_W\) denotes local \(W\)-algebra maps, and \(\class{gk-alead}{c}\) is the scalar by which the lift \(\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}\) of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) acts on the Lie algebra of \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\). An element of \(\mathrm{Hom}_W(\mathcal O,A)\) is a root of \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}\) in \(A\), the image of \(x\).

The maps \(\alpha_A\) are natural in \(A\). Let \(g\colon A\to B\) be a map in \(\mathrm{Test}_W\). The base change along \(g\) of the lift \(\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}\) is an automorphism of the base change of \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\), and the base change of the lift has the same reduction over \(\kappa\) as \(\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}\), because \(g\) induces the identity on the residue field \(\kappa\). So the base change of a \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-fixed class is \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-fixed, and the base change of the lift is the lift, by the uniqueness in Lemma lem:lift. The derivative at \(0\) commutes with base change, so the scalar of the base changed class is \(g(\class{gk-alead}{c})\), which says that \(\alpha_B\) of the base changed class is \(g\circ\alpha_A\) of the class.

Step 2 · The fiber of \(\alpha_A\) over a root \(\lambda\) is the set of deformations of the formal \(\mathcal O_L\)-module \((\class{gk-Hh}{H_h},\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma})\). We show that \(\alpha_A\) is bijective for every \(A\in\mathrm{Test}_W\). Fix \(\lambda\in\mathrm{Hom}_W(\mathcal O,A)\) and write \(A_\lambda\) for \(A\) with this \(\mathcal O\)-algebra structure. The ring \(\mathcal O=\mathcal O_L\otimes_{\mathbb Z_p}W\), with \(\mathcal O_L=\mathbb Z_p[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\), is the ring of integers of the completion of the maximal unramified extension of \(L=\mathbb Q_p(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}})\), and \(A_\lambda\) is a complete noetherian local \(\mathcal O\)-algebra with residue field \(\kappa\). A deformation of the formal \(\mathcal O_L\)-module \((\class{gk-Hh}{H_h},\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma})\) to \(A_\lambda\) is a formal \(\mathcal O_L\)-module \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\) over \(A_\lambda\) with an isomorphism \(\iota\) of formal \(\mathcal O_L\)-modules from the reduction of \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\) over \(\kappa\) to \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\), on which \(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}\) acts by \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\).

We claim that \(\alpha_A^{-1}(\lambda)\) is in bijection with the set of isomorphism classes of such deformations. Starting from a class in \(\alpha_A^{-1}(\lambda)\), Lemma lem:Omodule(ii) attaches such a deformation \((\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G},\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}},\iota)\) to the class. Conversely, a deformation \((\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G},[\,\cdot\,]_{\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}},\iota)\) of \((\class{gk-Hh}{H_h},\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma})\) to \(A_\lambda\) gives the class of \((\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G},\iota)\); this class is \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-fixed with lift \(\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}=[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]_{\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}}\) by Lemma lem:lift, and \(\class{gk-alead}{c}=\lambda(x)\) by the definition of a formal \(\mathcal O_L\)-module. The two constructions are inverse on objects because the lift is unique. They also respect isomorphism classes: an isomorphism of formal \(\mathcal O_L\)-module deformations is in particular an isomorphism in \(\mathrm{Def}(A)\), and conversely an isomorphism \(f\colon(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G},\iota)\to(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G'},\iota')\) in \(\mathrm{Def}(A)\) satisfies \(f\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}} f^{-1}=\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}'\) by uniqueness of lifts, so \(f\) is \(\mathcal O_L\)-linear.

Step 3 · A formal \(\mathcal O_L\)-module of height one has no deformations, so each fiber is one point. By Drinfeld [Dri74, Proposition 4.2], the functor of deformations of a formal \(\mathcal O_L\)-module of height \(d\) over \(\kappa\), on complete noetherian local \(\mathcal O\)-algebras with residue field \(\kappa\), is represented by a power series ring over \(\mathcal O\) in \(d-1\) variables. Here \(d=1\) by Lemma lem:Omodule(iii), so the representing ring is \(\mathcal O\) itself, with no variables, and every \(A_\lambda\) carries exactly one deformation class. Hence \(\alpha_A^{-1}(\lambda)\) is a single point for every \(\lambda\), and \(\alpha_A\) is bijective.

Step 4 · The bijection \(\alpha\) is an isomorphism \(\class{gk-nu}{\nu}\) from \(W[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\) to \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}\). By Lemma lem:fixedfunctor, \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}\) represents \(\mathrm{Def}^{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}\), so the natural transformation \(\alpha\) is an isomorphism between the functors on \(\mathrm{Test}_W\) represented by \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}\) and by \(\mathcal O\). Writing \(\text{よ}(B)=\mathrm{Hom}_W(B,-)\) for the Yoneda embedding, this says that \(\alpha\colon\text{よ}(\class{gk-RC}{R_C})\xrightarrow{\sim}\text{よ}(\mathcal O)\), and since \(\text{よ}\) is fully faithful, \(\alpha\) is induced by a unique isomorphism of \(W\)-algebras \(\class{gk-nu}{\nu}\colon\mathcal O\to \class{gk-RC}{R_C}\), namely \(\alpha_{\class{gk-RC}{R_C}}\) applied to the universal fixed class. By construction \(\class{gk-nu}{\nu}(x)\) is the scalar by which the universal lift of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) acts on the Lie algebra.

Step 2. The cotangent space of Lubin-Tate space is one chain

Key ideas:

cotangent space and coinvariants

Definition (Cotangent space). For a complete local ring \(R\) with maximal ideal \(\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}\) and residue field \(\kappa\), the cotangent space is the \(\kappa\)-vector space \(\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}/\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}^2\): the linear terms. For Lubin-Tate space it has dimension \(\class{gk-h}{h}\), with basis the classes of \(p,u_1,\dots,u_{h-1}\).

Definition (Coinvariants). For a \(\kappa\)-vector space \(V\) with an action of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\), the \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-coinvariants are \(V/(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)V\): the largest quotient on which \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) acts trivially.

chains and tops

Definition (Chain). Since \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) has order \(p^k\) and \(\kappa\) has characteristic \(p\), \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{p^k}=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^{p^k}-1=0\): \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1\) is nilpotent. A chain is a list \(v,(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)v,(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^2v,\dots,(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{\ell-1}v\) of nonzero vectors with \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^\ell v=0\) (a Jordan block). Its top is \(v\), its bottom is \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{\ell-1}v\).

the cotangent space of a DVR

Definition (Discrete valuation ring). A Noetherian local domain that is not a field and whose maximal ideal is generated by one element, a uniformizer. All the discrete valuation rings on this page are complete. \(\class{gk-Wring}{W}[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\) is one, with uniformizer \(\class{gk-unif}{\zeta_{p^k}-1}\).

Fact standard

The cotangent space of a discrete valuation ring is one line, spanned by the class of a uniformizer. For \(\class{gk-Wring}{W}[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\) it is spanned by \(\class{gk-unif}{\zeta_{p^k}-1}\), and the class of \(p\) is \(0\) there, since \(p=(\class{gk-unif}{\zeta_{p^k}-1})^{h}\cdot\text{unit}\).

In words. The fixed locus has no deformation parameters (it is finite over \(\class{gk-Wring}{W}\)); its one line of cotangent space is the ramified \(p\)-direction.

Used in prop:cotangent: the coinvariants are one line.
