Let \(\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}\) be the maximal ideal of \(R\). The cotangent space \(\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}/\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}^2\) is one chain of length \(\class{gk-h}{h}\): its top \(\class{gk-xtop}{x}\) restricts to \(\class{gk-unif}{\zeta_{p^k}-1}\) up to a unit, and its bottom is the class of \(p\).

Why there is one chain. We count the chains in three steps.

  1. Since \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) has \(p\)-power order, \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1\) is nilpotent on \(\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}/\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}^2\), so \(\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}/\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}^2\) splits into chains \(v,(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)v,(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^2v,\dots\) (Jordan blocks). Each chain has exactly one top, a vector that is not in the image of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1\). So the number of chains is the dimension of the coinvariants \((\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}/\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}^2)/(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)(\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}/\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}^2)\).
  2. These coinvariants are the cotangent space of the fixed locus \(\mathrm{Spf}\,\class{gk-RC}{R_C}\), since setting every \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}r-r\) to zero kills exactly the image of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1\) on the linear terms (prop:cotangent).
  3. By Step 1, the fixed locus is \(\mathrm{Spf}\,\class{gk-Wring}{W}[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\), and \(\class{gk-Wring}{W}[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\) is a discrete valuation ring. So its cotangent space is one line, spanned by \(\class{gk-unif}{\zeta_{p^k}-1}\), and \(\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}/\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}^2\) is one chain.

Since \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) fixes \(p\), \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1\) kills the class of \(p\), so it is the bottom of the chain. On the fixed locus the top goes to \(\class{gk-unif}{\zeta_{p^k}-1}\) and the bottom to \(0\), which matches \(p=(\class{gk-unif}{\zeta_{p^k}-1})^{h}\cdot\text{unit}\).

Definition (ramification index). Let \(L\) be a finite extension of \(\mathbb Q_p\), with ring of integers \(\mathcal O_L\) and uniformizer \(\varpi\). The ramification index of \(L\) over \(\mathbb Q_p\) is the integer \(e\) with \(p\mathcal O_L=(\varpi^e)\), and \(L\) is totally ramified over \(\mathbb Q_p\) when \(e=[L:\mathbb Q_p]\). For \(L=\mathbb Q_p(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}})\) we may take \(\varpi=\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}-1\), and \(e=\class{gk-h}{h}=p^{k-1}(p-1)=[L:\mathbb Q_p]\), so \(L\) is totally ramified.

Definition (uniserial). A module is uniserial if its submodules are totally ordered by inclusion, that is, if they form a single chain. For example, over a field \(\kappa\), the module \(\kappa[\varepsilon]/(\varepsilon^\ell)\) is uniserial, with submodules \((\varepsilon^i)\) for \(0\le i\le\ell\). The socle of a module is its largest semisimple submodule; for \(\kappa[\varepsilon]/(\varepsilon^\ell)\) with \(\ell\ge1\) it is the line \((\varepsilon^{\ell-1})\), the bottom of the chain.

Proofs

Lemma: shift · one step down the chain multiplies the \(\tau\)-weight by \(\chi\) · Proved

Lemma. Let \(M\) be a \(\kappa[\class{gk-Gp}{G'}]\)-module and \(i\ge0\). Then \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^iM\) is a \(\kappa[\class{gk-Gp}{G'}]\)-submodule of \(M\). If \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) acts on \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^iM/(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{i+1}M\) by a scalar \(\text{か}\in \kappa\), then \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) acts on \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{i+1}M/(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{i+2}M\) by the scalar \(\text{か}\,\chi(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})\).

The operator \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1\) is a shift operator: it moves each layer \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^iM/(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{i+1}M\) of the filtration of \(M\) onto the next layer \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{i+1}M/(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{i+2}M\), and this lemma says what that shift does to the \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\)-weight. Conjugating \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) by \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) gives \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^a\), and modulo \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^2\) the element \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^a-1\) is \(a(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\). So one step down the filtration of \(M\) by the submodules \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^iM\) multiplies the scalar by which \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) acts by \(a\), which is \(\chi(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})\).

\[\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\,(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\,\class{gk-tau}{\tau}^{-1}=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^{a}-1\equiv a(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\pmod{(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^2}\]

The proof in steps

1
Moving \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) past \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\) multiplies by \(a\), up to a term in \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^2\).
2
The error terms land two layers down, so \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) acts on the next layer by \(\text{か}\,\chi(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})\).
Proof.

Here \(\kappa=\overline{\mathbb F}_p\), \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}=\langle\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\rangle\rtimes\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle\) is the maximal finite subgroup, with \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) of order \(p^k\) and \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) of order \((p-1)^2\), \(C=\langle\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\rangle\), and \(\kappa[C]\) is the group algebra of \(C\), which acts on \(M\).

Step 1 · Moving \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) past \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\) multiplies by \(a\), up to a term in \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^2\). Here \(a\) is a positive integer with \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau} \class{gk-sigma}{\sigma} \class{gk-tau}{\tau}^{-1}=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^a\), so that the conjugation character is \(\chi(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})=a\bmod p\). By the binomial theorem, \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^a-1=(1+(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1))^a-1=a(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)+(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^2r\) with \(r\in \kappa[C]\), so for every \(m\in M\) we have \[\class{gk-tau}{\tau}((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)m)=(\class{gk-tau}{\tau} \class{gk-sigma}{\sigma} \class{gk-tau}{\tau}^{-1}-1)\,\class{gk-tau}{\tau} m=(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^a-1)\,\class{gk-tau}{\tau} m=(a(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)+(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^2r)\,\class{gk-tau}{\tau} m.\] In particular \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)M)\subset (\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)M\), and by induction on \(i\) each \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^iM\) is stable under \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\); each \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^iM\) is stable under \(C\) because \(\kappa[C]\) is commutative, so \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^iM\) is a \(\kappa[\class{gk-Gp}{G'}]\)-submodule.

Step 2 · The error terms land two layers down, so \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) acts on the next layer by \(\text{か}\,\chi(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})\). Suppose that \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) acts on \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^iM/(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{i+1}M\) by \(\text{か}\), and let \(m\in (\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^iM\), so that \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau} m=\text{か} m+m'\) with \(m'\in (\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{i+1}M\). The display above gives \[\class{gk-tau}{\tau}((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)m)=(a(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)+(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^2r)(\text{か} m+m')\equiv a\text{か}\,(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)m\pmod{(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{i+2}M},\] because \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^2r\,m\) and \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)m'\) both lie in \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{i+2}M\). The elements \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)m\) with \(m\in (\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^iM\) are all of \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{i+1}M\), so \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) acts on \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{i+1}M/(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{i+2}M\) by \(a\text{か}=\text{か}\,\chi(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})\).

Prop: cotangent · \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) is one chain under \(\sigma-1\), with the class of \(p\) at the bottom · Proved

Proposition. The \(\kappa[C]\)-module \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) is uniserial of length \(h\), isomorphic to \(\kappa[\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1]/((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^h)\), and its socle is spanned by the class of \(p\). Consequently the \(\kappa[C]\)-module \(\bar{\mathfrak m}/\bar{\mathfrak m}^2\) is uniserial of length \(h-1\). Both \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) and \(\bar{\mathfrak m}/\bar{\mathfrak m}^2\) are also uniserial as \(\kappa[\class{gk-Gp}{G'}]\)-modules.

Picture each line of the cotangent space \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) as a box, moving one box down. Passing to the fixed locus sets \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}=1\), so every box that \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\) lands on is erased and only the tops of the chains survive. The fixed locus is the formal spectrum of a discrete valuation ring, whose cotangent space is one line, so there is one top and one chain; the count takes nothing else from Theorem thm:fixedlocus. The chain we find is the total ramification of \(\mathbb Q_p(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}})/\mathbb Q_p\) seen inside Lubin-Tate space: in \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\) everything is built from the single uniformizer \(\pi=\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}-1\), in the chain \(\pi,\pi^2,\dots,\pi^{\class{gk-h}{h}}=p\cdot(\text{unit})\), whose length is the ramification index \(\class{gk-h}{h}\), and the cotangent space \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) has the same shape, playing the role of multiplication by \(\pi\), the line of \(\pi\) at the top and the line of \(p\) at the bottom.

The proof in steps

1
The cotangent space of the fixed locus is the space of coinvariants of \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\).
2
The number of chains is the dimension of the coinvariants, which is one.
3
The class of \(p\) spans the bottom of the chain.
4
Both chains are also single chains for the whole group \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\).
Proof.

Here \(\kappa=\overline{\mathbb F}_p\), \(\class{gk-Wring}{W}=W(\kappa)\) is its ring of Witt vectors, \(\class{gk-h}{h}=p^{k-1}(p-1)\), \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) is the one-dimensional formal group of height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\) over \(\kappa\), \(R=\pi_0E_{\class{gk-h}{h}}\cong\class{gk-Wring}{W}[[u_1,\dots,u_{h-1}]]\) is the Lubin-Tate ring, with maximal ideal \(\mathfrak m\), \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}=\langle\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\rangle\rtimes\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle\subset\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\) is the maximal finite subgroup, with \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) of order \(p^k\) and \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) of order \((p-1)^2\), acting on \(R\) by \(\class{gk-Wring}{W}\)-algebra maps, and \(\chi\) is the conjugation character, \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\class{gk-tau}{\tau}^{-1}=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^{\chi(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})}\). We write \(\mathcal O=\class{gk-Wring}{W}[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\) for the ring with uniformizer \(\pi=\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}-1\), \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}=\mathbb Z_p[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\), and we regard \(p\) as an element of \(\mathfrak m\).

Step 1 · The cotangent space of the fixed locus is the space of coinvariants of \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\). Here \(\mathfrak m\) is the maximal ideal of \(R\), \(C=\langle\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\rangle\) is cyclic of order \(p^k\), and \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1\). The cotangent space of a local ring is its maximal ideal modulo the square of the maximal ideal, and the coinvariants of \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) are the quotient \((\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2)_{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}=(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2)/(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2)\). Recall that \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}=R/\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)R\bigr)\), where \(\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)R\bigr)\) is the ideal generated by all \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma} r-r\). Since \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) fixes \(W\) and \(R=W+\mathfrak m\), the ideal \(\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)R\bigr)\) is generated by \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\mathfrak m\), and \(R\cdot(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\mathfrak m\subset(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\mathfrak m+\mathfrak m^2\): for \(w\in W\), \(r'\in\mathfrak m\) and \(y\in\mathfrak m\) we have \(w(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)y=(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)(wy)\) and \(r'(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)y\in\mathfrak m^2\). Hence the cotangent space of \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}\) at its closed point is the space of coinvariants \[\mathfrak m/(\mathfrak m^2+\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)R\bigr))=(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2)_{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}} .\]

Step 2 · The number of chains is the dimension of the coinvariants, which is one. The space \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) has dimension \(h\) over \(\kappa\), and \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) has \(p\)-power order, so \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\) is nilpotent on \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\). A nilpotent endomorphism splits a finite dimensional vector space into chains \(v,(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1) v,(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^2v,\dots\) (its Jordan blocks). The top of a chain is the vector \(v\) that \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\) never lands on, so each chain contributes one line to the cokernel of \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\), and the number of chains is the dimension of the coinvariants. By Theorem thm:fixedlocus, \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}\cong\mathcal O=W[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\) is a discrete valuation ring, so the cotangent space of \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}\) is one line, spanned by the class of the uniformizer \(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}-1\) (a generator of the maximal ideal). So the coinvariants are one dimensional, so there is a single chain, which is the first claim: \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\cong \kappa[\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1]/((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^h)\) as \(\kappa[C]\)-modules. Concretely, if \(x\) generates \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) over \(\kappa[\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1]\), then \(x,(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1) x,\dots,(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{h-1}x\) is a basis and \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1\) is the shift operator on this basis, moving each line down one and killing the last:

\[x\ \longmapsto\ (\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1) x\ \longmapsto\ (\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^2x\ \longmapsto\ \cdots\ \longmapsto\ (\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{h-1}x\ \longmapsto\ 0.\]

So \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) is uniserial, with submodules the \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^i(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2)\).

Step 3 · The class of \(p\) spans the bottom of the chain. The class of \(p\) in \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) is nonzero: \(R/pR=\kappa[[u_1,\dots,u_{h-1}]]\) has dimension \(h-1\), so \(p\) is part of a regular system of parameters of \(R\), and \(p\notin\mathfrak m^2\). The class of \(p\) is also \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-invariant, since \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) fixes \(W\). The invariants of a single chain are its bottom, the line \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{h-1}(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2)\) that \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\) kills, also called the socle; so the class of \(p\) spans the socle. The quotient of \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) by that line is \(\mathfrak m/(\mathfrak m^2+pR)=\bar{\mathfrak m}/\bar{\mathfrak m}^2\), where \(\bar{\mathfrak m}\) is the maximal ideal of \(R/pR\), and a single chain modulo its bottom line is a single chain of length \(h-1\).

Step 4 · Both chains are also single chains for the whole group \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\). The group \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) acts on \(R\) and fixes \(p\), so \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) and \(\bar{\mathfrak m}/\bar{\mathfrak m}^2\) are \(\kappa[\class{gk-Gp}{G'}]\)-modules. Every \(\kappa[\class{gk-Gp}{G'}]\)-submodule is a \(\kappa[C]\)-submodule, so the submodules over \(\kappa[\class{gk-Gp}{G'}]\) again form a chain, and both modules are uniserial over \(\kappa[\class{gk-Gp}{G'}]\) as well.

Remark (the tangent space and the ring). The action of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) on \(\bar{\mathfrak m}/\bar{\mathfrak m}^2\) is known: it follows from Cartier theory or from Grothendieck-Messing, and we do not claim it. What one really wants is to lift a description of the \(\mathfrak m\)-adic associated graded to the actual Lubin-Tate ring \(R\), and this proposition is where that lift begins. Modulo \(p\), the shift operator \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\) runs along a chain of length \(\class{gk-h}{h}-1\) and the prime \(p\) is invisible. In the integral cotangent space \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) the chain has length \(\class{gk-h}{h}\), and its last line is the class of \(p\), so that \([p]=(\text{unit})\cdot(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{h-1}x\); here this comes from the fixed locus.

Main Theorem 1 uses exactly this: lifting \(x\) to \(R\) gives a \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-equivariant surjection \(\class{gk-Symh}{\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_W}(\class{gk-rhoW}{\rho_W})\to R\) by Nakayama, and because \(p\) sits at the end of the chain, the kernel is generated by one relation \(f\) with \(f(0)\in pW^\times\) together with its \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-translates. So Main Theorem 1 describes the ring \(R\) itself, integrally. It does not give a closed formula for \(f\), and so it does not give an explicit formula for the Lubin-Tate action on \(\pi_0E_{\class{gk-h}{h}}\).

Remark (the induced reduced regular representation). Let \(C_p=\langle g\rangle\) with \(g=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^{p^{k-1}}\), and let \(\bar\rho=\mathrm{coker}\big(W\to W[C_p]\big)\), for the map \(1\mapsto1+g+\dots+g^{p-1}\), be the reduced regular representation of \(C_p\). Since \(1+g+\dots+g^{p-1}=\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma})\) and \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}(1+(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1))\equiv(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{\class{gk-h}{h}}\) modulo \(p\), we have, as \(\kappa[C]\)-modules,

\[\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\;\cong\;\kappa[\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1]/((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{\class{gk-h}{h}})\;=\;\kappa[C]/\big(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma})\big)\;=\;\big(\mathrm{Ind}_{C_p}^{C_{p^k}}\bar\rho\big)\otimes_W\kappa.\]

At \(k=1\) this is the reduced regular representation \(\bar\rho\otimes_W\kappa\) itself. At \(k\ge2\) it is induced from \(C_p\), of dimension \(p^{k-1}(p-1)=\class{gk-h}{h}\), and it is smaller than the reduced regular representation of \(C_{p^k}\), which has dimension \(p^k-1\). The induced module is the representation in the HHR formula (conj:action), in the formulation of Ray, so the proposition agrees with that formula on cotangent spaces. This first-order agreement does not prove the formula, which asks that the relations of the Lubin-Tate ring be linear in the homogeneous coordinates.

Step 3. One orbit of generators, one orbit of relations

Key ideas:

Nakayama, twice

Lemma (Nakayama, for complete local rings) standard

Let \(\varphi\colon S\to R\) be a local map of complete noetherian local rings. If \(\varphi\) is onto on cotangent spaces, it is onto. If \(I\subset S\) is an ideal and some elements of \(I\) span \(I/\mathfrak m_SI\), they generate \(I\).

In words. To generate a ring or an ideal, it is enough to generate the linear terms.

Used twice in the proof of thm:onthenose: the translates of \(\class{gk-xtop}{x}\) span \(\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}/\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}^2\), so \(\varphi\) is onto; the translates of \(\class{gk-frel}{f}\) span \(\class{gk-Krel}{K}\), so they generate the kernel.

induced representations

Definition (Induced representation). \(\class{gk-rhoW}{\rho_W}=\mathrm{Ind}_{\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle}^{\class{gk-Gp}{G'}}\tilde{\class{gk-chic}{\chi}}=\class{gk-Wring}{W}[\class{gk-Gp}{G'}]\otimes_{\class{gk-Wring}{W}[\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle]}\tilde{\class{gk-chic}{\chi}}\): free over \(\class{gk-Wring}{W}\) of rank \(p^k\), with basis \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^ie\), where \(e\) spans the line \(\tilde{\class{gk-chic}{\chi}}\).

Fact (Frobenius reciprocity) standard

For every \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-module \(M\), the \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-maps \(\class{gk-rhoW}{\rho_W}\to M\) correspond to the elements \(v\in M\) with \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}v=\tilde{\class{gk-chic}{\chi}}(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})v\), by \(e\mapsto v\).

In words. A \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-map out of \(\class{gk-rhoW}{\rho_W}\) is the same as one \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\)-eigenvector.

Used in the proof of thm:onthenose: \(e\mapsto\class{gk-xtop}{x}\) extends to \(\class{gk-rhoW}{\rho_W}\to\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}\), hence to \(\varphi\).

Fact (averaging) standard

Since \(\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle\) has order prime to \(p\), the element \(e_\chi=|\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle|^{-1}\sum_t\tilde{\class{gk-chic}{\chi}}(t)^{-1}t\) projects onto the \(\tilde{\class{gk-chic}{\chi}}\)-eigenvectors, compatibly on \(\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}\) and \(\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}/\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}^2\).

In words. Any lift of the top can be made a \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\)-eigenvector without changing its class.

Used in the proof of thm:onthenose, to choose \(\class{gk-xtop}{x}\).

regular local rings

Definition (Regular local ring). A noetherian local ring whose maximal ideal is generated by as many elements as its dimension. Examples: \(\class{gk-Symh}{\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_W}(\class{gk-rhoW}{\rho_W})=\class{gk-Wring}{W}[[x_0,\dots,x_{p^k-1}]]\), of dimension \(p^k+1\), and \(R=\class{gk-Wring}{W}[[u_1,\dots,u_{h-1}]]\), of dimension \(\class{gk-h}{h}\).

Fact standard (Matsumura, Commutative Ring Theory, Thm. 14.2)

If \(S\to R\) is a surjection of regular local rings, its kernel is generated by \(\dim S-\dim R\) elements that are part of a regular system of parameters of \(S\); any such elements form a regular sequence.

In words. Cutting a regular ring down to a regular ring takes exactly the codimension many equations, and they are as independent as possible.

Used in the proof of thm:onthenose: \((p^k+1)-\class{gk-h}{h}=\class{gk-nn}{n}+1\) relations, a regular sequence.

the gauge

Definition (Radical filtration of \(\kappa[C]\)). \(\rho_\kappa=\class{gk-rhoW}{\rho_W}/p\) is the group ring \(\kappa[\class{gk-Cs}{C}]=\kappa[\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1]/((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{p^k})\). Its radical filtration is \(\mathrm{rad}^i=((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^i)\): one chain of length \(p^k\).

Definition (The gauge). The gauge is \(\mathrm{rad}^{h-1}(\rho_\kappa)\): the last \(p^k-(\class{gk-h}{h}-1)=\class{gk-nn}{n}+1\) steps of that chain.

Fact thm:onthenose (3)

The relations read on cotangent spaces, \(\class{gk-Krel}{K}=I/\mathfrak m_SI\), are isomorphic to the gauge as \(\kappa[\class{gk-Gp}{G'}]\)-modules. So \(\class{gk-Krel}{K}\) is one chain of length \(\class{gk-nn}{n}+1\), with factors \(1,\class{gk-chic}{\chi},\dots,\class{gk-chic}{\chi}^{n}\) from the top.

In words. The relations are exactly the part of the induced representation that the Connective Tissue theorem says grows with \(k\). At \(k=1\) the gauge is the prequel’s: the affine functions on the branch orbit.

Used in the proof of thm:onthenose: one relation \(\class{gk-frel}{f}\) at the top of the chain.

Weierstrass preparation

Theorem (Weierstrass preparation) standard

A power series \(g\in\class{gk-Wring}{W}[[t]]\) not divisible by \(p\) factors uniquely as \(g=E\cdot u\), with \(E\) a distinguished polynomial (monic, all lower coefficients in \(p\class{gk-Wring}{W}\)) and \(u\) a unit.

In words. Up to a unit, a power series in one variable is a polynomial.

Used in thm:onthenose (5): on the fixed locus all \(x_i\) become one variable \(t\), and \(\class{gk-frel}{f}(t,\dots,t)=E(t)\cdot\)unit with \(E\) Eisenstein of degree \(\class{gk-h}{h}\), a polynomial in \(t^{p-1}\) by \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\)-invariance.

A single chain is generated by its top. Taking the top \(\class{gk-xtop}{x}\), made a \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\)-eigenvector by averaging, its \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-translates span \(\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}/\class{gk-mm}{\mathfrak m}^2\), so by Nakayama the map \(\class{gk-Symh}{\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_W}(\class{gk-rhoW}{\rho_W})\to R\), \(x_i\mapsto\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^i\class{gk-xtop}{x}\), is onto. Its kernel, read on cotangent spaces, is the gauge \(\mathrm{rad}^{h-1}(\rho_\kappa)\): here \(\rho_\kappa=\class{gk-rhoW}{\rho_W}/p\) is the group ring \(\kappa[\class{gk-Cs}{C}]\), a single chain of length \(p^k\) under \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1\), and the gauge is its last \(\class{gk-nn}{n}+1\) steps, where \(\class{gk-nn}{n}=p^{k-1}\), so that \(p^k=\class{gk-h}{h}+\class{gk-nn}{n}\). So the kernel is again one chain, of length \(\class{gk-nn}{n}+1\). Its top is one relation \(\class{gk-frel}{f}\), and by Nakayama \(\class{gk-frel}{f},\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\class{gk-frel}{f},\dots,\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^{\class{gk-nn}{n}}\class{gk-frel}{f}\) generate the kernel, a regular sequence since \(R\) is regular. The top of that chain involves \(p\), so \(\class{gk-frel}{f}(0)\in p\class{gk-Wring}{W}^\times\): the relation writes \(p\) as a power series in the \(x_i\) (thm:onthenose).

Proofs

Main theorems
Definition: the gauge, and how it relates to the gauge in physics

Definition (the gauge). Let \(\class{gk-Cs}{C}=\langle\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\rangle\) be the cyclic group of order \(p^k\), and let \(\rho_\kappa=\class{gk-rhoW}{\rho_W}/p\) be the reduction of the induced representation \(\class{gk-rhoW}{\rho_W}\) of the theorem below. Restricted to \(\class{gk-Cs}{C}\), the module \(\rho_\kappa\) is the group ring \(\kappa[\class{gk-Cs}{C}]=\kappa[\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1]/((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{p^k})\), a single chain of length \(p^k\) with radical powers \(\mathrm{rad}^i(\rho_\kappa)=(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^i\rho_\kappa\). The gauge is the submodule \(\mathrm{rad}^{\class{gk-h}{h}-1}(\rho_\kappa)=(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{\class{gk-h}{h}-1}\rho_\kappa\), the last \(p^k-(\class{gk-h}{h}-1)=\class{gk-nn}{n}+1\) lines of that chain, where \(\class{gk-nn}{n}=p^{k-1}\). In the presentation of the theorem below, the gauge is the space of linear combinations of the variables \(x_i\) that vanish in the cotangent space of \(R/p\). Modulo \(p\), the linear term of the relation \(\class{gk-frel}{f}\) is a unit multiple of the top \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{\class{gk-h}{h}-1}e\) of the gauge, and the gauge is a single chain, so the linear terms of \(\class{gk-frel}{f}\) and its \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-translates span the gauge. Main Theorem 1 says that this first-order picture holds on the nose: \(\class{gk-frel}{f}\) and its \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-translates generate all the relations.

Remark (the gauge in physics). In physics, a gauge transformation changes a potential without changing the field that the potential describes, so the physical content is the potential modulo gauge transformations. The curve has the same structure: a regluing datum plays the part of the potential, the curve it builds plays the part of the field, and coordinate changes play the part of gauge transformations, because two regluings that differ by a coordinate change build the same curve. To first order, the Connective Tissue theorem describes the tangent space of Lubin-Tate space as \(\class{gk-Ptheta}{P(\theta)}/\class{gk-rad}{\mathrm{rad}}^{\class{gk-h}{h}-1}\class{gk-Ptheta}{P(\theta)}\), the regluing data modulo the gauge. In the theorem below, a module of the same form appears as the linear relations among the variables \(x_i\) modulo \(p\). No gauge group acts on the ring \(S\) of the theorem, so there the word records this shared form and does not claim a physical theory.

Thm: onthenose · the Lubin-Tate ring, on the nose: one orbit of generators modulo one orbit of relations · ♡

Theorem (Lubin-Tate representation, on the nose). Let \(p\) be odd and \(k\ge1\).

(1) (Weights.) \(\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle\) acts on \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^i(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2)/(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{i+1}(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2)\) by \(\chi^{i+1}\), \(0\le i\le h-1\): the top is \(\chi\) and the bottom, spanned by the class of \(p\), is trivial.

(2) (Generators.) There is \(x\in\mathfrak m\) with \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau} x=\tilde{\chi}(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})x\) whose class generates \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) over \(\kappa[C]\). For any such \(x\), the map \(\varphi\colon S\to R\), \(x_i\mapsto\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^ix\), is a \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-equivariant surjection.

(3) (Relations.) Let \(I=\ker\varphi\). As \(\kappa[\class{gk-Gp}{G'}]\)-modules, \(I/\mathfrak m_SI\cong\mathrm{rad}^{h-1}(\rho_\kappa)\), uniserial of length \(n+1\) with factors \(1,\chi,\dots,\chi^n\) from the top; its radical is \(\mathrm{rad}^h(\rho_\kappa)=\ker(\rho_\kappa\to\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2)\), and the extension of the trivial character by the radical is nonsplit.

(4) (One relation.) If \(f\in I\) has \(f(0)\in pW^\times\), then \(f,\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma} f,\dots,\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^nf\) is a regular sequence generating \(I\), and \(f\) can be chosen \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\)-invariant. Its linear part is a unit multiple of \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{h-1}e\) modulo \(p\rho_W+(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^h\rho_W\). No \(C\)-stable \(W\)-submodule of \(I\) that is free of rank \(n+1\) generates \(I\).

(5) (On the fixed locus.) Let \(\xi\in\mathcal{O}\) be the image of \(x\) and \(E\) its minimal polynomial over \(W\). Then \(\xi\) is a uniformizer, \(E(t)=E_0(t^{p-1})\) with \(E_0\) Eisenstein of degree \(n\), and \(f(t,\dots,t)=E(t)\cdot\)unit in \(W[[t]]=S_C\).

(6) (Norm.) \(N(x)=\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/p^k}\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^ix\) is not divisible by \(p\) in \(R\).

A single chain is generated by its top, and we use this fact twice with Nakayama's lemma. The cotangent space of \(R\) is one chain of \(h\) lines under \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1\) (Proposition prop:cotangent), so one \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\)-eigenvector \(x\) at the top and its \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-translates generate \(R\); the relations, read on cotangent spaces, are the gauge \(\mathrm{rad}^{h-1}(\rho_\kappa)\), again one chain, of \(n+1\) lines, so one relation \(f\) at the top and its \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-translates generate the kernel, with \(f(0)\) equal to \(p\) times a unit. On the fixed locus all the variables become one variable \(t\), and \(f\) becomes an Eisenstein polynomial times a unit.

The proof in steps

1
Each step down the chain \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) multiplies the weight by \(\chi\). The bottom of the chain is the class of \(p\), which \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) fixes, so counting back up gives \(\chi\) at the top.
2
A chain is generated by its top. We average a lift of the top over \(\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle\) to get an eigenvector \(x\), and by the lemma of Nakayama an element that generates \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) generates \(R\) together with its \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-translates.
3
Since \(R\) is regular, the relations are seen in the cotangent space of \(S\). There the relations are the linear forms in the \(x_i\) whose image in \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) is a multiple of the class of \(p\), and these linear forms are the gauge.
4
The gauge is a chain, so by the lemma of Nakayama one relation at the top of the gauge generates \(I\) together with its \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-translates. The top of the gauge is the line that maps to the class of \(p\), so \(f(0)\) is \(p\) times a unit.
5
On the fixed locus all the \(x_i\) become one variable \(t\), so the presentation becomes \(W[[t]]/(f(t,\dots,t))\). By Theorem thm:fixedlocus this ring is \(W[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\), so \(f(t,\dots,t)\) is the minimal polynomial of a uniformizer times a unit.
6
Each \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^ix\) is nonzero modulo \(p\), because its class is at the top of the chain and not on the line of \(p\). Since \(R/p\) is a domain, the product \(N(x)\) is nonzero modulo \(p\).
Proof.

Here \(\kappa=\overline{\mathbb F}_p\), \(\class{gk-Wring}{W}=W(\kappa)\) is its ring of Witt vectors, \(\class{gk-h}{h}=p^{k-1}(p-1)\), \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) is the one-dimensional formal group of height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\) over \(\kappa\), \(R=\pi_0E_{\class{gk-h}{h}}\cong\class{gk-Wring}{W}[[u_1,\dots,u_{h-1}]]\) is the Lubin-Tate ring, with maximal ideal \(\mathfrak m\), \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}=\langle\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\rangle\rtimes\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle\subset\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\) is the maximal finite subgroup, with \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) of order \(p^k\) and \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) of order \((p-1)^2\), and \(N(x)=\prod_{i=0}^{p^k-1}\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^ix\) is the norm of \(x\).

Throughout, \(n=p^{k-1}\) and \(h=p^{k-1}(p-1)\), so that \(p^k=h+n\); \(C=\langle\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\rangle\), so that \(\kappa[C]=\kappa[\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1]/((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{p^k})\); \(\chi\) is the conjugation character, \(\chi(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})=a\bmod p\) where \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau} \class{gk-sigma}{\sigma} \class{gk-tau}{\tau}^{-1}=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^a\), and \(\tilde\chi\) is its Teichmüller lift. The module \(\rho_W=\mathrm{Ind}_{\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle}^{\class{gk-Gp}{G'}}\tilde\chi\) has generator \(e\), \(\rho_\kappa=\rho_W/p\), and \(S=W[[x_0,\dots,x_{p^k-1}]]\) is the completed symmetric algebra of \(\rho_W\), with \(x_i=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^ie\) and maximal ideal \(\mathfrak m_S\). A chain is a uniserial \(\kappa[C]\)-module \(M\), that is, a module isomorphic to \(\kappa[\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1]/((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^\ell)\); its lines are the \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^iM/(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{i+1}M\), its top is \(M/(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)M\), its bottom is the socle \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{\ell-1}M\), and its radical is \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)M\). The six items are proved in order, each using only the ones before it.

(1) Each step down the chain \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) multiplies the weight by \(\chi\). The bottom of the chain is the class of \(p\), which \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) fixes, so counting back up gives \(\chi\) at the top. By Proposition prop:cotangent, \(M=\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) is a chain of length \(h\), so its radical filtration is \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^iM\), each \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^iM/(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{i+1}M\) is a line for \(0\le i\le h-1\), and \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\) maps each line onto the next. Each \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^iM\) is a \(\kappa[\class{gk-Gp}{G'}]\)-submodule by Lemma lem:shift, so \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) acts on each line by a scalar. Let \(\lambda\) be the character of \(\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle\) on the top \(M/(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)M\). Since \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\class{gk-tau}{\tau}^{-1}=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^a\) and \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^a-1=(1+(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1))^a-1=a(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)+(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^2(\cdots)\), moving \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) past \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\) multiplies by \(a\) to first order: for \(m\in M\) we have \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)m)=(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^a-1)\,\class{gk-tau}{\tau}m=(a(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)+(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^2(\cdots))\,\class{gk-tau}{\tau}m\). So if \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) acts on one line by \(\text{か}\), then \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) acts on the next line by \(a\,\text{か}=\chi(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})\,\text{か}\), which is Lemma lem:shift. Going down the chain from the top, \(\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle\) acts on \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^iM/(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{i+1}M\) by \(\lambda\chi^i\). The bottom line is spanned by the class \([p]\) of \(p\), because \([p]\) is nonzero and \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)[p]=0\) (as \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) fixes \(p\)), and the vectors of a chain killed by \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\) are exactly those of the bottom line. Since \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau} p=p\), the group \(\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle\) acts on the bottom line by \(1\), so \(\lambda\chi^{h-1}=1\). As \(\chi^{p-1}=1\) and \(p-1\) divides \(h\), we have \(\chi^h=1\), so \(\lambda=\chi^{1-h}=\chi\), and the \(i\)-th line has weight \(\chi^{i+1}\).

(2) A chain is generated by its top. We average a lift of the top over \(\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle\) to get an eigenvector \(x\), and by the lemma of Nakayama an element that generates \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) generates \(R\) together with its \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-translates. By (1) the top of \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) has character \(\chi\). To find \(x\) we average over \(\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle\): the projector \(\mathrm{pr}_\chi=|\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle|^{-1}\sum_{t\in\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle}\tilde{\chi}(t)^{-1}t\) acts on \(\mathfrak m\) and on \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) compatibly, and for \(x'\in\mathfrak m\) with class outside \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2)\), the element \(x=\mathrm{pr}_\chi x'\) satisfies \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau} x=\tilde{\chi}(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})x\) and has the same image as \(x'\) in the top, since the top has character \(\chi\). The class of \(x\) then generates \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) over \(\kappa[C]\), because a chain is generated by any element outside its radical.

Now let \(x\) be any such element. By Frobenius reciprocity, \(e\mapsto x\) extends to a \(W[\class{gk-Gp}{G'}]\)-linear map \(j\colon\rho_W\to\mathfrak m\), hence to a continuous \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-equivariant \(W\)-algebra map \(\varphi\colon S\to R\) with \(x_i\mapsto\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^ix\). On cotangent spaces \(\varphi\) induces \(\mathfrak m_S/\mathfrak m_S^2=\kappa\bar{p}\oplus\rho_\kappa\to\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\), and the restriction \(\psi\colon\rho_\kappa\to\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) is already onto, since \(\psi(e)\) is the class of \(x\). By Nakayama, \(\varphi(\mathfrak m_S)R=\mathfrak m\), and since \(S\) is complete and \(R\) is \(\mathfrak m_S\)-adically separated, \(\varphi\) is onto.

Items (3) and (4) use two facts about \(\psi\). Restricted to \(C\), \(\rho_\kappa=\kappa[C]e\) is free of rank one, so \(\rho_\kappa\cong \kappa[\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1]/((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{p^k})\) is a chain of length \(p^k\), with \(\mathrm{rad}^i\rho_\kappa=(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^i\rho_\kappa\) and \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau} e=\chi(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})e\). The first fact is \(\ker\psi=(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^h\rho_\kappa\), because \(\psi\) is a surjection from a chain of length \(p^k\) onto a chain of length \(h\). The second fact is that \(\psi((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{h-1}e)\) is a nonzero vector of the bottom \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{h-1}(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2)\), which is the line spanned by \([p]\) (Proposition prop:cotangent), so \([p]=\gamma\,\psi((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{h-1}e)\) for a unique \(\gamma\in \kappa^\times\).

(3) Since \(R\) is regular, the relations are seen in the cotangent space of \(S\). There the relations are the linear forms in the \(x_i\) whose image in \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) is a multiple of the class of \(p\), and these linear forms are the gauge. Let \(I=\ker\varphi\). The source \(S\) has \(p^k+1\) cotangent lines, spanned by the \(x_i\) and \(p\), and the target \(R\) has \(h\), so \(n+1\) lines must be cut out. In detail, the ring \(S\) is regular local of dimension \(p^k+1\) and \(S/I\cong R\) is regular of dimension \(h\), so \(I\) is generated by \(p^k+1-h=n+1\) elements forming part of a regular system of parameters of \(S\), and \(K:=I/\mathfrak m_SI\) has dimension \(n+1\) over \(\kappa\). The image of \(K\) in \(\mathfrak m_S/\mathfrak m_S^2\) is \((I+\mathfrak m_S^2)/\mathfrak m_S^2\), which is the kernel of the map \(\mathfrak m_S/\mathfrak m_S^2\to\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) induced by \(\varphi\), because \(\varphi\) is onto with kernel \(I\). This kernel has dimension \((p^k+1)-h=n+1\), because the map on cotangent spaces is onto by (2). So \(K\) maps onto a space of its own dimension, and \(K\) injects into \(\mathfrak m_S/\mathfrak m_S^2\) as the whole kernel.

Since \(\varphi(p)=p\) and \([p]=\gamma\,\psi((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{h-1}e)\), we get \[K=\ker\big(\kappa\bar{p}\oplus\rho_\kappa\to\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\big) =\{(\alpha\bar{p},r):\ r\in-\alpha\gamma(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{h-1}e+(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^h\rho_\kappa\},\] \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-equivariantly. The projection \(K\to\rho_\kappa\) is \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-equivariant, as \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) fixes \(\bar{p}\), and the projection is injective, since \(r=0\) forces \(\alpha\gamma(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{h-1}e\in (\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^h\rho_\kappa\) and so \(\alpha=0\). Its image lies in the gauge \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{h-1}\rho_\kappa=\mathrm{rad}^{h-1}(\rho_\kappa)\), and both have dimension \(p^k-(h-1)=n+1\), so the image is the gauge. The gauge is a piece of the chain \(\rho_\kappa\), so the gauge is again a chain, of length \(n+1\), with radical \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^h\rho_\kappa=\ker\psi\).

For the lines of the gauge, \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau} e=\chi(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})e\), so \(\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle\) acts on \(\rho_\kappa/(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\rho_\kappa\) by \(\chi\), and Lemma lem:shift, applied to the module \(\rho_\kappa\) for \(i=0,1,\dots,p^k-2\) in turn, gives that \(\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle\) acts on \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^i\rho_\kappa/(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{i+1}\rho_\kappa\) by \(\chi^{i+1}\). The lines of \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{h-1}\rho_\kappa\) are those with \(h-1\le i\le p^k-1\), so they carry \(\chi^{h},\dots,\chi^{p^k}\), that is \(1,\chi,\dots,\chi^n\), as \(\chi^h=1\). The extension of the trivial character by the radical is nonsplit: a splitting would give an element of \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{h-1}\rho_\kappa\) outside \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^h\rho_\kappa\) that is killed by \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\). Such an element is \(w\,(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{h-1}e\) with \(w\) a unit of \(\kappa[C]\), but \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\cdot w\,(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{h-1}e=w\,(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^he\ne0\) as \(h\lt p^k\).

(4) The gauge is a chain, so by the lemma of Nakayama one relation at the top of the gauge generates \(I\) together with its \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-translates. The top of the gauge is the line that maps to the class of \(p\), so \(f(0)\) is \(p\) times a unit. For \(g\in\mathfrak m_S\) with constant term \(g(0)\) and linear part \(\ell\), the class of \(g\) in \(\mathfrak m_S/\mathfrak m_S^2\) is \((g(0)/p\bmod p)\bar{p}+\bar{\ell}\). By (3), an element of \(K\) lies outside the radical of \(K\) if and only if \(\alpha\ne0\): the top of \(K\) is the part with a nonzero \(\bar p\) component. So \(f\in I\) has class outside \(\mathrm{rad}\,K\) if and only if \(f(0)\in pW^\times\), and then \(\bar{\ell}\in-\alpha\gamma(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{h-1}e+(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^h\rho_\kappa\) with \(\alpha\gamma\ne0\), which is the claim about the linear part of \(f\). The constant term is where \(p\) enters: \(f(0)\) is \(p\) times a unit, and no element is inverted.

Since \(K\) is a chain, \(K=\kappa[\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1]\bar{f}\cong \kappa[\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1]/((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{n+1})\), and the classes of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^if\), \(0\le i\le n\), are \((1+(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1))^i\bar{f}\), a basis of \(K\). By Nakayama, \(f,\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma} f,\dots,\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^nf\) generate \(I\), and being part of a regular system of parameters they form a regular sequence. The top of \(K\) is trivial, so \(f\) can be replaced by its average over \(\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle\), which is \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\)-invariant.

Finally, suppose that \(\text{Ж}\subset I\) were a \(C\)-stable \(W\)-submodule, free of rank \(n+1\), generating the ideal \(I\). Since \(I=S\text{Ж}\), the image of \(\text{Ж}\) spans \(K=I/\mathfrak m_SI\) over \(\kappa\); this gives a \(C\)-equivariant surjection \(\text{Ж}/p\text{Ж}\to K\) between spaces of dimension \(n+1\), so \(\text{Ж}/p\text{Ж}\cong K\). Now \(K\) is cyclic over \(\kappa[C]\), so \(\text{Ж}\) is cyclic over \(W[C]\) by Nakayama, and \(\text{Ж}\cong W[C]/J\). Since \(\text{Ж}\) is torsion free, \(J\) is contracted from an ideal of \(W[1/p][C]=\prod_{i=0}^kW[1/p](\zeta_{p^i})\); the factors are fields because each \(\Phi_{p^i}\) is irreducible over \(W[1/p]\), as \(\Phi_{p^i}(1+t)\) is an Eisenstein polynomial over \(W\). An ideal of a product of fields is a product of some of the factors, and the factors have degrees \(1,\,p-1,\,p(p-1),\dots,p^{k-1}(p-1)\) over \(W[1/p]\), so \(\operatorname{rank}\text{Ж}\) is the sum of the degrees of the factors on which \(\text{Ж}[1/p]\) is nonzero, each factor counted once.

Evaluation at \(0\) is a \(C\)-equivariant ring homomorphism \(S\to W\), for the trivial action on \(W\), since \(C\) permutes the variables \(x_i\). Evaluation at \(0\) is nonzero on \(\text{Ж}\): if evaluation at \(0\) vanished on \(\text{Ж}\), then it would vanish on \(S\text{Ж}=I\), but \(I\) contains an element \(f\) with \(f(0)\in pW^\times\) by (3). So the trivial factor \(F\), of degree \(1\), occurs, and the degrees of the remaining factors must add up to \(n=p^{k-1}\ge1\). Each of them is divisible by \(p-1\), so \(p-1\) divides \(p^{k-1}\). But \(p^{k-1}\equiv1\) modulo \(p-1\), so \(p-1\) divides \(1\), which is false because \(p\) is odd. So no such \(\text{Ж}\) exists.

(5) On the fixed locus all the \(x_i\) become one variable \(t\), so the presentation becomes \(W[[t]]/(f(t,\dots,t))\). By Theorem thm:fixedlocus this ring is \(W[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\), so \(f(t,\dots,t)\) is the minimal polynomial of a uniformizer times a unit. The coinvariant ring of \(S\) is \(S_C=S/\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)S\bigr)\), where \(\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)S\bigr)\) is the ideal generated by all \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma} g-g\); the ideal \(\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)S\bigr)\) is generated by the \(x_i-x_j\), so \(S_C=W[[t]]\), with every \(x_i\) mapping to \(t\). Since \(\varphi\) is surjective and equivariant, \(\varphi\) maps \(\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)S\bigr)\) onto \(\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)R\bigr)\), so \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}=S/(I+\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)S\bigr))\) is the quotient of \(W[[t]]\) by the image of \(I\). By (4), \(I\) is generated by the \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^if\), which all have the same image \(f(t,\dots,t)\) in \(W[[t]]\), so \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}=W[[t]]/(f(t,\dots,t))\).

By Theorem thm:fixedlocus, \(\class{gk-RC}{R_C}\cong\mathcal{O}\), so we have a surjection \(W[[t]]\to\mathcal{O}\) with \(t\mapsto\xi\). We show that \(\xi\) is a uniformizer, that is, a generator of the maximal ideal \(\mathfrak m_{\mathcal O}\) of the discrete valuation ring \(\mathcal O\). The surjection induces a surjection on cotangent spaces, so the classes of \(p\) and \(\xi\) span the line \(\mathfrak m_{\mathcal O}/\mathfrak m_{\mathcal O}^2\). The class of \(p\) is zero, because \(p\) is a unit times \((\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}-1)^h\) and \(h\ge2\) for \(p\) odd. So the class of \(\xi\) spans the line, and \(\xi\) is a uniformizer.

As \(\mathcal O\) is totally ramified of degree \(h\) over \(W\), the minimal polynomial \(E\) of the uniformizer \(\xi\) is Eisenstein of degree \(h\). Since \(W[[t]]/(f(t,\dots,t))\cong\mathcal O\) is free of rank \(h\) over \(W\), the power series \(f(t,\dots,t)\) is not divisible by \(p\) (otherwise \(\mathcal O\) would surject onto \(\kappa[[t]]\), which is not finite over \(\kappa\)), and Weierstrass preparation writes \(f(t,\dots,t)\) as a unit times a monic polynomial of degree \(h\). This polynomial vanishes at \(\xi\) and is monic of degree \(h\), so this polynomial is \(E\), and \(f(t,\dots,t)=E(t)\cdot\)unit.

The automorphism \(\bar{\class{gk-tau}{\tau}}\) of \(\mathcal{O}\) induced by \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) is a \(W\)-algebra automorphism with \(\bar{\class{gk-tau}{\tau}}\xi=\tilde{\chi}(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})\xi\), so the roots of \(E\) are stable under multiplication by \(\tilde{\chi}(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})\), which generates \(\mu_{p-1}\) because \(\chi\) has order \(p-1\). As \((p-1)\mid h\), \(E(\tilde{\chi}(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})t)=E(t)\). Comparing the coefficients of \(t^j\) shows that the coefficient of \(t^j\) in \(E\) vanishes unless \(p-1\) divides \(j\), and so \(E(t)=E_0(t^{p-1})\) for a polynomial \(E_0\) of degree \(h/(p-1)=n\). The polynomial \(E_0\) is Eisenstein: \(E_0\) is monic, its other coefficients are coefficients of \(E\), hence divisible by \(p\), and its constant term is \(E_0(0)=E(0)\), which is not divisible by \(p^2\).

(6) Each \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^ix\) is nonzero modulo \(p\), because its class is at the top of the chain and not on the line of \(p\). Since \(R/p\) is a domain, the product \(N(x)\) is nonzero modulo \(p\). The class of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^ix\) generates \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\), so the class of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^ix\) is not in the socle \(\kappa[p]\), as \(h\ge2\); hence \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^ix\notin pR\). Since \(R/p\) is a domain, the product \(N(x)\) of the \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^ix\) is not in \(pR\) either.

Dependency tree of the proof

P.S.

Aside: The spirit of the answer: from symmetry to class field theory, after Lubin

Main Theorem 2 · The curve model

Question 2 Does the action of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) on Lubin-Tate space have a geometric model via decorated curves, starting from the group alone?

Answer: Main Theorem 2Yes. From the group \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) alone we build a curve. Some deformations of this curve keep a one-dimensional formal group of height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\) as a quotient of its Jacobian, and these deformations form a smooth formal scheme. Sending such a deformation to that formal group gives a \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-equivariant, formally smooth map onto Lubin-Tate space. At \(k=1\) this map is an isomorphism, as in the prequel. At \(k\ge2\) it has fibers: the deformations of the curve that the formal group does not notice.

Main Theorem 2 (thm:horsmooth, Fullmetal Equivalent Exchange).

  1. (i) \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\) is pro-representable,
  2. (ii) the functor \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) is formally smooth and a projection,
  3. (iii) the curve with its block is a lift of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\subseteq\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\), so \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) is a fibered model of the Lubin-Tate action of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\).
Proved every odd \(p\), every \(k\ge2\)

The setup, step by step

From the group to Lubin-Tate space. Click a step.

→→→→→→

The group

The group \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\subseteq\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\) is defined in the setup of Main Theorem 1.

Fact (Hewett, Bujard; cited). \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}=\langle\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\rangle\rtimes\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle\cong\mathbb Z/p^k\rtimes\mathbb Z/(p-1)^2\). Its center \(\class{gk-Gc}{G}=\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}^{p-1}\rangle\) has order \(p-1\).

This is the group whose action on Lubin-Tate space we want to model.

The curve

Idea 1 · Witt vectors give σ

Witt invented his vectors to classify cyclic extensions of degree \(p^k\) in characteristic \(p\); for a curve, those are the \(\mathbb Z/p^k\)-covers. Here \(W_k\) is the scheme of \(p\)-typical Witt vectors quotiented by the \(k\)-th power of Verschiebung; \(F\) is Frobenius. The map \(\class{gk-wp}{\wp}=F-1\) on \(W_k\) is the universal one: every such cover is a pullback of \(\wp\), and its deck group is translation by \(W_k(\mathbb F_p)\cong\mathbb Z/p^k\). So \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) acts as \(y\mapsto y+1\).

Idea 2 · the tame part picks the pullback

We want \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) to act too, by scaling \(x\mapsto\zeta x\). So we pull \(\wp\) back along \(x\mapsto[x^{p-1}]\): scaling \(x\) moves \([x^{p-1}]\) to \([\class{gk-theta}{\theta}][x^{p-1}]\), and the Teichmüller unit \([\class{gk-theta}{\theta}]\) commutes with \(\wp\), so \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) lifts.

\[\begin{array}{ccc}\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}^\circ & \longrightarrow & W_k \\ \big\downarrow & & \big\downarrow{\scriptstyle\,\wp\,=\,F-1} \\ \mathbb A^1 & \xrightarrow{\ x\,\mapsto\,[x^{p-1}]\ } & W_k\end{array}\]

Definition. \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) is the smooth projective curve \(\wp(y_0,\dots,y_{k-1})=(x^{p-1},0,\dots,0)\).

Fact (thm:curve). \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) acts faithfully on \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\). The first coordinate \(y_0^p-y_0=x^{p-1}\) is the curve of the prequel. The curves of least genus with this action form a family, and \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) is its simplest member. go to the proof →

The quotient and its branch points

Definition. \(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k}=\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}/\class{gk-Gc}{G}\), and \(\class{gk-B}{B}\subset\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k}\) is the set of branch points of \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\to\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k}\).

Idea 1 · the general principle

From Modeling Group Actions on Stacks, §7.9: “We can consider \(G\)-actions on \(X\) to be equivalent to \(G\)-covers with source \(X\), the latter defined via the notion of algebraic quotient. This equivalence takes a \(G\)-action on \(X\) and sends it to the cover \(q\colon X\to X/G\) which quotients by this action. Under this equivalence, ramification points correspond to points with nontrivial stabilizer group.” For the center acting on \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\), click a point to see its stabilizer:

Click a point (example: p = 3, k = 2).

Fact (thm:curve). The points with nontrivial stabilizer lie over \(\class{gk-B}{B}=\class{gk-B0}{B_0}\cup\{\class{gk-Qinf}{Q_\infty}\}\): the \(p^k\) points over \(x=0\), one \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-orbit, and the point \(Q_\infty\) over \(x=\infty\). go to the proof →

Idea 2 · decorate the center

Deforming \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) by itself is awkward: a curve with automorphisms has a moduli stack, not a space. So we decorate \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) with the action of its center \(\class{gk-Gc}{G}\). By Idea 1, a curve with an action of \(\class{gk-Gc}{G}\) is the same as its quotient \(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k}\) together with the branch points \(\class{gk-B}{B}\); and this curve with its marked points has no infinitesimal automorphisms, so its deformations form an honest smooth formal scheme. Nothing is lost by remembering the center: it acts trivially on Lubin-Tate space, so all of the action of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) survives.

Fact (lem:unif, decorate the center; proved in Tiny and Unique at k = 1 and in the tex for general k; no proof box on this page yet). \(\class{gk-DefXG}{\mathrm{Def}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\class{gk-Gc}{G})}\simeq\class{gk-DefYB}{\mathrm{Def}(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k},\class{gk-B}{B})}\), a smooth formal scheme with tangent space \(\class{gk-Mk}{M_k}=H^1(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k},T(-\class{gk-B}{B}))\).

The block

Definition. \(\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\) is the quotient of \(\mathrm{Jac}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k})[p^\infty]\) whose Dieudonné module is the slope \(1/\class{gk-h}{h}\) part of the crystalline \(H^1\). Its Hodge line is spanned by a form \(\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H}\).

Fact (lem:Dw, Dwork slopes). \(\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\) has dimension one and height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\), so \(\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\cong\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\). go to the proof →

The block is where the curve meets the formal group.

Block-preserving deformations

Definition. \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}(R)\) is the set of deformations \(X_R\) of \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) (with the center) over \(R\) along which the block lifts: \(\mathrm{Jac}(X_R)[p^\infty]\twoheadrightarrow\class{gk-HR}{H_R}\).

Fact (Grothendieck-Messing; cited). When the block lifts, the lift \(\class{gk-HR}{H_R}\) is unique.

The functor and the ghosts

Definition. \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\colon\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\to\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\) sends \(X_R\) to the lifted block \(\class{gk-HR}{H_R}\).

Definition. The ghosts are \(\class{gk-ghost}{\mathfrak g}=\ker d\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\): first-order deformations of the curve that the formal group does not notice.

Lubin-Tate space

Lubin-Tate space \(\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\) and the Lubin-Tate action are defined in the setup of Main Theorem 1.

Fact (Lubin-Tate; cited). \(\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\cong\mathrm{Spf}\,W[[u_1,\dots,u_{h-1}]]\).

The goal: we want to see the action of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) here through curves.

Lubin-Tate space and its symmetries

Throughout, \(p\) is an odd prime, \(k\ge1\) is an integer, \(\class{gk-h}{h}=p^{k-1}(p-1)\), \(W=W(\overline{\mathbb F}_p)\) is the ring of Witt vectors of \(\overline{\mathbb F}_p\), and \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) is the one-dimensional formal group of height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\) over \(\overline{\mathbb F}_p\). Our object of interest is the space of its deformations.

Lubin-Tate space \(\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\), the Lubin-Tate action and the group \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) are defined in the setup of Main Theorem 1. We would like to see this action through curves.

Theorem (Hewett, Bujard). The group \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) is unique up to conjugacy, and \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}=\langle\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\rangle\rtimes\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle\), with \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) of order \(p^k\), \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) of order \((p-1)^2\), and \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\class{gk-tau}{\tau}^{-1}=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^{\chi(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})}\) for a character \(\chi\) of order \(p-1\).

We write \(\class{gk-Gc}{G}=\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}^{p-1}\rangle\) for its center, of order \(p-1\), and \(\class{gk-Gbar}{\bar G}=\class{gk-Gp}{G'}/\class{gk-Gc}{G}\); on this page \(\class{gk-Gc}{G}\) alone always means the center. Let \(\class{gk-theta}{\theta}\in\mathbb F_p^\times\) be the reduction of \(\chi(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})\), a generator of \(\mathbb F_p^\times\), and fix a primitive \((p-1)^2\)-th root of unity \(\zeta\in\overline{\mathbb F}_p\) with \(\zeta^{p-1}=\class{gk-theta}{\theta}\).

The group writes the curve

Let us build a curve that \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) can act on, starting with \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\). Witt invented his vectors to classify cyclic extensions of degree \(p^k\) in characteristic \(p\), so we look in \(W_k\), the scheme of \(p\)-typical Witt vectors quotiented by the \(k\)-th power of Verschiebung. With \(F\) the Frobenius, the map \(\class{gk-wp}{\wp}=F-1\) on \(W_k\) is the universal \(\mathbb Z/p^k\)-cover: over an affine base every such cover is a pullback of \(\class{gk-wp}{\wp}\), and its deck group is translation by \(W_k(\mathbb F_p)\cong\mathbb Z/p^k\). That takes care of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\).

Now we want \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) to act as well, by scaling a coordinate \(x\mapsto\zeta x\), and this is what chooses the pullback. We pull \(\class{gk-wp}{\wp}\) back along \(x\mapsto[x^{p-1}]\): scaling \(x\) by \(\zeta\) moves \([x^{p-1}]\) to \([\class{gk-theta}{\theta}][x^{p-1}]\), and the Teichmüller unit \([\class{gk-theta}{\theta}]\) commutes with \(\class{gk-wp}{\wp}\) because \(\class{gk-theta}{\theta}\in\mathbb F_p\).

\[\begin{array}{ccc}\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}^\circ & \longrightarrow & W_k \\ \big\downarrow & & \big\downarrow{\scriptstyle\,\class{gk-wp}{\wp}\,=\,F-1} \\ \mathbb A^1 & \xrightarrow{\ x\,\mapsto\,[x^{p-1}]\ } & W_k\end{array}\]

Definition 3 (the curve). Let \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) be the smooth projective curve containing this pullback, with equation \(\class{gk-wp}{\wp}(y)=(x^{p-1},0,\dots,0)\) in the Witt coordinates \(y=(y_0,\dots,y_{k-1})\).

The group \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) acts on \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) by

\[\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\colon(x,y)\longmapsto(x,\,y+1),\qquad \class{gk-tau}{\tau}\colon(x,y)\longmapsto(\zeta x,\,[\class{gk-theta}{\theta}]\,y).\]

Since \(\class{gk-wp}{\wp}([\class{gk-theta}{\theta}]y)=[\class{gk-theta}{\theta}]\class{gk-wp}{\wp}(y)=(\class{gk-theta}{\theta}x^{p-1},0,\dots,0)=((\zeta x)^{p-1},0,\dots,0)\), the map \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) preserves the equation. Conjugating \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) by \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) gives \((x,y)\mapsto(x,y+[\class{gk-theta}{\theta}])\), so \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\class{gk-tau}{\tau}^{-1}=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^{[\class{gk-theta}{\theta}]}\) with \([\class{gk-theta}{\theta}]\in W_k(\mathbb F_p)\cong\mathbb Z/p^k\), and the center moves only \(x\), since \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}^{p-1}\colon(x,y)\mapsto(\class{gk-theta}{\theta}x,y)\) because \([\class{gk-theta}{\theta}]^{p-1}=1\).

This action is faithful, the first coordinate \(y_0^p-y_0=x^{p-1}\) is the curve of the prequel, and the curves of least genus with this action form a connected family of which \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) is the simplest member (Theorem thm:curve). Its genus is given by \(2g-2=(p-1)^2\frac{p^{2k}-1}{p^2-1}-p^k-1\), so that \(g=16\) at \((p,k)=(3,2)\).

Decorating the center

Deforming \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) by itself is awkward: a curve with automorphisms has a moduli stack and not a space. So we decorate \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) with the action of its center, using the principle of Modeling Group Actions on Stacks, §7.9: “We can consider \(G\)-actions on \(X\) to be equivalent to \(G\)-covers with source \(X\), the latter defined via the notion of algebraic quotient. This equivalence takes a \(G\)-action on \(X\) and sends it to the cover \(q\colon X\to X/G\) which quotients by this action. Under this equivalence, ramification points correspond to points with nontrivial stabilizer group.”

Definition 4 (the quotient and its branch points). Let \(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k}=\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}/\class{gk-Gc}{G}\), and let \(\class{gk-B}{B}\subset\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k}\) be the set of branch points of \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\to\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k}\).

The points of \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) with nontrivial stabilizer in \(\class{gk-Gc}{G}\) lie over \(\class{gk-B}{B}=\class{gk-B0}{B_0}\cup\{\class{gk-Qinf}{Q_\infty}\}\) (Theorem thm:curve), where \(\class{gk-B0}{B_0}\) is the set of \(p^k\) points over \(x=0\), one for each \(y\in W_k(\mathbb F_p)\), permuted by \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) as a single orbit, and \(\class{gk-Qinf}{Q_\infty}\) is the one point over \(x=\infty\). By the principle above, a curve with an action of \(\class{gk-Gc}{G}\) is the same as its quotient \(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k}\) together with \(\class{gk-B}{B}\), so \(\class{gk-DefXG}{\mathrm{Def}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\class{gk-Gc}{G})}\simeq\class{gk-DefYB}{\mathrm{Def}(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k},\class{gk-B}{B})}\) (Lemma lem:unif). This is a smooth formal scheme with tangent space \(\class{gk-Mk}{M_k}=H^1(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k},T(-\class{gk-B}{B}))\), and the pair \((\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k},\class{gk-B}{B})\) has no infinitesimal automorphisms because \(H^0(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k},T(-\class{gk-B}{B}))=0\). We lose nothing by remembering the center, since the center acts trivially on Lubin-Tate space.

The block

The curve meets the formal group inside the \(p\)-divisible group of its Jacobian, at a single slope.

Definition 5 (the block). We write \(H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}\) for \(H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}/W)\), a free \(W\)-module with a Frobenius. After inverting \(p\), the module \(H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}[1/p]\) is the direct sum of its parts of each slope. Let \(N\subset H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}\) be the set of elements of \(H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}\) that lie in the part of slope \(1/\class{gk-h}{h}\), so \(N=H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}\cap H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}[1/p]_{1/h}\). A submodule \(N\subset H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}\) is saturated if \(pv\in N\) implies \(v\in N\) for every \(v\in H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}\), that is, if \(H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}/N\) has no \(p\)-torsion; our \(N\) is saturated by construction, and we call \(N\) the saturated part of slope \(1/\class{gk-h}{h}\). Let \(\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\) be the quotient of \(\mathrm{Jac}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k})[p^\infty]\) whose Dieudonné module is \(N\). Its Hodge line \(\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H}\subset N/pN\) is spanned by a holomorphic form on \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\).

By Dwork, in the form of Lemma lem:Dw, the block \(\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\) exists and has dimension one and height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\), so \(\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\cong\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\), and we fix such an isomorphism once and for all. Since \(N\) is saturated, \(N/pN\) sits inside \(H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}/pH^1_{\mathrm{cris}}=H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k})\), and it has dimension \(\class{gk-h}{h}\). The Hodge sequence \(0\to H^0(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\Omega^1)\to H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k})\to H^1(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O})\to0\) sends \(N/pN\) to \(H^1(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O})\), and the kernel of this map on \(N/pN\) is the Hodge line \(\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H}\). We write

\[\class{gk-N0}{\bar N_0}=(N/pN)/\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H}\subset H^1(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O})\]

for the image of the block, a subspace of dimension \(\class{gk-h}{h}-1\).

Definition 6 (block-preserving). A deformation \(X_R\) of \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\), with the center, over \(R\) is block-preserving if the quotient \(\mathrm{Jac}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k})[p^\infty]\twoheadrightarrow\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\) lifts to a quotient \(\mathrm{Jac}(X_R)[p^\infty]\twoheadrightarrow\class{gk-HR}{H_R}\). Let \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}(R)\) be the set of block-preserving deformations over \(R\), up to isomorphism.

The functor

Lemma (the lifted block is unique). Let \(X_R\) be a deformation of \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\), with the center, over \(R\). If the quotient \(\mathrm{Jac}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k})[p^\infty]\twoheadrightarrow\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\) lifts to a quotient \(\mathrm{Jac}(X_R)[p^\infty]\twoheadrightarrow\class{gk-HR}{H_R}\), then the lifted quotient is unique.

Proof. We work along small extensions \(R'\to R\) with square-zero kernel and trivial divided powers, with the quotient already lifted to \(\class{gk-HR}{H_R}\) over \(R\). Write \(G=\mathrm{Jac}(X_{R'})[p^\infty]\). The crystal of \(\class{gk-HR}{H_R}\) evaluated on \(R'\) is a direct summand \(\mathbb D(\class{gk-HR}{H_R})_{R'}\) of \(\mathbb D(G)_{R'}=H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(X_{R'}/R')\). Let \(H_{R'}\) be any lift of the quotient, with kernel \(K\), so that \(0\to K\to G\to H_{R'}\to0\) is exact. The Hodge filtration of a \(p\)-divisible group sits in an exact sequence \(0\to\omega\to\mathbb D\to\mathrm{Lie}(\text{dual})\to0\), functorial in the group. The quotient \(G\to H_{R'}\) makes \(\mathbb D(\class{gk-HR}{H_R})_{R'}\) a submodule of \(\mathbb D(G)_{R'}\), so \(\omega_{H_{R'}}\subset\mathrm{Fil}_{R'}\cap\mathbb D(\class{gk-HR}{H_R})_{R'}\), where \(\mathrm{Fil}_{R'}=\omega_G\) is the Hodge filtration of the curve. Conversely, let \(m\in\mathbb D(\class{gk-HR}{H_R})_{R'}\) lie in \(\mathrm{Fil}_{R'}\). Its image in \(\mathrm{Lie}(H_{R'}^\vee)\) maps to \(0\) in \(\mathrm{Lie}(G^\vee)\). The dual sequence \(0\to H_{R'}^\vee\to G^\vee\to K^\vee\to0\) is exact (Cartier duality on the finite flat levels), and \(\mathrm{Lie}\) is left exact, so the image of \(m\) is \(0\) and \(m\in\omega_{H_{R'}}\). Hence

\[\omega_{H_{R'}}=\mathrm{Fil}_{R'}\cap\mathbb D(\class{gk-HR}{H_R})_{R'}.\]

By Grothendieck-Messing this filtration determines the lift. When \(\ker(R\to\overline{\mathbb F}_p)\) has divided powers, \(\mathbb D(\class{gk-HR}{H_R})_R\) is the constant block \(N\otimes_WR\), and the Hodge filtration is \(\mathrm{Fil}_R\cap(N\otimes_WR)\).

By the lemma, we may keep the formal group and forget the curve.

Definition 7 (the functor). Let \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\colon\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\to\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\) send a block-preserving deformation \(X_R\), with its identification \(\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\colon X_R\otimes\overline{\mathbb F}_p\cong\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\), to the lifted block with the identification that \(\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\) induces,

\[\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}(X_R)=\big(\class{gk-HR}{H_R},\ \class{gk-HR}{H_R}\otimes_R\overline{\mathbb F}_p=\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\cong\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\big),\]

and send an isomorphism of deformed curves to the isomorphism it induces on the lifted blocks.

The functor \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) is the higher version of sending an elliptic curve to its formal group, the Serre-Tate picture at height 2.

At \(k\ge2\) the curve has more deformations than the formal group can see, and we give them a name.

Definition 8 (the ghosts). The ghosts are \(\class{gk-ghost}{\mathfrak g}=\ker\big(d\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\colon T\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\to T\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\big)\), the first-order deformations of the curve that the formal group does not notice.

For the proof we will also need the Kodaira-Spencer class \(\class{gk-KS}{\mathrm{KS}}(v)\) of \(v\in\class{gk-Mk}{M_k}\), the displacement map \(\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}(v)=\class{gk-KS}{\mathrm{KS}}(v)\cup\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H}\in H^1(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O})\), the block-preserving directions \(T^{\mathrm{bl}}=T\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}=\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}^{-1}(\class{gk-N0}{\bar N_0})\subset\class{gk-Mk}{M_k}\), and the space \(\Omega_1\) of holomorphic forms dual to \(H^1(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O})_{\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}}\).

How the group acts on each space

Each of these spaces carries an action of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\), and in every case an element \(g\) acts by twisting the identification of the special fiber. On \(\class{gk-DefYB}{\mathrm{Def}(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k},\class{gk-B}{B})}\), a point is a deformation \((\mathcal Y,\mathcal B)\) with a marking \(\alpha\colon(\mathcal Y,\mathcal B)\otimes\overline{\mathbb F}_p\cong(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k},\class{gk-B}{B})\), and \(g\) sends \(\alpha\) to \(\bar g\circ\alpha\). This action goes through \(\class{gk-Gbar}{\bar G}\), since the center acts trivially on \(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k}\), and it is strict, since \(H^0(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k},T(-\class{gk-B}{B}))=0\). On \(\class{gk-DefXG}{\mathrm{Def}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\class{gk-Gc}{G})}\), the element \(g\) twists the identification with \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\), and the equivalence \(\class{gk-DefXG}{\mathrm{Def}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\class{gk-Gc}{G})}\simeq\class{gk-DefYB}{\mathrm{Def}(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k},\class{gk-B}{B})}\) is equivariant, so this is the same action.

The locus \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\subset\class{gk-DefYB}{\mathrm{Def}(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k},\class{gk-B}{B})}\) is stable under \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\): twisting the marking composes the quotient with the automorphism of \(\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\) induced by \(g\), which does not affect whether the block lifts. On \(\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\), the element \(g\) sends \(\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\) to \(g\circ\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\), and this is the action we are trying to model.

Every ingredient of \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) is natural in the marked object, so \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) is equivariant relative to our fixed isomorphism \(\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\cong\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\): that choice transports the action of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) on \(\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\) to a homomorphism \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\to\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\cong\class{gk-OD}{\mathcal O_D}^\times\), and \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) is equivariant for that homomorphism. We do not claim that it is conjugate to a prescribed embedding of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) in \(\class{gk-OD}{\mathcal O_D}^\times\).

Proof, in five steps

1 · Fixed lattice, moving line

Along any deformation the crystal of the curve stays fixed and only its Hodge filtration moves; where the block quotient lifts, it lifts uniquely, and that lift is \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\).

Thm: constancy · along any deformation the crystal stays put; only the Hodge filtration moves · Proved

Theorem (fixed lattice, moving line). Let \(X_R\) be the curve of a family of surgeries over an Artinian \(W\)-algebra \(R\) with residue field \(\kappa\) whose maximal ideal carries divided powers compatible with those on \((p)\) (this includes every square-zero extension of \(\kappa\), which is all the proofs use, and every \(W/p^n\); \(\kappa[\varepsilon]/(\varepsilon^{p+1})\) has none). Then \(H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(X_R/R)\cong H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}/W)\otimes_WR\) canonically, compatibly with Frobenius where \(R\) carries a lift of it: the crystal does not see the surgery, and only the Hodge filtration moves. This holds functorially, and \(\bar{G}\)-equivariantly over \(\kappa\).

A deformation changes the curve, but crystalline cohomology only sees the curve modulo nilpotents, so the lattice stays fixed and only the Hodge filtration moves.

The proof in steps

1
\(\mathrm{Spec}\,\kappa\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Spec}\,R\) is a divided-power thickening, and \(X_R\) is a deformation of \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) over it.
2
So \(H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(X_R/R)\) is the value of the crystal of \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) on this thickening, \(H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}\otimes_WR\) [Illusie]; only the Hodge filtration depends on \(X_R\).
3
The center acts on \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\), hence on the crystal by functoriality: equivariance over \(\kappa\).
Proof.

Since the maximal ideal of \(R\) carries divided powers compatible with those on \((p)\), \(\mathrm{Spec}\,\kappa\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Spec}\,R\) is a divided-power thickening, and \(X_R\) is a deformation of \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) over it. By the crystalline nature of de Rham cohomology [Illusie], \(H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(X_R/R)\) is the value of the crystal \(H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}/W)\) on this thickening, that is \(H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}\otimes_WR\); the identification is functorial in \(X_R\), and respects Frobenius when \(R\) carries a lift of it. Only the Hodge filtration depends on \(X_R\). The center \(\class{gk-Gc}{G}\) acts on \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\), hence on the crystal by functoriality, which gives the equivariance over \(\kappa\).

2 · The derivative is a cup product

A first-order deformation \(v\in\class{gk-Mk}{M_k}=H^1(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},T)^{\class{gk-Gc}{G}}\) of the curve has a Kodaira-Spencer class \(\class{gk-KS}{\mathrm{KS}}(v)\in H^1(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},T)\). The form \(\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H}\) turns vector fields into functions by contraction, \(\xi\mapsto\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H}(\xi)\), and this is the first map of the exact sequence of clean baby, \[0\to T_{\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}}\xrightarrow{\ \class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H}\ }\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O}_{\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}}\to\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O}_D\to0,\qquad D=\mathrm{div}(\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H}).\] On \(H^1\), the map \(H^1(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},T)\to H^1(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O})\) induced by contraction with \(\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H}\) is the cup product with \(\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H}\), so \(\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}(v)=\class{gk-KS}{\mathrm{KS}}(v)\cup\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H}\) is the Kodaira-Spencer class contracted with \(\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H}\). By Grothendieck-Messing and Katz-Oda, this class is how the Hodge line moves when the curve moves, so \(d\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}(v)=\class{gk-KS}{\mathrm{KS}}(v)\cup\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H}\) for \(v\) block-preserving.

3 · The derivative is onto (clean baby for all \(k\))

Contracting with \(\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H}\) gives \(0\to T\to\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O}\to\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O}_D\to0\), and \(H^1(\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O}_D)=0\), so \(d\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) is onto.

Lemma: cleanbaby · the derivative is onto (clean baby for all \(k\)): multiplying by \(\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H}\) loses nothing · Proved

Lemma (the derivative is onto; clean baby for all \(k\)). Let \(p\) be odd and \(k\ge1\). The displacement map \(\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}\colon \class{gk-Mk}{M_k}\to H^1(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O})_{\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}}\), \(v\mapsto\class{gk-KS}{\mathrm{KS}}(v)\cup\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H}\), is onto. Dually, \(\eta\mapsto\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H}\cdot\eta\) is injective from the forms dual to \(H^1(\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O})_{\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}}\) to the \(\class{gk-Gc}{G}\)-invariant quadratic differentials \(H^0(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\Omega^{\otimes2})^{\class{gk-Gc}{G}}\), which are dual to \(\class{gk-Mk}{M_k}\). At \(k=1\), with \(\omega_1=x^{p-3}dx\), this is my prequel's Lemma 10.11 (clean baby), and there the map is even an isomorphism (Proposition 10.14).

The proof in steps

1
\(\class{gk-Mk}{M_k}=H^1(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k},T(-\class{gk-B}{B}))=H^1(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},T)^{\class{gk-Gc}{G}}\), because \(\class{gk-Gc}{G}\) is tame. In the dual picture, a quadratic differential with a simple pole downstairs, \(\bigl(d(u^{p-1})\bigr)^2/u^{p-1}\), pulls back to \((p-1)^2u^{p-3}(du)^2\), holomorphic upstairs.
2
Contracting with \(\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H}\) gives \(0\to T\to\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O}\to\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O}_D\to0\) with \(D\) finite, so \(H^1(T)\to H^1(\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O})\) is onto.
3
Isotypic parts are exact for a group of order prime to \(p\), so \(\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}\) is onto \(H^1(\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O})_{\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}}\).
4
Dually, \(\eta\mapsto\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H}\cdot\eta\) is injective, because a product of two nonzero sections on a curve is nonzero.

This is the argument of Lemma 10.11 of Tiny and Unique (the case \(k=1\)), run for general \(k\).

Proof.

Since \(|\class{gk-Gc}{G}|=p-1\) is prime to \(p\), \(\class{gk-Mk}{M_k}=H^1(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k},T(-\class{gk-B}{B}))=H^1(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},T)^{\class{gk-Gc}{G}}\): near each of the \(p^k+1\) ramified points the invariant vector fields are \(u\,g(u^{p-1})\,\partial_u=(p-1)\,u^{p-1}g(u^{p-1})\,\partial_{u^{p-1}}\), which are the fields on \(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k}\) vanishing at \(\class{gk-B}{B}\). Contracting with \(\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H}\) gives an exact sequence \[0\to T_{\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}}\to\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O}_{\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}}\to\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O}_D\to0,\qquad D=\mathrm{div}(\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H}),\] which is \(\class{gk-Gc}{G}\)-equivariant once the middle term is twisted by the weight \(\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}\) of \(\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H}\) (the derivative is a cup product). Since \(D\) is finite, \(H^1(\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O}_D)=0\), so \(H^1(T)\to H^1(\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O})\) is onto; taking isotypic parts is exact for a group of order prime to \(p\), so \(\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}\colon\class{gk-Mk}{M_k}\to H^1(\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O})_{\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}}\) is onto. Dually, \(\eta\mapsto\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H}\cdot\eta\) is injective, because a product of two nonzero sections on a curve is nonzero.

Cor: dPhi · the derivative \(d\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) is onto, with kernel the ghosts · ♡

Corollary (the derivative is onto). Let \(p\) be odd and \(k\ge2\). The map \(d\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\colon T^{\mathrm{bl}}\otimes \kappa\to T\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\otimes \kappa\) is onto, the sequence of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-modules \[0\longrightarrow\class{gk-ghost}{\mathfrak g}\longrightarrow T^{\mathrm{bl}}\otimes \kappa\xrightarrow{\;d\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\;}T\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\otimes \kappa\longrightarrow0\] is exact, and \(d\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) identifies \(T^{\mathrm{bl}}\otimes \kappa/\class{gk-ghost}{\mathfrak g}\) with \(\class{gk-N0}{\bar{N}_0}\) as \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-modules.

Proof.
