By Definition def:block-preserving (block-preserving deformations), \(T^{\mathrm{bl}}=\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}^{-1}(\class{gk-N0}{\bar{N}_0})\), and by Theorem thm:Aprime(ii), \(d\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) is \(\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}\) restricted to \(T^{\mathrm{bl}}\) with target \(\class{gk-N0}{\bar{N}_0}=\mathrm{Hom}(\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H},\mathrm{Lie}\,\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}^\vee)=T\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\otimes \kappa\). The map \(\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}\) is onto by Lemma lem:cleanbaby, and a surjection restricted to the full preimage of a subspace is onto that subspace, so \(d\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) is onto; the kernel of \(d\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) is \(\class{gk-ghost}{\mathfrak g}\) by Definition def:ghost (ghosts). Since \(d\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) is \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-equivariant (Theorem thm:Aprime(ii)), \(d\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) identifies \(T^{\mathrm{bl}}\otimes \kappa/\class{gk-ghost}{\mathfrak g}\) with \(\class{gk-N0}{\bar{N}_0}\) as \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-modules. If the Conjecture in "For future work" holds, then \(\class{gk-N0}{\bar{N}_0}=\bar F^{\,p^{k-1}-1}H^1(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\mathcal O)_{\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}}\). What this gives is Corollary cor:dPhiW (the derivative on the block).

4 · Steering to good lifts

Good deformations always lift one more step, so \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\) is smooth (Schlessinger), and Nakayama makes \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) a projection.

Lemma: onestep · the block always lifts one more step, in \(T^{\mathrm{bl}}\otimes I\) ways · Proved

Lemma (steering to good lifts). Let \(R'\to R\) be a small extension with kernel \(I\), let \(X_R\) be block-preserving with lifted quotient \(\class{gk-HR}{H_R}\), and let \(X'\) be any lift of \(X_R\) to \(R'\). There is an element \[\mathrm{ob}(X')\ \in\ I\otimes_\kappa\big(H^1(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\mathcal{O})_{\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}}\big/\class{gk-N0}{\bar{N}_0}\big)\] such that \(X'\) is block-preserving iff \(\mathrm{ob}(X')=0\), and for \(v\in \class{gk-Mk}{M_k}\otimes I\), \[\mathrm{ob}(X'+v)\;=\;\mathrm{ob}(X')-\mathrm{pr}\big(\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}(v)\big),\qquad \class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}(v)=\class{gk-KS}{\mathrm{KS}}(v)\cup\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H},\quad \mathrm{pr}\colon H^1(\mathcal{O})_{\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}}\otimes I\to\big(H^1(\mathcal{O})_{\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}}/\class{gk-N0}{\bar{N}_0}\big)\otimes I .\] Consequently the block-preserving lifts of \(X_R\) to \(R'\) form a nonempty torsor under \(T^{\mathrm{bl}}\otimes_\kappaI\), \(T^{\mathrm{bl}}=\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}^{-1}(\class{gk-N0}{\bar{N}_0})\).

What this lemma says. A random lift of a block-preserving curve may fail to preserve the block, but it can always be fixed: changing the lift by a tangent vector \(v\) moves the obstruction, \(\mathrm{ob}(X^\prime+v)=\mathrm{ob}(X^\prime)-\mathrm{pr}(\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}(v))\), and since \(\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}\) is onto, some \(v\) kills it. So this lemma is about the locus \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\) where the quotient lifts: it shows that \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\) never gets stuck, one small step at a time, and that is what makes \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\) smooth (thm:horsmooth). The block can always be carried one step further, if you steer correctly.

Why a deformation need not lift the quotient. This is a dimension count, not this lemma. A tangent vector \(v\in\class{gk-Mk}{M_k}\) preserves the block exactly when \(\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}(v)\in\class{gk-N0}{\bar{N}_0}\), that is, \(T^{\mathrm{bl}}=\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}^{-1}(\class{gk-N0}{\bar{N}_0})\). The map \(\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}\) is onto all of \(H^1(\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O})_{\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}}\) (lem:cleanbaby), which is bigger than \(\class{gk-N0}{\bar{N}_0}\): its dimension is \(\dim\Omega_1\), against \(\dim\class{gk-N0}{\bar{N}_0}=\class{gk-h}{h}-1\), and \(\dim\Omega_1=10,58,91\) against \(\class{gk-h}{h}-1=5,19,17\) at the three computed points (thm:horsmooth). So many directions \(v\) land outside \(\class{gk-N0}{\bar{N}_0}\), and along those the quotient does not lift. The failure is the reason the steering is needed.

This is the implicit function theorem, done one order at a time; the fold above explains the picture.

The proof in steps

1
Let \(R^\prime\to R\) be a small extension with kernel \(I\), let \(X_R\) be a deformation over \(R\) along which the quotient has lifted to \(\mathrm{Jac}\,X_R[p^\infty]\twoheadrightarrow\class{gk-HR}{H_R}\), and let \(X^\prime\) be a deformation over \(R^\prime\) restricting to \(X_R\). We ask whether the quotient lifts again, to a quotient \(H^\prime\) of \(\mathrm{Jac}\,X^\prime[p^\infty]\) over \(R^\prime\) restricting to \(\class{gk-HR}{H_R}\).
2
By Grothendieck-Messing it does exactly when a class \(\mathrm{ob}(X^\prime)\in I\otimes(H^1(\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O})_{\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}}/\class{gk-N0}{\bar N_0})\) vanishes.
3
Changing \(X^\prime\) by \(v\in\class{gk-Mk}{M_k}\otimes I\) changes this class by \(-\mathrm{pr}(\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}(v))\).
4
Since \(\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}\) is onto (lem:cleanbaby), some \(v\) makes the class vanish, and the lifts along which the quotient lifts differ by \(T^{\mathrm{bl}}\otimes I\).
Proof.

Let \(R^\prime\to R\) be a small extension of Artinian \(W\)-algebras with residue field \(\kappa\) and kernel \(I\). Let \(X_R\) be a deformation over \(R\) along which the quotient \(\mathrm{Jac}\,\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}[p^\infty]\twoheadrightarrow\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\) has lifted to \(\mathrm{Jac}\,X_R[p^\infty]\twoheadrightarrow\class{gk-HR}{H_R}\), and let \(X^\prime\) be a deformation over \(R^\prime\) restricting to \(X_R\). We call \(X^\prime\) a good lift if the quotient lifts once more, to a quotient \(\mathrm{Jac}\,X^\prime[p^\infty]\twoheadrightarrow H^\prime\) over \(R^\prime\) restricting to \(\class{gk-HR}{H_R}\).

Now, let us ask what would prevent the quotient from lifting to \(R^\prime\). Put \(\text{Ш}^\prime=\mathbb D(\mathrm{Jac}\,X_R[p^\infty])(R^\prime)\) and \(N^\prime=\mathbb D(\class{gk-HR}{H_R})(R^\prime)\subset\text{Ш}^\prime\), a direct summand of rank \(\class{gk-h}{h}\). By thm:constancy neither depends on \(X^\prime\); only the Hodge filtration \(\mathrm{Fil}^\prime=\mathrm{Fil}(X^\prime)\subset\text{Ш}^\prime\) does. We choose \(\tilde e\in N^\prime\) lifting a generator of the Hodge line of \(\class{gk-HR}{H_R}\), and a complement \(N^\prime=R^\prime\tilde e\oplus N^{\prime\prime}\). By Grothendieck-Messing, \(X^\prime\) is a good lift exactly when \(\tilde e\in\mathrm{Fil}^\prime+N^{\prime\prime}\): if \(\tilde e=f+q\) with \(f\in\mathrm{Fil}^\prime\) and \(q\in N^{\prime\prime}\), then \(\tilde e-q\) spans a line of \(N^\prime\) inside \(\mathrm{Fil}^\prime\), and conversely. Since \(X_R\) is good, the class \(\mathrm{ob}(X^\prime)\) of \(\tilde e\) modulo \(\mathrm{Fil}^\prime+N^{\prime\prime}\) lies in \(I\otimes(H^1(\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O})_{\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}}/\class{gk-N0}{\bar N_0})\).

Let's grab the steering wheel and see what we can do: if we change the lift by \(v\in\class{gk-Mk}{M_k}\otimes I\), the Hodge filtration moves by \(\class{gk-KS}{\mathrm{KS}}(v)\) (Katz-Oda), so \(\mathrm{ob}(X^\prime+v)=\mathrm{ob}(X^\prime)-\mathrm{pr}(\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}(v))\). Since \(\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}\) is onto by lem:cleanbaby, some \(v\) makes the class vanish, and two good lifts differ exactly by \(\ker(\mathrm{pr}\circ\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}})\otimes I=T^{\mathrm{bl}}\otimes I\).

Intuition: steering to good lifts is the implicit function theorem, one order at a time

1. The ambient space. All deformations of the curve, \(\class{gk-DefYB}{\mathrm{Def}(Y_k,B)}\), form a smooth formal scheme of dimension \(\dim\class{gk-Mk}{M_k}\), because curves are unobstructed. At \((p,k)=(3,2)\) this is \(25\).

2. The equations. "The block lifts" is a list of equations on that space, one for each dimension of the space where the obstruction lives, \(H^1(\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O})_{\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}}/\class{gk-N0}{\bar N_0}\): that is \(\dim\Omega_1-(\class{gk-h}{h}-1)\) equations. At \((3,2)\): \(10-5=5\).

3. The implicit function theorem. If the derivative of the equations is onto, their zero set is smooth, of dimension (ambient) minus (number of equations). Here that derivative is \(\mathrm{pr}\circ\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}\), which is onto by the clean baby. So \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\) is smooth of dimension \(\dim\class{gk-Mk}{M_k}-\dim\Omega_1+(\class{gk-h}{h}-1)\). At \((3,2)\): \(25-5=20\).

4. Why one order at a time. On formal schemes we climb along small extensions \(R^\prime\to R\) with kernel \(I\) (for example \(\kappa[\varepsilon]/\varepsilon^3\to \kappa[\varepsilon]/\varepsilon^2\)). A lift \(X^\prime\) of a good deformation \(X_R\) always exists; its obstruction \(\mathrm{ob}(X^\prime)\) measures how far the lifted Hodge line sticks out of the block. Changing the lift by a tangent vector \(v\) moves the obstruction by \(\mathrm{pr}(\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}(v))\): that is the steering wheel. Since \(\mathrm{pr}\circ\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}\) is onto, we can steer to \(\mathrm{ob}(X^\prime)=0\), a good lift; and all good lifts differ by \(\ker(\mathrm{pr}\circ\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}})\otimes I=T^{\mathrm{bl}}\otimes I\).

5. What Schlessinger gets. Good lifts always exist, so \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\) is smooth, and its tangent space is \(T^{\mathrm{bl}}\). That is part (i) of Fullmetal Equivalent Exchange.

Thm: horsmooth · (i) \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\) is pro-representable, (ii) the functor \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) is formally smooth and a projection, (iii) the curve is a lift of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) · Proved

Theorem (Fullmetal Equivalent Exchange). Let \(p\) be odd and \(k\ge2\). Then:

(i) \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\) is pro-represented by a formally smooth \(W\)-algebra of dimension \[n_{\mathrm{hor}}\;=\;\dim \class{gk-Mk}{M_k}-\dim\Omega_1+h-1 \;=\;3g_{\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k}}-2+p^k-\dim\Omega_1+h-1 ,\] with tangent space \(T^{\mathrm{bl}}=\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}^{-1}(\class{gk-N0}{\bar{N}_0})\); it is a closed formal subscheme of \(\mathrm{Def}(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k},B)\), stable under the strict action of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) through the marking.

(ii) The functor \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\colon\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\to\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\) of Definition def:Phi (the functor) is formally smooth; in suitable coordinates it is the projection \[\mathrm{Spf} W[[u_1,\dots,u_{h-1},t_1,\dots,t_{n_{\mathrm{hor}}-h+1}]] \longrightarrow\mathrm{Spf} W[[u_1,\dots,u_{h-1}]],\] so its fibers are connected, formally smooth, with tangent space the ghosts \(\class{gk-ghost}{\mathfrak g}=\ker d\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\), of dimension \(n_{\mathrm{hor}}-h+1\). In this sense \(\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\simeq\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}/\class{gk-ghost}{\mathfrak g}\), where \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}/\class{gk-ghost}{\mathfrak g}\) is the groupoid quotient of \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\) in which two deformations with the same image under \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) are isomorphic.

(iii) The curve with its block is a lift of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\subseteq\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\): for a suitable choice of the isomorphism \(\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\cong \class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\), the homomorphism \(\iota\colon \class{gk-Gp}{G'}\to\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\cong \class{gk-OD}{\mathcal{O}_D}^\times\) that it induces is injective, with image the standard \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\). So \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) respects the marking action on the source and the action on \(\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\); hence \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}^*\colon\pi_0\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\to \pi_0\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\) is an injective \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-equivariant map of \(W\)-algebras, identifying \(\pi_0\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\) with the subring of functions constant along the fibers.

At \((3,2)\), \((5,2)\) and \((3,3)\) the dimension \(n_{\mathrm{hor}}\) is \(20\), \(104\) and \(185\), and \(\dim\class{gk-ghost}{\mathfrak g}\) is \(15\), \(85\) and \(168\).

The proof in steps

1
(i) Schlessinger: gluing works because the lifted quotient is unique, the tangent space is \(T^{\mathrm{bl}}\), and good lifts always exist (steering to good lifts), so \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\) is a smooth formal scheme; its dimension comes from the clean baby.
2
(ii) Since \(d\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) is onto (clean baby for all \(k\)), Nakayama gives coordinates in which \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) is the projection; the fibers have tangent space the ghosts.
3
(iii) \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) is natural in the marked curve, and the curve sees the right group.
Proof.

There are three things to show: that \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\) is a smooth formal scheme, that \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) is a projection, and that everything is \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-equivariant. We take them in turn.

(i) We check Schlessinger's conditions for \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\), which is a subfunctor of \(\class{gk-DefYB}{\mathrm{Def}(Y_k,B)}\) with one point over \(\kappa\). Gluing works because the lifted quotient is unique: if two good deformations agree over \(R_0\), their lifted quotients agree there, so they glue over \(R_1\times_{R_0}R_2\), and the comparison map is a bijection because it is one for \(\class{gk-DefYB}{\mathrm{Def}(Y_k,B)}\). Over \(\kappa[\varepsilon]/(\varepsilon^2)\), steering to good lifts (with \(R=\kappa\)) says that the good first-order deformations are exactly \(T^{\mathrm{bl}}\), so \(T^{\mathrm{bl}}\) is the tangent space. The same lemma says more: along every small extension, a good deformation has a good lift. So \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\) is pro-represented by a formally smooth \(W[[t_1,\dots,t_n]]\) with \(n=\dim T^{\mathrm{bl}}\).

Let's count how big it is. Since \(\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}\) is onto \(H^1(\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O})_{\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}}\) (clean baby for all \(k\)), \(T^{\mathrm{bl}}=\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}^{-1}(\class{gk-N0}{\bar N_0})\) has codimension \(\dim\Omega_1-(\class{gk-h}{h}-1)\) in \(\class{gk-Mk}{M_k}\), and \(\dim\class{gk-Mk}{M_k}=3g_{Y_k}-3+p^k+1\) by decorate the center. Since \(T^{\mathrm{bl}}\to\class{gk-Mk}{M_k}\) is injective, the map of hulls is onto, so \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\) is a closed formal subscheme of \(\class{gk-DefYB}{\mathrm{Def}(Y_k,B)}\). It is stable under \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\), because \(g\in\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) only changes the identification of the quotient, not whether it lifts; and the action is strict because \(\class{gk-DefYB}{\mathrm{Def}(Y_k,B)}\) has no infinitesimal automorphisms, \(H^0(Y_k,T(-B))=0\).

(ii) Now we look at \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) in coordinates. Write \(B=W[[t_1,\dots,t_n]]\) for the ring of \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\), \(C=W[[u_1,\dots,u_{h-1}]]\) for Lubin-Tate space, and \(f_i=\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}^*(u_i)\). Since \(d\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) is onto (clean baby for all \(k\)), the \(f_i\) are linearly independent in \(\mathfrak m_B/(\mathfrak m_B^2+pB)\), and we can complete them by \(t^\prime_1,\dots,t^\prime_{n-h+1}\) to a basis. The map \(W[[u,t^\prime]]\to B\) sending \(u_i\mapsto f_i\) and \(t^\prime_j\mapsto t^\prime_j\) is then an isomorphism on \(\mathfrak m/(\mathfrak m^2+p)\), so it is onto by Nakayama; modulo \(p\) both sides are power series rings in the same number of variables, so it is an isomorphism. In these coordinates \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}^*\) is the inclusion \(W[[u]]\subset W[[u,t^\prime]]\): \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) is the projection, its fibers are \(\mathrm{Spf}\,W[[t^\prime]]\), and their tangent space is \(\ker d\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\), the ghosts \(\class{gk-ghost}{\mathfrak g}\). In these coordinates the lifting property is visible. Let \(A\to A_0\) be a surjective map of Artinian local \(W\)-algebras with residue field \(\kappa\). A point of \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}(A_0)\) is a choice of values in \(\mathfrak m_{A_0}\) for the \(u_i\) and the \(t^\prime_j\), and a point of \(\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}(A)\) over it is a choice of lifts of the \(u_i\) to \(\mathfrak m_A\). We lift the \(t^\prime_j\) to \(\mathfrak m_A\) in any way. So \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) is formally smooth.

(iii) Last, equivariance. Every ingredient of \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) is natural in the marked curve, and moving the marking by \(g\in\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) changes the identification \(\class{gk-HR}{H_R}\otimes \kappa=\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\cong\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) by \(\iota(g)\). And the curve sees the right group (prop:rho): \(\iota\) is injective, and its image is the standard \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\). Reading (ii) on functions, \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}^*\) is injective because \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) is onto, and its image \(W[[u]]\) is the ring of functions constant along the fibers.

The numbers: \(\dim\class{gk-Mk}{M_k}=25,143,259\), \(\dim\Omega_1=10,58,91\) and \(h-1=5,19,17\) at the three points.

Example: the dimensions in (i) and (ii) at \((p,k)=(3,2),(5,2),(3,3)\)

The numbers at \((3,2)\), \((5,2)\), \((3,3)\) come from \(\dim \class{gk-Mk}{M_k}\), \(\dim\Omega_1\) and \(h-1\).

\((p,k)\)\(\class{gk-h}{h}\)\(\dim\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}=\class{gk-h}{h}-1\)(i) \(n_{\mathrm{hor}}=\dim\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\)(ii) \(\dim\class{gk-ghost}{\mathfrak g}=n_{\mathrm{hor}}-(\class{gk-h}{h}-1)\)
\((3,2)\)\(6\)\(5\)\(25-10+5=20\)\(20-5=15\)
\((5,2)\)\(20\)\(19\)\(143-58+19=104\)\(104-19=85\)
\((3,3)\)\(18\)\(17\)\(259-91+17=185\)\(185-17=168\)

The column (i) is \(n_{\mathrm{hor}}=\dim\class{gk-Mk}{M_k}-\dim\Omega_1+(\class{gk-h}{h}-1)\), with \(\dim\class{gk-Mk}{M_k}=25,143,259\) and \(\dim\Omega_1=10,58,91\) as quoted at the end of the proof. Already at \((3,2)\), the fibers of \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) have \(15\) ghost directions over the \(5\) directions of Lubin-Tate space.

5 · The curve sees the right group

\(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) acts on the formal group faithfully, as the standard \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\).

Remark (why lem:Dw: the formal group is a quotient, not a summand). At \(k=1\) the idempotents of the center split the \(p\)-divisible group of the Jacobian, and the summand of dimension one is the formal group (Tiny and Unique, Theorem C.1 and Corollary C.1′). At \(k\ge2\) no idempotent isolates it. Dwork's theorem on slopes finds the part \(\class{gk-Nblk}{N}\) of slope \(1/\class{gk-h}{h}\) of the crystal of the curve: of rank \(\class{gk-h}{h}\), with \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) acting with characteristic polynomial \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}\). The formal group appears only as a quotient \(\mathrm{Jac}\,\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}[p^\infty]\twoheadrightarrow\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\cong\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\). At \(k\ge2\) a deformation of the curve need not lift this quotient, so the space is the locus \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\) where it does lift, and only there is \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) defined. \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\) is bigger than Lubin-Tate space, so \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) has fibers of positive dimension: the ghosts \(\class{gk-ghost}{\mathfrak g}=\ker d\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) are their tangent directions.

The lemma used, then the proposition:

Lemma: Dw · (i) a faithful character has slopes \(n/\class{gk-h}{h}\), \(n=1,\dots,\class{gk-h}{h}-1\), each once, (ii) a character of smaller order has slopes \(>1/\class{gk-h}{h}\), (iii) the slope \(1/\class{gk-h}{h}\) part is a line over \(\mathbb Q_p(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}})\), with \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) acting as \(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}\) · Proved (cited)

Lemma (Dwork slopes). Let \(p\) be odd, \(k\ge 1\), \(h=p^{k-1}(p-1)\). For a character \(\psi\colon \mathbb{Z}/p^k\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_p^{\times}\) let \(L(\psi,T)\) be the \(L\)-function over \(\mathbb{A}^1_{\mathbb{F}_p}\) of the cover \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\smallsetminus\{\infty\}\to\mathbb{A}^1\), with \(\psi\) evaluated on Frobenius elements. Slopes are \(p\)-adic, normalized by \(v_p(p)=1\).

(i) If \(\psi\) is faithful, then \(L(\psi,T)\) is a polynomial of degree \(h-1\) with slopes \(n/h\), \(n=1,\dots,h-1\), each of multiplicity one.

(ii) If \(\psi\) has order \(p^j\) with \(1\le j<k\), then \(L(\psi,T)\) has slopes \(n/\varphi(p^j)\), \(n=1,\dots,\varphi(p^j)-1\), each of multiplicity one. In particular its smallest slope is \(1/\varphi(p^j)>1/h\).

(iii) Let \(N\subset H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}/\mathbb{Z}_p)\otimes\mathbb{Q}_p\) be the part of slope \(1/h\). Then \(\dim_{\mathbb{Q}_p}N=h\), \(N\) is stable under \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\), and the characteristic polynomial of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) on \(N\) is the cyclotomic polynomial \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}\). Thus \(N\) is a vector space of dimension one over \(\mathbb{Q}_p(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}})\), with \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) acting as \(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}\).

The proof in steps

1
Let \(\psi\colon\mathbb Z/p^k\to\overline{\mathbb Q}_p^{\times}\) be a character of the Galois group of \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\)\(\to\mathbb P^1\), which is generated by \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\). Its sum over \(\mathbb F_{p^r}\) is \(S_r(\psi)=\sum_z\psi(\text{Frobenius element at }z)\), and \(L(\psi,T)=\exp\bigl(\sum_r S_r(\psi)T^r/r\bigr)\). The Newton polygon of \(L(\psi,T)\) records the \(p\)-adic sizes of its reciprocal roots (its slopes), and the Hodge polygon is the lower bound for them given by the degree of \(f(z)=z^{p-1}\). We start at \(k=1\). For \(\psi\) of order \(p\) the sum is a sum of Gauss sums, Hasse-Davenport makes \(L(\psi,T)\) a product of \(p-2\) linear factors, and Stickelberger gives their slopes \(n/(p-1)\), each once. So at \(k=1\) the Newton polygon equals the Hodge polygon.
2
For \(k\ge2\) the sums are no longer Gauss sums: a character of order \(p^k\) also reads the carries of the Witt trace, which is not additive (the explanation just below shows why). Liu and Wan's \(T\)-adic exponential sums still work. They put the sums for every character of \(\mathbb Z/p,\mathbb Z/p^2,\dots\) into one power series in \(T\), and choosing a character \(\psi\) means plugging in \(T=\pi_\psi=\psi(1)-1\). Let \(L^*(\psi,s)=L(\psi,s)(1-s)\) be the \(L\)-function over \(\mathbb G_m\), and let the \(C\)-function be \(C_f(s,\pi_\psi)=\prod_{i\ge0}L^*(\psi,p^is)\). Then \(L^*(\psi,s)=C_f(s,\pi_\psi)/C_f(ps,\pi_\psi)\). In units of \(T\), \(C_f\) has slopes \(0,1,2,\dots\), each once: this holds at \(k=1\) by the first step, and [LWan, Theorem 2.9] carries it to every nontrivial \(\psi\). For faithful \(\psi\), \(v_p(\pi_\psi)=1/\class{gk-h}{h}\), so \(C_f\) has slopes \(0,1/\class{gk-h}{h},2/\class{gk-h}{h},\dots\), each once. Dividing by \(C_f(ps)\) and dropping the factor \(1-s\) leaves exactly the slopes \(n/\class{gk-h}{h}\), \(0<n<\class{gk-h}{h}\), each once. This proves (i).
Explanation of the main technique for Newton polygons: the same idea as for \(k=1\), but we need Liu and Wan's \(T\)-adic exponential sums

In order, each resting on the one below: (1) counting points is summing characters; (2) slopes are sizes of Frobenius eigenvalues; (3) at \(k=1\) the sums are Gauss sums, and Stickelberger gives their sizes; (4) for \(k\ge2\) the sums stop being Gauss sums; (5) the \(T\) trick climbs over that wall.

1

Counting points is summing characters.

Take the \(k=1\) curve \(y^p-y=x^{p-1}\) over \(\mathbb F_q\). For a fixed \(x\), put \(a=x^{p-1}\). The map \(y\mapsto y^p-y\) is additive with kernel \(\mathbb F_p\), so it hits each value either \(p\) times or not at all, and it hits \(a\) exactly when \(\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathbb F_q/\mathbb F_p}(a)=0\) (Hilbert 90 for Artin-Schreier).

A yes/no condition is an average of characters: summing all additive characters \(\psi\) of \(\mathbb F_p\), \[\sum_{\psi}\psi(\mathrm{Tr}\,a)=\begin{cases}p&\mathrm{Tr}(a)=0,\\0&\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}\] which is exactly the number of \(y\). So \[\#\{\text{affine points}\}=\sum_{x}\sum_{\psi}\psi\bigl(\mathrm{Tr}\,x^{p-1}\bigr)=q+\sum_{\psi\ne1}S(\psi),\qquad S(\psi)=\sum_{x\in\mathbb F_q}\psi\bigl(\mathrm{Tr}\,x^{p-1}\bigr).\]

The trivial character gives the expected count \(q\); each nontrivial character \(\psi\) contributes one exponential sum \(S(\psi)\), and these carry the information about the curve. The curve splits into pieces, one for each character of its Galois group \(\mathbb Z/p\), each measured by its own sum. For general \(k\) the group is \(\mathbb Z/p^k\): more characters, more pieces.

This is Lefschetz. The points of the curve are the fixed points of Frobenius, and the Lefschetz trace formula counts them by traces: \(\#X(\mathbb F_q)=1-\mathrm{Tr}(\mathrm{Frob}\mid H^1)+q\). The group \(\mathbb Z/p\) splits \(H^1=\bigoplus_{\psi\ne1}H^1_\psi\), one piece per character, with projector \(e_\psi=\frac1p\sum_g\psi(g)^{-1}g\), and \(\mathrm{Tr}(\mathrm{Frob}\mid H^1_\psi)=\frac1p\sum_g\psi(g)^{-1}\,\mathrm{Tr}(\mathrm{Frob}\circ g)\), where each \(\mathrm{Tr}(\mathrm{Frob}\circ g)\) counts the fixed points of the twisted Frobenius \(\mathrm{Frob}\circ g\). Untangling gives \(S(\psi)=-\mathrm{Tr}(\mathrm{Frob}\mid H^1_\psi)\), up to the point at \(\infty\): the character sum is the trace of Frobenius on the \(\psi\)-piece of the cohomology. (The same idea opens my Gauss sums note, and it is how the proof of lem:Dw passes to crystalline cohomology.)

2

Slopes are sizes of Frobenius eigenvalues.

Lefschetz holds over every \(\mathbb F_{q^r}\), not just \(\mathbb F_q\). Writing \(\alpha_1,\alpha_2,\dots\) for the eigenvalues of Frobenius on the \(\psi\)-piece \(H^1_\psi\), \[S_r(\psi)=-\mathrm{Tr}\bigl(\mathrm{Frob}^r\mid H^1_\psi\bigr)=-\sum_i\alpha_i^{\,r}.\] Package all \(r\) at once: \[L(\psi,s)=\exp\Bigl(\sum_{r\ge1}S_r(\psi)\frac{s^r}{r}\Bigr)=\prod_i(1-\alpha_is)=\det\bigl(1-s\,\mathrm{Frob}\mid H^1_\psi\bigr).\] So the \(L\)-function of the sums is the characteristic polynomial of Frobenius on the \(\psi\)-piece: its reciprocal roots are the eigenvalues.

The eigenvalues are algebraic integers, and their \(p\)-adic sizes \(v_q(\alpha_i)\in[0,1]\) (normalized by \(v_q(q)=1\)) are the slopes. They are the slopes of the Newton polygon of \(L(\psi,s)\), and they are the slopes of the crystal of the curve on that piece: a piece of slope \(1/\class{gk-h}{h}\) is the Dieudonné module of a one-dimensional formal group of height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\). So finding the block means finding the eigenvalues of size exactly \(1/\class{gk-h}{h}\). Over \(\mathbb F_p\) (as in lem:Dw), \(q=p\) and the slope is \(v_p(\alpha)\).

3

At \(k=1\) the sums are Gauss sums.

For \(\psi\) of order \(p\), count solutions of \(z^{p-1}=u\) with the multiplicative characters \(\omega^{-j}\circ N\), \(j=0,\dots,p-2\) (the tame part enters here): \(S_r(\psi)=\sum_{j=1}^{p-2}g_r(\omega^{-j})\), a sum of Gauss sums. Hasse-Davenport, \(-g_r(\chi)=(-g_1(\chi))^r\), makes the \(L\)-function a product of linear factors, \(L(\psi,s)=\prod_{j=1}^{p-2}\bigl(1+g_1(\omega^{-j})\,s\bigr)\), so the eigenvalues are the Gauss sums themselves, and Stickelberger gives their sizes: \(v_p\bigl(g_1(\omega^{-j})\bigr)=j/(p-1)\). Slopes \(j/(p-1)\), each once (the case \(k=1\) of lem:Dw; geometrically, my Gauss sums note).


My note "The Frobenius eigenvalues of Artin-Schreier-Witt curves are Gauss sums" (rin.io/pdfs/Gauss_sums.pdf) writes Frobenius at \(k=1\) as the Gauss-sum correspondence \(G=-\sum_b\chi(b)\psi(b)\), an element of the group ring of the automorphisms following Coleman's proof of Hasse-Davenport.

By Stickelberger, its eigenvalue on the \(j=1\) piece is a unit times \(\lambda=1-\zeta\), and \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) acts there as \(\zeta\); so \(F=(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\cdot\text{unit}\) on the formal group, the one identity which shines through each section.


So the correspondence may be a global, geometric form of the one identity at \(k=1\) (lem:F2 at every \(k\)).

4

For \(k\ge2\) the sums stop being Gauss sums.

The sums for \(k\ge2\) are built from the Witt trace. Let's review the Witt trace first, in case Witt vectors are new friends for you. Let \(q=p^r\), and let \(\mathbb Z_q\) be the ring of integers of the unramified extension of \(\mathbb Q_p\) of degree \(r\).

The Witt vectors of length \(k\) over \(\mathbb F_q\) are \(\mathbb Z_q\) cut off after \(k\) \(p\)-adic digits: \(W_k(\mathbb F_q)=\mathbb Z_q/p^k\), and in particular \(W_k(\mathbb F_p)=\mathbb Z/p^k\).

Frobenius acts on \(\mathbb Z_q\), and the Witt trace is the field trace from \(\mathbb Z_q\) down to \(\mathbb Z_p\), reduced mod \(p^k\): \[\mathrm{Tr}_W(w)=w+\mathrm{Frob}(w)+\dots+\mathrm{Frob}^{r-1}(w)\in\mathbb Z/p^k.\] The Teichmüller lift \([u]\) of \(u\in\mathbb F_q\) is the root of unity in \(\mathbb Z_q\) that reduces to \(u\), with \([0]=0\). The Teichmüller lift is multiplicative: \([uv]=[u][v]\). In other words, \(\mathrm{Tr}_W([u])\) is the trace of \(u\) remembered to \(k\) digits. The first digit of \(\mathrm{Tr}_W([u])\) is the ordinary trace \(\mathrm{Tr}(u)\in\mathbb F_p\), and the higher digits carry more.

Let's see the carry once, in \(\mathbb Z_5\) mod \(25\). We have \([1]=1\), and \([2]\) is the fourth root of unity that is \(2\) mod \(5\), namely \(7\) (since \(7^2=49\equiv-1\)). So \([1]+[1]=2\) but \([1+1]=[2]\equiv7\pmod{25}\): equal mod \(5\), different mod \(25\). In other words, the Teichmüller lift is not additive past the first digit. The difference is the carry, which is \(C(z,w)=\bigl(z^p+w^p-(z+w)^p\bigr)/p\) in my Gauss sums note.


Why does the Witt trace appear?

The reason is Artin-Schreier-Witt theory. At \(k=1\), Frobenius acts on the solutions of \(y^p-y=a\) by adding \(\mathrm{Tr}(a)\in\mathbb Z/p\), which is why the point count above asks for \(\mathrm{Tr}(a)=0\).

For general \(k\), let \(\wp(y)=\mathrm{Frob}(y)-y\) on Witt vectors of length \(k\). Frobenius acts on the solutions of \(\wp(y)=(a,0,\dots,0)=[a]\) by adding \(\mathrm{Tr}_W([a])\in\mathbb Z/p^k\). So \(\mathrm{Tr}_W([z^{p-1}])\) is the Galois element that Frobenius picks at the point over \(z\), and a character \(\psi\) of \(\mathbb Z/p^k\) reads that Galois element: \[S_r(\psi)=\sum_z\psi\bigl(\mathrm{Tr}_W[z^{p-1}]\bigr).\]

Here is where the Gauss sum argument gets gunked up beyond k=1: a Gauss sum needs an additive character of \(\mathbb F_q\). For \(\psi\) of order \(p\), the map \(u\mapsto\psi(\mathrm{Tr}_W[u])\) reads only the first digit, the ordinary trace, which is additive in \(u\). So the Gauss sum argument works. For \(\psi\) of order \(p^m\), \(m\ge2\), the map \(u\mapsto\psi(\mathrm{Tr}_W[u])\) reads the carries too, so the map is not additive, and there is no Gauss sum to form. \(L(\psi,s)\) is still a polynomial (of degree \(\class{gk-h}{h}-1\) for faithful \(\psi\)) whose reciprocal roots are the eigenvalues, but there is no formula for these eigenvalues.

(My note tries the \(\mathbb Z/p^2\) case anyway: its Theorem 4.3 is conditional on its Conjecture 4.4. So lem:Dw uses the \(T\)-adic sums of Liu and Wan instead.)

5

The \(T\) trick: keep the group element, choose the character last.

Take the whole tower \(\mathbb Z/p,\mathbb Z/p^2,\dots\) together, which is the group \(\mathbb Z_p\), and record the Galois element that Frobenius picks at \(z\) as the \(p\)-adic integer \(a_z=\mathrm{Tr}\bigl(\hat z^{\,p-1}\bigr)\in\mathbb Z_p\), where \(\hat z\) is the Teichmüller lift of \(z\).

Write \(a_z\) in the group ring of \(\mathbb Z_p\), which is \(\mathbb Z_p[[T]]\) (the Iwasawa algebra), where the group element \(a\) becomes \((1+T)^a\): \[S_r(T)=\sum_{z}(1+T)^{a_z}\in\mathbb Z_p[[T]].\] Choosing a character is plugging in a value of \(T\). At \(T=\zeta_{p^m}-1\) we get \((1+T)^a=\zeta_{p^m}^{\,a}\), and \(S_r(T)\) becomes the sum for a character of order \(p^m\). In other words, one power series holds every \(k\).


So we never compute the sums for general \(k\). We study the one \(T\)-adic power series \(S_r(T)\). The Newton polygon of the \(T\)-adic object has slopes \(0,1,2,\dots\) in units of \(T\).

The case \(k=1\), where the Gauss sum argument works, forces these slopes, together with the theorem of Liu and Wan [LWan, Theorem 2.9]. Each \(k\) is then a change of units: \(T=\zeta_{p^k}-1\) has size \(v_p(T)=1/\class{gk-h}{h}\), so slope \(j\) in \(T\) becomes slope \(j/\class{gk-h}{h}\) in \(p\). The case \(k=1\) controls the tower.

3
A character of order \(p^j\) with \(1\le j<k\) factors through the Galois group of \(X_j\to\mathbb{P}^1\), so its \(L\)-function is the one attached to \(X_j\), and the second step applies with \(j\) in place of \(k\). This proves (ii).
4
For the cohomology, we split \(H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}\) into eigenspaces \(H_\psi\) of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\), and the Lefschetz trace formula gives \(\det(1-FT\mid H_\psi)=L(\psi,T)\) for \(\psi\ne1\). By (i) each faithful \(\psi\) contributes a line of slope \(1/h\), and by (ii) no other \(\psi\) contributes. There are \(h\) faithful characters, and they form one Galois orbit. This proves (iii).
Proof.

Step 1: \(k=1\). Let \(\psi\) have order \(p\) and let \(q=p^r\). The characters \(\chi\) of \(\mathbb{F}_q^{\times}\) with \(\chi^{p-1}=1\) are \(\omega^{-j}\circ N_{\mathbb{F}_q/\mathbb{F}_p}\), \(j=0,\dots,p-2\), where \(\omega\) is the Teichmüller character. Counting solutions of \(z^{p-1}=u\) with these characters, \[S_r(\psi):=\sum_{z\in\mathbb{F}_q}\psi\bigl(\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathbb{F}_q/\mathbb{F}_p}z^{p-1}\bigr) =1+\sum_{j=0}^{p-2}\ \sum_{u\in\mathbb{F}_q^{\times}}\omega^{-j}(N_{\mathbb{F}_q/\mathbb{F}_p}u)\,\psi(\mathrm{Tr}\,u) =\sum_{j=1}^{p-2} g_r(\omega^{-j}),\] since the term \(j=0\) equals \(-1\). Here \(g_r(\chi)\) is the Gauss sum of \(\chi\circ N_{\mathbb{F}_q/\mathbb{F}_p}\) over \(\mathbb{F}_q\). By the Hasse-Davenport relation \(-g_r(\chi)=(-g_1(\chi))^r\), so \[L(\psi,T)=\exp\Bigl(\sum_{r\ge1}S_r(\psi)\frac{T^r}{r}\Bigr) =\prod_{j=1}^{p-2}\bigl(1+g_1(\omega^{-j})\,T\bigr).\] By Stickelberger's theorem \(v_p\bigl(g_1(\omega^{-j})\bigr)=j/(p-1)\) for \(0\le j\le p-2\). Hence \(L(\psi,T)\) has degree \(p-2\) and slopes \(n/(p-1)\), \(n=1,\dots,p-2\), each once. This is (i) for \(k=1\).

Step 2: faithful \(\psi\), any \(k\). We hope to get every \(k\) from \(k=1\). The tool is the \(T\)-adic exponential sum of [LWan] for \(f(z)=z^{p-1}\) over \(\mathbb{F}_p\). In the notation of [LWan], \(a=1\), \(\Delta=[0,p-1]\), \(D=p-1\), and \(W(j)=1\) for every \(j\ge0\). For a character \(\psi\) of order \(p^m\) put \(\pi_\psi=\psi(1)-1\), so \(v_p(\pi_\psi)=1/\bigl(p^{m-1}(p-1)\bigr)\).

Let's see why the \(T\)-adic sum knows about our cover. The Frobenius element of the cover at a point \(z\in\mathbb{F}_{p^r}\) is the Witt trace \(\mathrm{Tr}_{W_k(\mathbb{F}_{p^r})/W_k(\mathbb{F}_p)}\bigl([z^{p-1}]\bigr)\). This Witt trace is the class of \(\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p^r}/\mathbb{Q}_p}(\hat{z}^{\,p-1})\) modulo \(p^k\), with \(\hat{z}\) the Teichmüller lift of \(z\). Therefore the specialization \(T=\pi_\psi\) of the \(T\)-adic sum of \(f\) is the character sum of \(\psi\) over the torus \(\mathbb{G}_m\). Let \(L^{*}(\psi,s)\) be the \(L\)-function of \(\psi\) over \(\mathbb{G}_m\), and let \(C_f(s,T)\) be the \(C\)-function of [LWan]. Then \[L^{*}(\psi,s)=L(\psi,s)\,(1-s)=\frac{C_f(s,\pi_\psi)}{C_f(ps,\pi_\psi)},\qquad C_f(s,\pi_\psi)=\prod_{i\ge0}L^{*}(\psi,p^{i}s).\] The factor \(1-s\) accounts for the point \(z=0\), at which Frobenius is trivial.

Next we compare two polygons. The Newton polygon \(\mathrm{NP}_{\pi_\psi}(f)\) is the Newton polygon of \(C_f(s,\pi_\psi)\) in the variable \(s\), measured in \(\mathrm{ord}_{\pi_\psi}\). The Hodge polygon \(\mathrm{HP}_p(\Delta)\) is the lower bound of [LWan] for the Newton polygon. In terms of \(\mathrm{ord}_{\pi_\psi}\), the Hodge polygon \(\mathrm{HP}_p(\Delta)\) has the slopes \(j=0,1,2,\dots\), each of length one. We start with \(\psi\) of order \(p\). By the case \(k=1\) above, the polynomial \(L^{*}(\psi,s)\) has slopes \(n/(p-1)\), \(n=0,\dots,p-2\), so \(C_f(s,\pi_\psi)\) has slopes \(n/(p-1)\) for all \(n\ge0\), each once. Since \(v_p(\pi_\psi)=1/(p-1)\), these slopes say \(\mathrm{NP}_{\pi_\psi}(f)=\mathrm{HP}_p(\Delta)\). The theorem of Liu and Wan [LWan, Theorem 2.9] then gives the same equality for every nontrivial \(\psi\).

Now let \(\psi\) be faithful, of order \(p^k\). Then \(v_p(\pi_\psi)=1/\class{gk-h}{h}\), so \(C_f(s,\pi_\psi)\) has slopes \(j/\class{gk-h}{h}\), \(j\ge0\), each once, and \(C_f(ps,\pi_\psi)\) has slopes \(1+j/\class{gk-h}{h}\). The quotient \(L^{*}(\psi,s)\) has slopes \(j/\class{gk-h}{h}\), \(0\le j<\class{gk-h}{h}\), and dividing by \(1-s\) removes \(j=0\). This proves (i). As a sanity check, the degree agrees with [LWei, Theorem 1.3], and the slopes agree with [DWX, Example 1.4].

Explanation of the main technique for Newton polygons: the same idea as for \(k=1\), but we need Liu and Wan's \(T\)-adic exponential sums

In order, each resting on the one below: (1) counting points is summing characters; (2) slopes are sizes of Frobenius eigenvalues; (3) at \(k=1\) the sums are Gauss sums, and Stickelberger gives their sizes; (4) for \(k\ge2\) the sums stop being Gauss sums; (5) the \(T\) trick climbs over that wall.

1

Counting points is summing characters.

Take the \(k=1\) curve \(y^p-y=x^{p-1}\) over \(\mathbb F_q\). For a fixed \(x\), put \(a=x^{p-1}\). The map \(y\mapsto y^p-y\) is additive with kernel \(\mathbb F_p\), so it hits each value either \(p\) times or not at all, and it hits \(a\) exactly when \(\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathbb F_q/\mathbb F_p}(a)=0\) (Hilbert 90 for Artin-Schreier).

A yes/no condition is an average of characters: summing all additive characters \(\psi\) of \(\mathbb F_p\), \[\sum_{\psi}\psi(\mathrm{Tr}\,a)=\begin{cases}p&\mathrm{Tr}(a)=0,\\0&\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}\] which is exactly the number of \(y\). So \[\#\{\text{affine points}\}=\sum_{x}\sum_{\psi}\psi\bigl(\mathrm{Tr}\,x^{p-1}\bigr)=q+\sum_{\psi\ne1}S(\psi),\qquad S(\psi)=\sum_{x\in\mathbb F_q}\psi\bigl(\mathrm{Tr}\,x^{p-1}\bigr).\]

The trivial character gives the expected count \(q\); each nontrivial character \(\psi\) contributes one exponential sum \(S(\psi)\), and these carry the information about the curve. The curve splits into pieces, one for each character of its Galois group \(\mathbb Z/p\), each measured by its own sum. For general \(k\) the group is \(\mathbb Z/p^k\): more characters, more pieces.

This is Lefschetz. The points of the curve are the fixed points of Frobenius, and the Lefschetz trace formula counts them by traces: \(\#X(\mathbb F_q)=1-\mathrm{Tr}(\mathrm{Frob}\mid H^1)+q\). The group \(\mathbb Z/p\) splits \(H^1=\bigoplus_{\psi\ne1}H^1_\psi\), one piece per character, with projector \(e_\psi=\frac1p\sum_g\psi(g)^{-1}g\), and \(\mathrm{Tr}(\mathrm{Frob}\mid H^1_\psi)=\frac1p\sum_g\psi(g)^{-1}\,\mathrm{Tr}(\mathrm{Frob}\circ g)\), where each \(\mathrm{Tr}(\mathrm{Frob}\circ g)\) counts the fixed points of the twisted Frobenius \(\mathrm{Frob}\circ g\). Untangling gives \(S(\psi)=-\mathrm{Tr}(\mathrm{Frob}\mid H^1_\psi)\), up to the point at \(\infty\): the character sum is the trace of Frobenius on the \(\psi\)-piece of the cohomology. (The same idea opens my Gauss sums note, and it is how the proof of lem:Dw passes to crystalline cohomology.)

2

Slopes are sizes of Frobenius eigenvalues.

Lefschetz holds over every \(\mathbb F_{q^r}\), not just \(\mathbb F_q\). Writing \(\alpha_1,\alpha_2,\dots\) for the eigenvalues of Frobenius on the \(\psi\)-piece \(H^1_\psi\), \[S_r(\psi)=-\mathrm{Tr}\bigl(\mathrm{Frob}^r\mid H^1_\psi\bigr)=-\sum_i\alpha_i^{\,r}.\] Package all \(r\) at once: \[L(\psi,s)=\exp\Bigl(\sum_{r\ge1}S_r(\psi)\frac{s^r}{r}\Bigr)=\prod_i(1-\alpha_is)=\det\bigl(1-s\,\mathrm{Frob}\mid H^1_\psi\bigr).\] So the \(L\)-function of the sums is the characteristic polynomial of Frobenius on the \(\psi\)-piece: its reciprocal roots are the eigenvalues.

The eigenvalues are algebraic integers, and their \(p\)-adic sizes \(v_q(\alpha_i)\in[0,1]\) (normalized by \(v_q(q)=1\)) are the slopes. They are the slopes of the Newton polygon of \(L(\psi,s)\), and they are the slopes of the crystal of the curve on that piece: a piece of slope \(1/\class{gk-h}{h}\) is the Dieudonné module of a one-dimensional formal group of height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\). So finding the block means finding the eigenvalues of size exactly \(1/\class{gk-h}{h}\). Over \(\mathbb F_p\) (as in lem:Dw), \(q=p\) and the slope is \(v_p(\alpha)\).

3

At \(k=1\) the sums are Gauss sums.

For \(\psi\) of order \(p\), count solutions of \(z^{p-1}=u\) with the multiplicative characters \(\omega^{-j}\circ N\), \(j=0,\dots,p-2\) (the tame part enters here): \(S_r(\psi)=\sum_{j=1}^{p-2}g_r(\omega^{-j})\), a sum of Gauss sums. Hasse-Davenport, \(-g_r(\chi)=(-g_1(\chi))^r\), makes the \(L\)-function a product of linear factors, \(L(\psi,s)=\prod_{j=1}^{p-2}\bigl(1+g_1(\omega^{-j})\,s\bigr)\), so the eigenvalues are the Gauss sums themselves, and Stickelberger gives their sizes: \(v_p\bigl(g_1(\omega^{-j})\bigr)=j/(p-1)\). Slopes \(j/(p-1)\), each once (the case \(k=1\) of lem:Dw; geometrically, my Gauss sums note).


My note "The Frobenius eigenvalues of Artin-Schreier-Witt curves are Gauss sums" (rin.io/pdfs/Gauss_sums.pdf) writes Frobenius at \(k=1\) as the Gauss-sum correspondence \(G=-\sum_b\chi(b)\psi(b)\), an element of the group ring of the automorphisms following Coleman's proof of Hasse-Davenport.

By Stickelberger, its eigenvalue on the \(j=1\) piece is a unit times \(\lambda=1-\zeta\), and \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) acts there as \(\zeta\); so \(F=(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\cdot\text{unit}\) on the formal group, the one identity which shines through each section.


So the correspondence may be a global, geometric form of the one identity at \(k=1\) (lem:F2 at every \(k\)).

4

For \(k\ge2\) the sums stop being Gauss sums.

The sums for \(k\ge2\) are built from the Witt trace. Let's review the Witt trace first, in case Witt vectors are new friends for you. Let \(q=p^r\), and let \(\mathbb Z_q\) be the ring of integers of the unramified extension of \(\mathbb Q_p\) of degree \(r\).

The Witt vectors of length \(k\) over \(\mathbb F_q\) are \(\mathbb Z_q\) cut off after \(k\) \(p\)-adic digits: \(W_k(\mathbb F_q)=\mathbb Z_q/p^k\), and in particular \(W_k(\mathbb F_p)=\mathbb Z/p^k\).

Frobenius acts on \(\mathbb Z_q\), and the Witt trace is the field trace from \(\mathbb Z_q\) down to \(\mathbb Z_p\), reduced mod \(p^k\): \[\mathrm{Tr}_W(w)=w+\mathrm{Frob}(w)+\dots+\mathrm{Frob}^{r-1}(w)\in\mathbb Z/p^k.\] The Teichmüller lift \([u]\) of \(u\in\mathbb F_q\) is the root of unity in \(\mathbb Z_q\) that reduces to \(u\), with \([0]=0\). The Teichmüller lift is multiplicative: \([uv]=[u][v]\). In other words, \(\mathrm{Tr}_W([u])\) is the trace of \(u\) remembered to \(k\) digits. The first digit of \(\mathrm{Tr}_W([u])\) is the ordinary trace \(\mathrm{Tr}(u)\in\mathbb F_p\), and the higher digits carry more.

Let's see the carry once, in \(\mathbb Z_5\) mod \(25\). We have \([1]=1\), and \([2]\) is the fourth root of unity that is \(2\) mod \(5\), namely \(7\) (since \(7^2=49\equiv-1\)). So \([1]+[1]=2\) but \([1+1]=[2]\equiv7\pmod{25}\): equal mod \(5\), different mod \(25\). In other words, the Teichmüller lift is not additive past the first digit. The difference is the carry, which is \(C(z,w)=\bigl(z^p+w^p-(z+w)^p\bigr)/p\) in my Gauss sums note.


Why does the Witt trace appear?

The reason is Artin-Schreier-Witt theory. At \(k=1\), Frobenius acts on the solutions of \(y^p-y=a\) by adding \(\mathrm{Tr}(a)\in\mathbb Z/p\), which is why the point count above asks for \(\mathrm{Tr}(a)=0\).

For general \(k\), let \(\wp(y)=\mathrm{Frob}(y)-y\) on Witt vectors of length \(k\). Frobenius acts on the solutions of \(\wp(y)=(a,0,\dots,0)=[a]\) by adding \(\mathrm{Tr}_W([a])\in\mathbb Z/p^k\). So \(\mathrm{Tr}_W([z^{p-1}])\) is the Galois element that Frobenius picks at the point over \(z\), and a character \(\psi\) of \(\mathbb Z/p^k\) reads that Galois element: \[S_r(\psi)=\sum_z\psi\bigl(\mathrm{Tr}_W[z^{p-1}]\bigr).\]

Here is where the Gauss sum argument gets gunked up beyond k=1: a Gauss sum needs an additive character of \(\mathbb F_q\). For \(\psi\) of order \(p\), the map \(u\mapsto\psi(\mathrm{Tr}_W[u])\) reads only the first digit, the ordinary trace, which is additive in \(u\). So the Gauss sum argument works. For \(\psi\) of order \(p^m\), \(m\ge2\), the map \(u\mapsto\psi(\mathrm{Tr}_W[u])\) reads the carries too, so the map is not additive, and there is no Gauss sum to form. \(L(\psi,s)\) is still a polynomial (of degree \(\class{gk-h}{h}-1\) for faithful \(\psi\)) whose reciprocal roots are the eigenvalues, but there is no formula for these eigenvalues.

(My note tries the \(\mathbb Z/p^2\) case anyway: its Theorem 4.3 is conditional on its Conjecture 4.4. So lem:Dw uses the \(T\)-adic sums of Liu and Wan instead.)

5

The \(T\) trick: keep the group element, choose the character last.

Take the whole tower \(\mathbb Z/p,\mathbb Z/p^2,\dots\) together, which is the group \(\mathbb Z_p\), and record the Galois element that Frobenius picks at \(z\) as the \(p\)-adic integer \(a_z=\mathrm{Tr}\bigl(\hat z^{\,p-1}\bigr)\in\mathbb Z_p\), where \(\hat z\) is the Teichmüller lift of \(z\).

Write \(a_z\) in the group ring of \(\mathbb Z_p\), which is \(\mathbb Z_p[[T]]\) (the Iwasawa algebra), where the group element \(a\) becomes \((1+T)^a\): \[S_r(T)=\sum_{z}(1+T)^{a_z}\in\mathbb Z_p[[T]].\] Choosing a character is plugging in a value of \(T\). At \(T=\zeta_{p^m}-1\) we get \((1+T)^a=\zeta_{p^m}^{\,a}\), and \(S_r(T)\) becomes the sum for a character of order \(p^m\). In other words, one power series holds every \(k\).


So we never compute the sums for general \(k\). We study the one \(T\)-adic power series \(S_r(T)\). The Newton polygon of the \(T\)-adic object has slopes \(0,1,2,\dots\) in units of \(T\).

The case \(k=1\), where the Gauss sum argument works, forces these slopes, together with the theorem of Liu and Wan [LWan, Theorem 2.9]. Each \(k\) is then a change of units: \(T=\zeta_{p^k}-1\) has size \(v_p(T)=1/\class{gk-h}{h}\), so slope \(j\) in \(T\) becomes slope \(j/\class{gk-h}{h}\) in \(p\). The case \(k=1\) controls the tower.

Step 3: non-faithful \(\psi\). A character of order \(p^j\), \(1\le j<k\), factors through the Galois group of \(X_j\to\mathbb{P}^1\), and its \(L\)-function is the one attached to \(X_j\). Apply the argument for faithful \(\psi\) with \(j\) in place of \(k\). This proves (ii).

Step 4: cohomology. The curve \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) and the automorphism \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) are defined over \(\mathbb{F}_p\), so the \(p\)-Frobenius \(F\) acts \(\mathbb{Q}_p\)-linearly on \(H=H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}/\mathbb{Z}_p)\otimes\mathbb{Q}_p\) and commutes with \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\). Over \(\mathbb{Q}_p(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}})\) decompose \(H=\bigoplus_\psi H_\psi\) into eigenspaces of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\). The cover \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\to\mathbb{P}^1\) is totally ramified over \(\infty\), so the Lefschetz trace formula in crystalline cohomology [KM], applied to \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^i F^r\) for all \(i\) and \(r\), gives \[\det\bigl(1-FT\mid H_\psi\bigr)=L(\psi,T)\quad(\psi\ne1),\qquad H_{1}=H^1(\mathbb{P}^1)=0 .\] By (i) each faithful \(\psi\) contributes a line of slope \(1/h\), and by (ii) no other \(\psi\) contributes. There are \(h\) faithful characters, and they form one orbit under \(\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_p(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}})/\mathbb{Q}_p)\). Hence \(N\) has dimension \(h\) and \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) acts on \(N\otimes\mathbb{Q}_p(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}})\) with each primitive \(p^k\)-th root of unity as an eigenvalue exactly once. This proves (iii).

Lemma: F2 · (F2) for general \(k\) · Proved in the prequel \(k=1\) ♡ \(\text{every }k\)

Notation for this lemma

Let \(W(\bar{\mathbb F}_p)\) be the Witt ring, and let \(\mathcal L=H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}/W(\bar{\mathbb F}_p))_{\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}}\) be the part of the crystalline cohomology of \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) on which the center acts by \(\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}\), with its Frobenius \(F\) and Verschiebung \(V\). Let \(\mathcal O=W(\bar{\mathbb F}_p)[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\) and \(\pi=\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}-1\), and let \(\mathrm{Frob}\) be the Frobenius of \(W(\bar{\mathbb F}_p)\), extended to \(\mathcal O\) by \(\mathrm{Frob}(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}})=\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}\).

The block is \(N=N_{\mathbb Q}\cap\mathcal L\), where \(N_{\mathbb Q}\) is the part of slope \(1/h\) of \(\mathcal L\otimes\mathbb Q\).

Modulo \(p\): \(M=\mathcal L/p\) is the \(\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}\) part of \(H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k})\), and \(\mathrm{Fil}\subset M\) is its Hodge filtration \(H^0(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\Omega^1)_{\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}}\), which is the kernel of \(F\) on \(M\). We write \(\overline N\) for the image of \(N/pN\) in \(M\), and \(\overline N_0\) for the image of \(\overline N\) in \(M/\mathrm{Fil}=H^1(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\mathcal O_{\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}})_{\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}}\). Let \(\Lambda=\kappa[\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1]/((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^h)=\mathcal O/p\). Let \(M_{\mathrm{new}}\subset M\) be the reduction modulo \(p\) of the kernel of \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma})\) on \(\mathcal L\).

The element \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau} \) satisfies \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau} \class{gk-sigma}{\sigma} \class{gk-tau}{\tau} ^{-1}=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^{[\epsilon^{-1}]}\) for some \(\epsilon\in\mathbb F_p^\times\), where \([\,\cdot\,]\) is the Teichmüller lift to \(\mathbb Z/p^k\), and \(\omega\) is the Teichmüller character. Put \[\tilde{\varpi}=\frac1{p-1}\sum_{j\in\mathbb F_p^\times}\omega(j)^{-1}\bigl(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}^{[j]}-1\bigr)\in\mathbb Z_p[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}],\qquad \varpi=-\sum_{j\in\mathbb F_p^\times}j^{-1}\bigl(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^{[j]}-1\bigr)\in\Lambda ,\] so that \(\varpi\) is the reduction of \(\tilde{\varpi}\). Then \(\tilde{\varpi}\in\pi(1+\pi\mathbb Z_p[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}])\) is a uniformizer of \(\mathcal O\), \(\Lambda=\bar{\mathbb F}_p[\varpi]/(\varpi^h)\), \(\varpi^{h-1}=(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{h-1}\), and \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau} \varpi \class{gk-tau}{\tau} ^{-1}=\epsilon^{-1}\varpi\) (this is lem:varpi: \(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}^{[j]}-1\equiv[j]\pi\) modulo \(\pi^2\) and \(\frac1{p-1}\sum_j\omega(j)^{-1}[j]\equiv1\) modulo \(p\); conjugation by \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau} \) sends \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^{[j]}\) to \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^{[\epsilon^{-1}j]}\), and we reindex the sum).

)

Lemma ((F2) for general \(k\). PROVED GRANTING Lemma lem:Dw (Dwork slopes).

(1) \(N\) is a free \(\mathcal O\)-module of rank one, stable under \(F\), \(V\) and \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\).

(2) There is a generator \(e\) of \(N\) with \[Fe=\tilde{\varpi}\,e,\qquad Ve=\frac{p}{\tilde{\varpi}}\,e,\qquad \class{gk-tau}{\tau} e=\tilde{\lambda}\,e,\quad \tilde{\lambda}\in\mu_{(p-1)^2}(W(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_p)).\]

(3) \(N/pN\to M\) is injective. Its image \(\overline{N}=\Lambda\bar{e}\) is a free \(\Lambda\)-module of rank one contained in \(M_{\mathrm{new}}\), and \[F\bar{e}=\varpi\,\bar{e},\qquad V\bar{e}=-\varpi^{h-1}\bar{e},\qquad \class{gk-tau}{\tau}\bar{e}=\lambda\bar{e} .\] In particular \(F\bar{e}=(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1) u_0\bar{e}\) with \(u_0\in\Lambda^\times\), \(u_0(0)=1\), which is (F2).

(4) \(\overline{N}\cap\mathrm{Fil}=(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{h-1}\overline{N}\), so \(\dim\overline{N}_0=h-1\); and \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^j\overline{N}\subset F^jM\) for all \(j\ge 0\).

The proof in steps

1
(1): the slope \(1/h\) part has rank \(h\) and \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) satisfies \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}\) on it (lem:Dw (Dwork slopes)), so it is a line over the fraction field of \(\mathcal O\), and \(N\) is free of rank one.
2
(2): the single slope forces \(Fe_1=\pi w e_1\) with \(w\) a unit, and since \(c\mapsto\mathrm{Frob}(c)/c\) is onto \(\mathcal O^\times\), a rescaling gives \(Fe_1=\tilde{\varpi} e_1\).
3
A second rescaling, by an element of \(U^1\), makes \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) act by a root of unity and keeps \(Fe=\tilde{\varpi} e\); then \(Ve=(p/\tilde{\varpi})e\).
4
(3): \(N/pN\) injects into \(M\) as \(\Lambda\bar{e}\), killed by \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^h\), and \(p/\tilde{\varpi}\equiv-\pi^{h-1}\) gives \(V\bar{e}=-\varpi^{h-1}\bar{e}\).
5
(4): \(F(a\bar{e})\) vanishes exactly when \(a\in(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{h-1}\Lambda\), and \(\varpi^j\bar{e}=F^j\bar{e}\).
Proof.

(1) The projector onto the \(\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}\) part has coefficients in \(\mathbb{Z}_p\) (the values of \(\class{gk-theta}{\theta}\) lie in \(\mu_{p-1}(\mathbb{Z}_p)\)), so it commutes with \(F\), \(V\) and \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\), and \(\mathcal L\) is a direct summand of \(H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}/W(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_p))\) stable under all three. Let \(N_\mathbb{Q}\) be the part of slope \(1/h\). It is canonical, hence stable under \(F\), \(V\) and \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\). By Lemma lem:Dw (Dwork slopes) it has rank \(h\) and \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) has characteristic polynomial \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}\) on it. \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}(1+t)\) is an Eisenstein polynomial over \(W(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_p)\), so \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}\) is irreducible there, \(W(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_p)[x]/\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}=\mathcal O\) is a discrete valuation ring with uniformizer \(\pi\) and residue field \(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_p\), and \(N_\mathbb{Q}\) is a module over its fraction field of dimension one. The element \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}^{p-1}\) is central and acts \(\mathcal O\)-linearly on \(N_\mathbb{Q}\), so by a scalar; hence \(N_\mathbb{Q}\) lies in one isotypic part, which is the \(\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}\) part by Theorem thm:norm(d) (\(u\)-cancellation). \(N=N_\mathbb{Q}\cap\mathcal L\) is finitely generated and torsion free over \(\mathcal O\), so free, of rank one.

(2) \(F\) is \(\mathrm{Frob}\)-semilinear over \(W(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_p)\) and commutes with \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\), so \(F(ax)=\mathrm{Frob}(a)F(x)\) for \(a\in\mathcal O\). Write \(Fe_1=a e_1\) for a generator \(e_1\), and write \(v_\pi(a)\) for the valuation of \(a\), so that \(a=\pi^{v_\pi(a)} w\) with \(w\in\mathcal O^\times\). Then \(F^h(N)=\pi^{h\,v_\pi(a)}N=p^{v_\pi(a)}N\), so \(F^h\) is \(p^{v_\pi(a)}\) times a bijective semilinear map of \(N\) and \(N_\mathbb{Q}\) has the single slope \(v_\pi(a)/h\). Hence \(v_\pi(a)=1\). The map \(c\mapsto\mathrm{Frob}(c)/c\) is onto \(\mathcal O^\times\): on \(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_p^\times\) it is \(c\mapsto c^{p-1}\), on each graded piece \((1+\pi^i\mathcal O)/(1+\pi^{i+1}\mathcal O)=\bar{\mathbb{F}}_p\) it is \(x\mapsto x^p-x\), and \(\mathcal O\) is complete. So after rescaling \(e_1\) we have \(Fe_1=\tilde{\varpi} e_1\). Now \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) is semilinear for the automorphism \(\gamma\) of \(\mathcal O\) with \(\gamma(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}})=\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}^{[\epsilon^{-1}]}\), and \(\gamma(\tilde{\varpi})=\omega(\epsilon^{-1})\tilde{\varpi}\) by Lemma lem:varpi (A uniformizer that \(\tau\) scales). Write \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau} e_1=ae_1\). From \(F\class{gk-tau}{\tau}=\class{gk-tau}{\tau} F\) we get \(\mathrm{Frob}(a)=\omega(\epsilon^{-1})a\). Choose \(\beta\in\mu_{(p-1)^2}(W(\bar{\mathbb{F}}_p))\) with \(\beta^{p-1}=\omega(\epsilon^{-1})\); then \(a_0=a/\beta\) is fixed by \(\mathrm{Frob}\), so \(a_0\in\mathbb{Z}_p[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]^\times=\mu_{p-1}\times U^1\) with \(U^1=1+\pi\mathbb{Z}_p[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\). Write \(a_0=\xi a_1\). Since \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}^{p-1}\) acts on \(\mathcal L\) by an element of \(\mu_{p-1}\), the norm of \(a_1\) for the group \(\Gamma=\langle\gamma\rangle\) of order \(p-1\) lies in \(\mu_{p-1}\cap U^1=1\). \(U^1\) is a pro-\(p\) group and \(|\Gamma|\) is prime to \(p\), so \(a_1^{-1}=\gamma(c)/c\) for some \(c\in U^1\). Put \(e=ce_1\). Then \(Fe=\tilde{\varpi} e\) (as \(\mathrm{Frob}(c)=c\)) and \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau} e=\xi\beta e\). Finally \(Ve=be\) with \(F(be)=\mathrm{Frob}(b)\tilde{\varpi} e=pe\), so \(b=p/\tilde{\varpi}\).

(3) \(N\) is saturated in \(\mathcal L\) and \(\mathcal L/p=M\), so \(N/pN\) injects, and \(N/pN=(\mathcal O/p)\bar{e}\) with \(\mathcal O/p=\bar{\mathbb{F}}_p[x]/(x-1)^h=\Lambda\). \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma})\) kills \(N\) and \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}(1+t)\equiv t^h\) modulo \(p\), so \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^h\bar{e}=0\) and \(\overline{N}\subset M_{\mathrm{new}}\). Write \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}(1+t)=t^h+p\,g(t)\) with \(g(0)=1\); then \(p/\pi=-\pi^{h-1}/g(\pi)\), and \(p/\tilde{\varpi}\equiv-\pi^{h-1}\) modulo \(\pi^h\), which reduces to \(-(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{h-1}=-\varpi^{h-1}\).

(4) \(F(a\bar{e})=a^{\mathrm{Frob}}\varpi\bar{e}\) vanishes iff \(a\in(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)^{h-1}\Lambda\). And \(\varpi^j\bar{e}=F^j\bar{e}\).

NOTE (Rin): in (4), the proof shows that F(aē) = 0 exactly when a ∈ (σ-1)^{h-1}Λ. Reading this as N̄ ∩ Fil = (σ-1)^{h-1}N̄ uses that Fil is the kernel of F on M; the proof does not say so.

Prop: rho · \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) acts faithfully on the formal group, as the standard \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) up to conjugacy · Proved

Proposition (the curve sees the right group). The map \(\iota\colon \class{gk-Gp}{G'}\to\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\cong \class{gk-OD}{\mathcal{O}_D}^\times\) is injective, and its image is conjugate in \(\class{gk-OD}{\mathcal{O}_D}^\times\) to \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\); so after conjugating the fixed isomorphism \(\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\cong \class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) by a unit of \(\class{gk-OD}{\mathcal{O}_D}\), \(\iota\) is the standard embedding composed with an automorphism of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\).

The proof in steps

1
We want two things from the map \(\iota\colon\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\to\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\cong \class{gk-OD}{\mathcal O_D}^\times\): that it is injective, and that its image is the standard \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\).
2
Each generator survives: \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) acts on \(\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\) with order exactly \(p^k\) (Dwork), and the center with order exactly \(p-1\).
3
Nothing mixed dies either: an element of the kernel commutes with \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\), so it is \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^a g\) with \(g\in\class{gk-Gc}{G}\), and orders force it to be trivial.
4
The image is a subgroup of \(\class{gk-OD}{\mathcal O_D}^\times\) of the same order as \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\); by the classification of finite subgroups (Hewett, Bujard) it is conjugate to \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\).
Proof.

We want to know two things about the map \(\iota\colon\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\to\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\cong \class{gk-OD}{\mathcal O_D}^\times\) that the curve hands us: that it loses nothing, meaning it is injective, and that its image is the same \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) that everyone else uses. Let's start by looking at what the generators of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) do to \(\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\).

The wild generator \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) first. By Dwork's theorem (lem:Dw (iii)), \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) acts on the block through \(\mathbb Z_p[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\), sending \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) to \(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}\); so on \(\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\) it has order exactly \(p^k\), and nothing in \(\langle\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\rangle\) is lost. The center \(\class{gk-Gc}{G}\) next: it acts on \(\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\) by a Teichmüller scalar of exact order \(p-1\) (thm:norm (d)), so nothing is lost there either.

Could something mixed still die, a power of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) times an element of the center? Let \(K\) be the kernel of \(\iota\), a normal subgroup of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\). Since \(K\) meets \(\langle\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\rangle\) trivially and \(\langle\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\rangle\) is normal in \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) (it is the Sylow \(p\)-subgroup), the commutator \([K,\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}]\) lies in \(K\cap\langle\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\rangle\), which is trivial, so everything in \(K\) commutes with \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\). The centralizer of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) is isomorphic to \(\langle\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\rangle\times\class{gk-Gc}{G}\), because the conjugation character has order \(p-1\). So an element of \(K\) looks like \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^a g\) with \(g\in\class{gk-Gc}{G}\), and then \(\iota(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma})^a=\iota(g)^{-1}\). The left side has \(p\)-power order and the right side has order prime to \(p\), so both are \(1\): \(p^k\) divides \(a\) and \(g=1\). Hence \(K\) is trivial, and \(\iota\) is injective.

It remains to see that the image is the standard \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) and not some other copy. The image \(\iota(\class{gk-Gp}{G'})\) is a finite subgroup of \(\class{gk-OD}{\mathcal O_D}^\times\) of order \(p^k(p-1)^2\) containing an element of order \(p^k\). Finite subgroups of \(\class{gk-OD}{\mathcal O_D}^\times\) are classified (Hewett, Bujard): every such subgroup lies in a maximal one, and the maximal ones are all conjugate to \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\). Since the orders agree, \(\iota(\class{gk-Gp}{G'})\) is conjugate to \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\).

Dependency tree of the proof

Examples: the numbers at small \((p,k)\)
