\(p^{k-1}(p-1)\)\(p^k(p-1)^2\)\(2g-2=(p-1)^2\frac{p^{2k}-1}{p^2-1}-p^k-1\)\(p^k+1\)\(3g_Y-3+p^k+1\), \(g_Y=\frac{p(p^{k-1}-1)(p^k-1)}{2(p+1)}\)computed\(\dim M_k-\dim\Omega_1+(h-1)\)\(\dim M_k-\dim\Omega_1\)

After inverting \(p\), the functor \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) followed by the Gross-Hopkins period map is the block period map \(\class{gk-Pper}{P}\): this is the aside the Gross-Hopkins period map.

AMinimal Family of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-curves, used Above

These proof boxes lived in sections that are folded away in this version; the text above links to them.

Thm: curve · the minimal \(G^{\prime}\)-curve: its breaks, its genus, and its family · Proved

Theorem (tiny, and forced up to lower order). Let \(X\) be a curve with a faithful \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-action on which \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) has a fixed point, of minimal genus among such curves (at \((p,k)=(3,2)\) the fixed-point hypothesis is automatic, Lemma lem:nofree (no free model at \(p=3\))).

(i) (Breaks.) \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) has a unique fixed point \(Q_\infty\), fixed by all of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\); the \(C_{p^k}\)-tower has upper breaks \(u_j=p^{\,j-1}(p-1)\) there, equivalently lower breaks \(w_j=(p-1)m_j\) with \(m_j=1+p(p^{2j-2}-1)/(p+1)\); the tame part acts on the cotangent line of \(X/\langle\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\rangle\) at \(Q_\infty\) through the extremal character; and \[Y=X/G,\] \[g_{Y}=\frac{p(p^{k-1}-1)(p^k-1)}{2(p+1)},\] \[2g_{X}-2=(p-1)(2g_{Y}-2)+(p^k+1)(p-2).\]

(ii) (The family.) \(Y\to Y/C_{p^k}=\mathbb{P}^1_s\) is the Artin-Schreier-Witt tower \(\wp(y)=(s,f_1,\dots,f_{k-1})\) with \(f_j\in \kappa[s]\) of \(\bar{\class{gk-tau}{\tau}}\)-weight \(\class{gk-theta}{\theta}\) and degree \(<p^j\), and \(X\) is its cover \(z^{p-1}=s\). Every such datum has the breaks of (i). The data form an affine space containing \((s,0,\dots,0)\), so the minimal \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-curves are a connected family of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-equivariant deformations of \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\), the member with datum \((s,0,\dots,0)\).

(iii) (\(k=2\).) The datum is \((s,cs)\), \(c\in \kappa\), and two of them give isomorphic \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-curves, with the group identified up to \(\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Gp}{G'})\), if and only if \(c-c'\in\mathbb{F}_p\); as \(C_{p^2}\)-torsors over \(\mathbb{P}^1_s\) with \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) and \(s\) fixed they are pairwise distinct. The minimal \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-curves at \(k=2\) are a one-parameter family, a torsor under the \(\mathbb{Z}/p\)-covers of \(Y_1\) with conductor \(\le1\) at \(Q_\infty\); \(X_2\) is the point \(c\in\mathbb{F}_p\).

(iv) In every member \(\class{gk-B0}{B_0}\) is a free \(C_{p^k}\)-torsor and \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) fixes exactly one point of it.

The proof in steps

1
Minimal genus means the smallest breaks the group allows; the breaks fix the Weierstrass semigroup but not the curve, and the lower terms are moduli.
2
Riemann-Hurwitz for \(X\to X/\langle\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\rangle\) writes the genus through the different exponents \(d_Q\), which increase with the breaks, and \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) has \(2g_{\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}}-2=-2p^k+d_{p-1}\).
3
By lem:weightbreak (the tame part divides the first break) every \(\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle\)-orbit of fixed points contributes at least \(d_{p-1}\), with equality only for one \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-fixed point with breaks \(p^{j-1}(p-1)\), so minimal genus forces exactly that.
4
At \(Q_\infty\) the break relation (eq:breakchar) shows that the tame part acts through the extremal character, unique up to \(\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Gp}{G'})\).
5
(ii): the quotient \(Y\to\mathbb P^1_s\) is an Artin-Schreier-Witt tower with components of weight \(\class{gk-theta}{\theta}\), and the reduced data with these degree bounds are exactly the ones with these breaks.
6
(iii): at \(k=2\) the data are \((s,cs)\); comparing torsors, \(\bar{G}\)-curves and \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-curves shows two members agree up to \(\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Gp}{G'})\) exactly when \(c-c'\in\mathbb F_p\).
7
(iv): \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^{p^{k-1}}\) and the quotient translate the Witt coordinates freely on the fiber \(s=0\), and \(\bar{\class{gk-tau}{\tau}}\) fixes only \(y=0\).
Proof.

The genus is a monotone function of the ramification breaks, so minimal genus means the smallest breaks the group allows, and the breaks determine the Weierstrass semigroup at \(Q_\infty\). What they do not determine is the curve: the semigroup sees the leading term of each equation and is blind to the terms below it, and those terms are moduli.

(i) Write \(Z=X/\langle\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\rangle\) and \(F=\mathrm{Fix}(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma})\ne\varnothing\), a set permuted by \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) since \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) normalizes \(\langle\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\rangle\). Riemann-Hurwitz for \(X\to Z\) reads \[2g_X-2\;=\;p^k(2g_Z-2)+\sum_{Q}d_Q ,\] the sum over the points with nontrivial stabilizer in \(\langle\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\rangle\), \(d_Q\ge0\) the different exponent, and \(d_Q=d(\ell_1(Q),\dots,\ell_k(Q))\) for \(Q\in F\) by Lemma lem:wildRH (different and genus at one wild point), a function strictly increasing in each lower break, hence in each upper break by Herbrand. For breaks \(u_j=p^{\,j-1}w\) the lower breaks are \(\ell_j=wm_j\) (Herbrand's function is homogeneous), and Lemma lem:wildRH (different and genus at one wild point) gives \[d_w:=d(wm_1,\dots,wm_k)=w\,d_1-(p^k-1)(w-1),\] since only the term \((p^k-1)(\ell_1-\ell_0)\) with \(\ell_0=-1\) fails to scale. The curve \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) has \(Z=\mathbb{P}^1_z\), one point of \(F\), breaks \(u_j=p^{\,j-1}(p-1)\) there, and no other ramification of \(\langle\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\rangle\) (its tower is étale over \(\mathbb{A}^1_z\)), so \(2g_{\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}}-2=-2p^k+d_{p-1}\).

Now let \(X\) be any curve in the class and \(Q\in F\) with \(\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle\)-orbit of size \(a\), \(g=\gcd(a,p-1)\). By Lemma lem:weightbreak (the tame part divides the first break) and monotonicity, \(d_Q\ge d_{w_a}\) with \(w_a=(p-1)g/a\), and all \(a\) points of the orbit have the same breaks, so the orbit contributes at least \(a\,d_{w_a}=(p-1)g\,d_1-(p^k-1)((p-1)g-a)\). Comparing with \(d_{p-1}=(p-1)d_1-(p^k-1)(p-2)\), \[a\,d_{w_a}-d_{p-1}\;=\;(p-1)(g-1)\big(d_1-(p^k-1)\big)+(p^k-1)(a-1)\;\ge\;0,\] using \(d_1\ge(p^k-1)(\ell_1+1)\ge2(p^k-1)\), with equality iff \(a=1\) (then \(g=1\)); and \(g=1\) forces \(a=1\), because every prime dividing \(a\mid(p-1)^2\) divides \(p-1\). So every \(\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle\)-orbit in \(F\) contributes at least \(d_{p-1}\) to the sum, with equality only for a single \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-fixed point with breaks exactly \(p^{\,j-1}(p-1)\). If \(g_Z\ge1\) then \(2g_X-2\ge d_{p-1}>-2p^k+d_{p-1}\). If \(g_Z=0\) then \(2g_X-2\ge-2p^k+(\#\text{orbits in }F)\,d_{p-1}\ge-2p^k+d_{p-1}\), with equality iff \(F\) is one \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-fixed point \(Q_\infty\) with those breaks and \(\langle\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\rangle\) has no other ramification. So minimal genus forces exactly that, and \(g_X\) is the genus of \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\). At \(Q_\infty\), (eq:breakchar) with \(\gamma=\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) and \(u_1=p-1\) says \(\xi^{-(p-1)}=\chi(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})\) with \(\xi\) of order \((p-1)^2\): the tame part acts on the cotangent line of \(Z\) at \(\bar{Q}_\infty\) through a character whose \((p-1)\)-th power is \(\chi^{-1}\), the extremal character. On \(Y=X/G\), with \(s=z^{p-1}\), the extremal character is unique, \(s\mapsto\class{gk-theta}{\theta} s\); on \(X\) its lifts to the \(G\)-cover \(z^{p-1}=s\) are \(z\mapsto\zeta^{b}z\) with \(b\equiv1\pmod{p-1}\), and these are permuted simply transitively by the automorphisms \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\mapsto\class{gk-tau}{\tau}^{b}\), \(b\equiv1\pmod{p-1}\), of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\), which fix \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) and \(\chi\). So there is one extremal character up to \(\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Gp}{G'})\). The genus formulas are tame Riemann-Hurwitz for \(X\to Y\) (totally ramified at the \(p^k+1\) points of \(B\)) and Lemma lem:wildRH (different and genus at one wild point) for \(Y\to\mathbb{P}^1\) with the breaks \(u_j=p^{\,j-1}\) of Lemma lem:jumps (the minimal jumps).

(ii) \(Y\to\mathbb{P}^1_s\) is a \(C_{p^k}\)-cover of \(\mathbb{P}^1\) branched at one point, hence an Artin-Schreier-Witt tower \(\wp(y)=f\) with \(f\in W_k(\kappa[s])\); \(\bar{\class{gk-tau}{\tau}}\) normalizes \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) through \(\chi\), so it acts on \(y\) by the Teichmüller scalar \([\class{gk-theta}{\theta}]\) up to a translation by \(C_{p^k}\), and \(f(\class{gk-theta}{\theta} s)=[\class{gk-theta}{\theta}]f(s)\): every \(f_j\) has weight \(\class{gk-theta}{\theta}\). Take \(f\) reduced (Lemma lem:jumps (the minimal jumps)); the breaks of (i) on \(Y\), \(u_j=p^{\,j-1}\), say \(\deg f_0=1\) and \(\deg f_j<p^j\) for \(j\ge1\), and conversely by Lemma lem:jumps (the minimal jumps) every reduced datum with these degree bounds has these breaks. Scaling \(s\) makes \(f_0=s\). The semigroup of Lemma lem:reduced(d) (reduced coordinates) is the same for every member; the \(f_j\) with \(j\ge1\) are invisible to it.

(iii) At \(k=2\) a polynomial of weight \(\class{gk-theta}{\theta}\) and degree \(<p\) is \(cs\), so the data are \((s,cs)\), \(c\in \kappa\). We compare three notions of isomorphism. As \(C_{p^2}\)-torsors over \(\mathbb{P}^1_s\), with \(s\) and \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) fixed: \((s,cs)\) and \((s,c's)\) give the same torsor iff their difference \((0,(c-c')s)\) is \(\wp(d,g)\) for some \((d,g)\in W_2(\kappa(s))\); the first coordinate forces \(d\in\mathbb{F}_p\), and then \(\wp(d,g)=(0,g^p-g)\) (the Witt carry in the second coordinate of \((d^p,g^p)-(d,g)\) vanishes because \(d^p=d\)), so one needs \(g^p-g=(c-c')s\) in \(\kappa(s)\), impossible for \(c\ne c'\) since the right side has a simple pole at \(\infty\). The torsors are pairwise distinct. As \(\bar{G}\)-curves, with the group identified up to \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\mapsto\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^u\) and \(s\) up to scalars: an isomorphism \(\varphi\colon Y_c\to Y_{c'}\) with \(\varphi\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}= \class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^u\varphi\), \(u\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^2)^\times\), and \(\varphi\bar{\class{gk-tau}{\tau}}=\bar{\class{gk-tau}{\tau}}\varphi\) descends to an automorphism of \(\mathbb{P}^1_s\) fixing the wild point and commuting with \(s\mapsto\class{gk-theta}{\theta} s\), hence \(s\mapsto\lambda s\). On \(Y_{c'}\) the generator \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^u\) has datum \(u^{-1}\cdot(s,c's)\), and \(u^{-1}=(u_0,u_1)\in W_2(\mathbb{F}_p)\) acts by \(u^{-1}\cdot(s,c's)=(u_0s,\,u_0c's+u_1s^p)\sim(u_0s,\,(u_0c'+u_1)s)\); so \(\varphi\) exists iff \((\lambda s,c\lambda s)\) and \((u_0s,(u_0c'+u_1)s)\) are the same torsor, iff \(\lambda=u_0\) and \(c\lambda=u_0c'+u_1\), iff \(c-c'=u_1/u_0\in\mathbb{F}_p\). Conversely for \(c-c'\in\mathbb{F}_p\) the map \((y_0,y_1)\mapsto(y_0,y_1-(c-c')y_0)\) is such an isomorphism, carrying \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) to \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^{1-p(c-c')}\). As \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-curves, with the group identified up to \(\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Gp}{G'})\): every automorphism of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) has the form \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\mapsto\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^u\), \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\mapsto\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^b\class{gk-tau}{\tau}^v\) with \(v\equiv1\pmod{p-1}\) (so that \(\chi\) is preserved), and \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^b\class{gk-tau}{\tau}^v\) is conjugate to \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}^v\) in \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) since \(1-\chi(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})\) is a unit mod \(p\); inner automorphisms do not change the isomorphism class of a \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-curve, and \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\mapsto\class{gk-tau}{\tau}^v\) with \(v\equiv1\pmod{p-1}\) acts trivially on \(Y\) and permutes the lifts of \(\bar{\class{gk-tau}{\tau}}\) to the Kummer cover \(z^{p-1}=s\), as in (i). So the classification of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-curves up to \(\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Gp}{G'})\) is that of \(\bar{G}\)-curves up to \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\mapsto\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^u\): iff \(c-c'\in\mathbb{F}_p\). Read over \(Y_1\), the members are the Artin-Schreier extensions \(\tilde{y}_1^{\,p}-\tilde{y}_1=R(y_0)+ay_0\), \(a=c^{1/p}-c\), so they are a torsor under the classes \(ay_0\), \(a\in \kappa\), the \(\mathbb{Z}/p\)-covers of \(Y_1\) with conductor \(\le1\) at \(Q_\infty\).

(iv) The torsor is free because \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^{p^{k-1}}\) translates the top Witt coordinate freely on the fiber \(s=0\) and the quotient translates the next; \(\bar{\class{gk-tau}{\tau}}\) fixes \(s=0\) and scales \(y\), so it fixes exactly the point \(y=0\) of the fiber.

NOTE (Rin): this proof is taken from only-the-gauge-grows-draft-s5.tex, not from new-theoremA.tex (the listed file): there the proof of parts (i) to (iii) is only commented replacement text. I compared the two: the s5 text of (i) to (iii) is the same text, word for word.

Asides

Side roads. Nothing in Main Theorems 1 and 2 depends on them.

After inverting \(p\): the Gross-Hopkins period map and the block period map

\(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) followed by the Gross-Hopkins period map

On the rigid generic fiber (curves over \(\mathcal O_C\), \(C\) a complete extension of \(W[1/p]\), after inverting \(p\)),

Doing the functor \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) (on the rigid generic fiber) and the Gross-Hopkins period map \(\class{gk-GH}{\mathrm{GH}}\) second gives the Hodge line of the block of the curve: \(\class{gk-GH}{\mathrm{GH}}\circ\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}=\class{gk-Pper}{P}\),

Where \(\class{gk-Pper}{P}\) sends a deformed curve to the Hodge line of the block of that curve. So \(\class{gk-Pper}{P}\) maps onto \(\mathbb P^{h-1}\).

Over Artinian rings only \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) is defined: \(\class{gk-GH}{\mathrm{GH}}\) needs crystals (divided powers on \((p)\)) and \(p\) inverted.

Definition (the block period map). Let \(L\) be a complete rank one valued extension field of \(K=W[1/p]\), and let \(x\) be a point of \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L})\), with curve \(X_x\) over \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L}\). Let \(N\subset H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}/W)\) be the Dieudonné module of the block \(\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\) of \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\). The crystalline comparison is the isomorphism \[c_x\colon\ H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}/W)\otimes_WL\ \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\ H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(X_x/\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L})\otimes L\] that identifies the cohomology of the special fiber \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) with the cohomology of the deformed curve \(X_x\); the set-up below constructs \(c_x\). The block period map \(\class{gk-Pper}{P}\) sends \(x\) to the subspace \[\class{gk-Pper}{P}(x)=c_x^{-1}\bigl(\mathrm{Fil}^1H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(X_x/\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L})\otimes L\bigr)\cap\bigl(N\otimes_WL\bigr)\quad\text{of }N\otimes_WL.\]

Proposition. The subspace \(\class{gk-Pper}{P}(x)\) is a line in \(N\otimes_WL\), and \[\class{gk-Pper}{P}(x)=\class{gk-GH}{\mathrm{GH}}\bigl(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}(x)\bigr)\quad\text{in }\ \mathbb{P}(N\otimes_WK)(L)=\mathbb{P}^{h-1}(L).\] So after inverting \(p\), on the rigid generic fiber, the block period map \(\class{gk-Pper}{P}\) is the functor \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) followed by the Gross-Hopkins period map \(\class{gk-GH}{\mathrm{GH}}\). The proof is the proof of Lemma GHcomposite below.

What is a period map? A period map takes a deformation and records where its Hodge filtration sits inside a vector space that does not move. \(\class{gk-Pper}{P}\) does this: the vector space is \(N\otimes_WL\), the block of the cohomology of \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\), and \(\class{gk-Pper}{P}\)\((x)\) is where the Hodge line of the deformed curve \(X_x\) sits inside the block. By the Proposition, \(\class{gk-Pper}{P}\) is the Gross-Hopkins period map of the formal group of the curve. We say block period map because \(\class{gk-Pper}{P}\) only sees the block: \(\class{gk-Pper}{P}\) is constant on the fibers of \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\), so \(\class{gk-Pper}{P}\) does not see the ghosts.

Set-up: the conventions, the cited inputs, and the construction of \(c_x\)

Conventions and inputs

Here \(\bar{\mathbb F}_p\) is the residue field, \(W=W(\bar{\mathbb F}_p)\) and \(K=W[1/p]\). Dieudonné crystals are contravariant, as in Berthelot, Breen and Messing [BBM82]. For a \(p\)-divisible group \(G\) over a base \(S\) on which \(p\) is locally nilpotent, \(\mathbb{D}(G)\) is a crystal of finite locally free modules on the crystalline site of \(S\) over \(\mathbb{Z}_p\); the crystal \(\mathbb{D}(G)\) is contravariant in \(G\), commutes with base change, is exact in \(G\), and carries the Hodge sequence \[0\to\omega_G\to\mathbb{D}(G)_S\to\mathrm{Lie}(G^\vee)\to0,\] functorial in \(G\). Over \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L}\) we write \(\mathbb{D}(G)_{\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L}}=\varprojlim_m\mathbb{D}(G\otimes\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L}/p^m)_{\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L}/p^m}\). We use the following standard inputs.

(G1a) For an abelian scheme \(J/S\) there is an isomorphism of crystals \(R^1f_{\mathrm{cris}*}\mathcal{O}_J\cong\mathbb{D}(J[p^\infty])\), compatible with base change, and carrying \(\mathrm{Fil}^1H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(J/S)\) to \(\omega_{J[p^\infty]}\) ([BBM82]).

(G1b) For a smooth proper curve \(X/S\) with a section and Jacobian \(J\), Abel-Jacobi gives an isomorphism \(H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}(X/S)\cong H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}(J/S)\) of crystals with Hodge filtrations (Mazur and Messing [MM74]). With (G1a): \(R^1f_{\mathrm{cris}*}\mathcal{O}_X\cong\mathbb{D}(J[p^\infty])\), carrying \(\mathrm{Fil}^1H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(X/S)\) to \(\omega_{J[p^\infty]}\).

(G3) The Gross-Hopkins period map [GH94] is given on points by the formula for \(\class{gk-GH}{\mathrm{GH}}(y)\) below (this is the description of Rapoport and Zink [RZ96, Ch. 5]).

(G4) \(\class{gk-GH}{\mathrm{GH}}\) is étale, and surjective on \(C\)-points for every complete algebraically closed extension \(C\) of \(K\) ([GH94] for \(C=\widehat{\overline K}\); Scholze and Weinstein [SW13] in general).

The rigidity trivialization

Let \(B=\bar{\mathbb F}_p\oplus J\) be an augmented \(\bar{\mathbb F}_p\)-algebra with \(J^{m_0}=0\), with \(\pi\colon B\to\bar{\mathbb F}_p\) and \(\iota\colon\bar{\mathbb F}_p\to B\). Choose \(n\) with \(p^n\ge m_0\). The \(n\)-th power of the absolute Frobenius of \(B\) kills \(J\), so \(\varphi_B^{\,n}=\iota\circ\mathrm{Frob}^n\circ\pi\), where \(\mathrm{Frob}\) is the Frobenius of \(\bar{\mathbb F}_p\). Hence for a \(p\)-divisible group \(G\) over \(B\), with \(G_0=\pi^*G\) and \(G_c=\iota^*G_0\) the constant group, \[G^{(p^n)}=\iota^*\bigl(G_0^{(p^n)}\bigr)=G_c^{(p^n)}.\] Define, in \(\mathrm{Hom}(G,G_c)\otimes\mathbb{Q}\), \[\rho_G=p^{-n}\,V^n_{G_c}\circ F^n_G\colon\quad G\xrightarrow{F^n_G}G^{(p^n)}=G_c^{(p^n)}\xrightarrow{V^n_{G_c}}G_c.\]

(i) \(\rho_G\) is a quasi-isogeny, with inverse \(p^{-n}V^n_G\circ F^n_{G_c}\), because \(F^nV^n=V^nF^n=p^n\).

(ii) \(\pi^*\rho_G=\mathrm{id}_{G_0}\).

(iii) \(\rho_G\) does not depend on \(n\): passing from \(n\) to \(n+1\) inserts \(V\circ F=p\) on \(G^{(p^n)}=G_c^{(p^n)}\).

(iv) \(\rho\) is natural: for \(f\colon G\to H\) over \(B\) we have \(\rho_H\circ f=(\iota^*f_0)\circ\rho_G\), since \(F\) and \(V\) are natural.

(v) \(\rho\) commutes with base change along maps of augmented \(\bar{\mathbb F}_p\)-algebras.

Each of the five properties follows from the one-line computation indicated.

Now let \(A'\) be a power series ring over \(W\) (we use \(A'=A\) and \(A'=R_{\mathrm{LT}}\)), let \(G\) be a \(p\)-divisible group over \(A'\) with special fiber \(G_0\) over \(\bar{\mathbb F}_p\), and let \(L\) be a complete rank one valued extension field of \(K\). A point \(x\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{cont}}(A',\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L})\) sends \(\mathfrak m_{A'}\) to topologically nilpotent elements, so for \(m_0\gg0\) the reduction of \(x\) factors as \(\bar x\colon B_{m_0}:=A'/(p,\mathfrak m_{A'}^{m_0})\to\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L}/p\), and \(B_{m_0}\) is augmented with nilpotent augmentation ideal. Write \(G_x=x^*G\). Evaluating the crystal on the thickening \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L}\to\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L}/p\) (divided powers on \((p)\)) and using base change gives \[\class{gk-tauGH}{\tau}_{G,x}:=\mathbb{D}(\bar x^*\rho_G)_{\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L}}\otimes\mathbb{Q}\colon\quad\mathbb{D}(G_0)_W\otimes_WL\ \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\ \mathbb{D}(G_x)_{\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L}}\otimes_{\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L}}L.\] By (iii) and (v) the map \(\class{gk-tauGH}{\tau}_{G,x}\) is independent of \(m_0\) and \(n\). When \(\bar x\) factors through \(\bar{\mathbb F}_p\) (for example when \(L=K\)), \(\rho=\mathrm{id}\) and \(\class{gk-tauGH}{\tau}_{G,x}\) is the integral base change isomorphism of the crystal.

The three maps

Let \(X_A\to\operatorname{Spec}A\) be the algebraization of the universal curve over \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}=\operatorname{Spf}A\), \(J_A\) its Jacobian, \(G=J_A[p^\infty]\), and \(q\colon G\to H_A\) the universal block quotient, an epimorphism of \(p\)-divisible groups over \(A\) with \(p\)-divisible kernel; on the special fiber, \(\mathbb{D}(q_0)\) is the inclusion \(N\subset H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}/W)\). The curve \(X_A\) has a section (by Hensel's lemma), as (G1b) requires. A point \(x\in\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L}):=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{cont}}(A,\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L})\) is the same as a compatible system of block-preserving deformations over the Artinian rings \(A/(p^m,\mathfrak m_A^{e_m})\), pulled back to \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L}/p^m\). Put \(X_x=x^*X_A\), \(H_x=x^*H_A\) and \(\mathrm{Fil}_x=\mathrm{Fil}^1H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(X_x/\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L})\). Via (G1a) and (G1b), the crystalline comparison is \[c_x:=\class{gk-tauGH}{\tau}_{G,x},\] and the Gross-Hopkins period map is \[\class{gk-GH}{\mathrm{GH}}(y):=\class{gk-tauGH}{\tau}_{H^{\mathrm{univ}},y}^{-1}\bigl(\omega_{H_y}\otimes L\bigr)\in\mathbb{P}(N\otimes_WK)(L),\qquad y\in\mathrm{Def}(H_h)(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L}).\] In the formula for \(\class{gk-GH}{\mathrm{GH}}(y)\), the line \(\omega_{H_y}\subset\mathbb{D}(H_y)\) corresponds, under \(\mathbb{D}(H)^\vee=\mathbb{D}(H^\vee)=\mathrm{Lie}\,E(H)\), to the hyperplane \(\ker(\mathrm{Lie}\,E(H_y)\to\mathrm{Lie}\,H_y)\) used in [GH94].

♡ · complete modulo named gaps (standard inputs (G1) to (G4): crystals of curves and Jacobians; the Gross-Hopkins map is étale and onto); no wrong step found · lem:GHcomposite
Supporting results

Lemma: GHcomposite · after inverting \(p\), Gross-Hopkins composed with \(\text{Б}\) is the period map \(P\) · ♡

Lemma. Let \(L\) be a complete rank one valued extension field of \(K\) and \(x\in\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L})\). Then:

(a) \(c_x(N\otimes_WL)=\mathbb{D}(H_x)_{\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L}}\otimes L\) inside \(H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(X_x/\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L})\otimes L\), and \(c_x|_{N\otimes L}=\class{gk-tauGH}{\tau}_{H^{\mathrm{univ}},\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}(x)}\).

(b) \(\mathrm{Fil}_x\cap\mathbb{D}(H_x)_{\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L}}=\omega_{H_x}\), a direct summand of rank one of \(\mathbb{D}(H_x)_{\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L}}\).

(c) \(P(x)\) is a line in \(N\otimes_WL\), and \[P(x)=\class{gk-GH}{\mathrm{GH}}\bigl(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}(x)\bigr)\quad\text{in }\ \mathbb{P}(N\otimes_WK)(L)=\mathbb{P}^{h-1}(L).\]

In particular the pointwise map \(P\) is the map on points of the rigid analytic morphism \(\class{gk-GH}{\mathrm{GH}}\circ\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}^{\mathrm{rig}}\colon(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}})^{\mathrm{rig}}\to\mathbb{P}^{h-1}_K\). The identity holds at every point of the open ball, not only formally at the base point.

The proof in steps

1
Step 1: naturality of the quasi-isogenies, pulled back along the point and sent through the Dieudonné functor, gives the square (eq:square), and base change gives (a).
2
Step 2: for an exact sequence \(0\to K\to G\to H\to0\) of \(p\)-divisible groups, the Hodge submodule of \(H\) is \(\omega_G\cap\mathbb D(H)_S\), by left exactness of \(\mathrm{Lie}\) on the dual sequence.
3
Passing to the limit over \(\mathcal O_L/p^m\) and using (G1a), (G1b) gives (b).
4
Step 3: (a) and (b), tensored with \(L\), give \(P(x)=\class{gk-GH}{\mathrm{GH}}(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}(x))\) at every point of the open ball, after inverting \(p\) (on the rigid generic fiber).
Proof.

Step 1 [PROVED GRANTING (G1a), (G1b)]. Fix \(m_0\) and \(n\) as above and work over \(B_{m_0}=A/(p,\mathfrak m_A^{m_0})\). By naturality (iv) applied to \(q\colon G\to H_A\), \[\rho_{H_A}\circ q=(\iota^*q_0)\circ\rho_{G}\] as quasi-isogenies over \(B_{m_0}\). Pull back along \(\bar{x}\) and apply the contravariant functor \(\mathbb{D}(\ )_{\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L}}\otimes\mathbb{Q}\): \[\class{gk-tauGH}{\tau}_{G,x}\circ\bigl(\mathbb{D}(q_0)\otimes L\bigr)=\bigl(\mathbb{D}(q_x)\otimes L\bigr)\circ\class{gk-tauGH}{\tau}_{H_A,x}.\qquad(\text{eq:square})\] Here \(\mathbb{D}(q_0)\) is the inclusion of \(N\), and \(\mathbb{D}(q_x)\colon\mathbb{D}(H_x)_{\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L}}\to\mathbb{D}(G_x)_{\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L}} =H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(X_x/\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L})\) is injective with locally free cokernel \(\mathbb{D}(\ker q_x)_{\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L}}\), by exactness of \(\mathbb{D}\). Since \(H_A=\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}^*H^{\mathrm{univ}}\) and \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}^*\colon R_{\mathrm{LT}}\to A\) is local, base change (v) gives \(\class{gk-tauGH}{\tau}_{H_A,x}=\class{gk-tauGH}{\tau}_{H^{\mathrm{univ}},\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}(x)}\). This is (a).

Step 2 [PROVED]. Let \(0\to K\to G\to H\to0\) be an exact sequence of \(p\)-divisible groups over a base \(S\) with \(p\) nilpotent. The argument in the proof of Lemma (the lifted block is unique) uses only this exact sequence, so it gives \[\omega_H=\omega_G\cap\mathbb{D}(H)_S .\] Passing to the limit over \(S=\operatorname{Spec}\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L}/p^m\) (inverse limits are left exact) and using (G1a), (G1b) for \(\omega_{G_x}=\mathrm{Fil}_x\) gives (b); the rank is \(\dim H_x=1\) and the quotient \(\mathrm{Lie}(H_x^\vee)\) is free.

Step 3 [PROVED GRANTING (G1a), (G1b), (G3)]. By (a), (b) and flatness of \(L\) over \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L}\), \[\begin{aligned} P(x)&=c_x^{-1}\bigl(\mathrm{Fil}_x\otimes L\ \cap\ c_x(N\otimes L)\bigr) =c_x^{-1}\bigl((\mathrm{Fil}_x\cap\mathbb{D}(H_x)_{\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L}})\otimes L\bigr)\\ &=\class{gk-tauGH}{\tau}_{H^{\mathrm{univ}},\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}(x)}^{-1}\bigl(\omega_{H_x}\otimes L\bigr)=\class{gk-GH}{\mathrm{GH}}(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}(x)). \end{aligned}\] The last assertion follows because \(\class{gk-GH}{\mathrm{GH}}\) and \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}^{\mathrm{rig}}\) are rigid analytic and a point of the rigid space \((\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}})^{\mathrm{rig}}\) with values in \(L\) is an element of \(\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{cont}}(A,\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L})\).

NOTE (Rin): the proof uses set-up stated before the lemma in new-blockperiod.tex and not shown here (m_0, n, the naturality (iv) and base change (v), the inputs (G1a), (G1b), (G3)), so it cannot be checked from this block alone. Notation: τ_{G,x} is a comparison map, not the group element τ.

Cor: Pimage · the period map \(P\) on \(\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}\) is onto \(\mathbb P^{h-1}\) on the rigid generic fiber · ♡

Corollary. Let \(C\) be a complete algebraically closed extension of \(K\). Then \(P\colon\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}(\mathcal{O}_C)\to\mathbb{P}^{h-1}(C)\) is surjective. Moreover every classical point of \(\mathbb{P}^{h-1}_K\) is the image under \(P\) of a classical point of \((\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}})^{\mathrm{rig}}\), and \(P\) is smooth as a rigid analytic morphism.

The proof in steps

1
Every point of the target of \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) lifts, by sending the extra coordinates \(t_j\) to \(0\).
2
By (G4) the map \(\class{gk-GH}{\mathrm{GH}}\) is onto on \(\mathcal O_C\)-points, so \(P\), which is \(\class{gk-GH}{\mathrm{GH}}\) after \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) once \(p\) is inverted (on the rigid generic fiber), is onto.
3
A classical point has a nonempty étale fiber under \(\class{gk-GH}{\mathrm{GH}}\), hence a point over a finite extension, which lifts through \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\).
4
Smoothness: \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}^{\mathrm{rig}}\) is a relative open polydisc and \(\class{gk-GH}{\mathrm{GH}}\) is étale.
Proof.

Status: [PROVED GRANTING (G1a), (G1b), (G3), (G4) and the presentation \(A=R_{\mathrm{LT}}[[t_1,\dots,t_m]]\)]. For any \(L\), the map \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\colon\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{cont}}(A,\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L})\to\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{cont}}(R_{\mathrm{LT}},\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal{O}_L})\) is surjective: extend a point by \(t_j\mapsto0\). By (G4), \(\class{gk-GH}{\mathrm{GH}}\) is surjective on \(\mathcal{O}_C\)-points, so \(P=\class{gk-GH}{\mathrm{GH}}\circ\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) is surjective on \(\mathcal{O}_C\)-points. If \(z\) is a classical point of \(\mathbb{P}^{h-1}_K\) with residue field \(L_0\), the fibre \(\class{gk-GH}{\mathrm{GH}}^{-1}(z)\) is a nonempty étale rigid space over \(L_0\), so it has a point over a finite extension \(L_1\) of \(L_0\); lift it through \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) over \(\mathcal{O}_{L_1}\). Smoothness: \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}^{\mathrm{rig}}\) is a relative open polydisc and \(\class{gk-GH}{\mathrm{GH}}\) is étale.

NOTE (Rin): the proof writes "P = GH∘Φ is surjective" without saying "after inverting p (on the rigid generic fiber)" in that sentence. The setting (points over O_C, C a field) is the rigid generic fiber, so nothing is wrong, but the tex sentence should carry those words.

The spirit of the answer: from symmetry to class field theory, after Lubin

From symmetry to class field theory: three steps, three owners

Lubin's own account (MathOverflow, 2015)

Why Lubin and Tate thought to use one-dimensional formal groups over the integers of a local field to construct local class field theory (question 220796, titled "Motivating Lubin-Tate theory").

"For whatever reason, I wanted to know about the endomorphism rings of these things, and gradually recognized the similarity between, on the one hand, the case of elliptic curves and their supersingular reduction mod \(p\), when that phenomenon did occur, and, on the other hand, formal groups over \(p\)-adic integer rings of higher height than \(1\)."

"Some while after my thesis, I was on a bus from Brunswick to Boston, and found not only that I could construct formal groups in all cases that had this maximal endomorphism structure, but that one of them could take the polynomial form \(\pi x+x^q\). Tate told me that when he saw this, Everything Fell Into Place."

"But if the endomorphism ring of your formal group is \(\mathfrak o\) and the Tate module of the formal group is a rank-one module over this endomorphism ring, can the isomorphism between the Galois group of \(k(F[p^\infty])\) over \(k\) and the subgroup \(\mathfrak o^*\subset k^*\) fail to make you think of the reciprocity map?"Jonathan Lubin, October 2015

Lubin found formal groups with maximal endomorphisms, \([\pi](x)=\pi x+x^q\), and Tate saw class field theory in their torsion. I come at Lubin's formal group from symmetry. At height \(\class{gk-h}{h}=p^{k-1}(p-1)\), the element \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) of order \(p^k\) in \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) makes \(\mathbb Z_p[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\) act on \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\), and the fixed points of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) on Lubin-Tate space carry exactly a Lubin-Tate formal module.
The curve sees the same object: on the block, Frobenius is \((\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}-1)\) times a unit, the crystalline form of \(\pi x+x^q\). Lubin went from elliptic curves to formal groups; \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) goes back, from \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) to a curve. I never use reciprocity. rewrite here · novelty of step B at general k unsure (check Gross 1986)

Lubin's road next to mine, step by step

Lubin (1965)Me, at \(\class{gk-h}{h}=p^{k-1}(p-1)\), \(\class{gk-L}{L}=\mathbb Q_p(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}})\)
0 · Start. Supersingular elliptic curves, compared with formal groups of height \(>1\) over \(p\)-adic rings.0 · Start. Big automorphisms: \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) of order \(p^k\) in \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\). Lubin starts from endomorphisms, I start from automorphisms.
1 · Maximal endomorphisms. \(\mathrm{End}=\mathfrak o\), the integers of a local field of degree \(h\) over \(\mathbb Q_p\).1. \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) generates \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}=\mathbb Z_p[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\) inside \(\mathrm{End}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\): a formal \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\)-module of height one. standard · lem:Omodule
2 · The bus. A formal group with \([\pi](x)=\pi x+x^q\) exists: the Lubin-Tate formal groups.2. The fixed points of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) on \(\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\) are \(\mathrm{Spf}\,\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O}\), and the deformation there is a Lubin-Tate formal \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\)-module: the symmetry picks out Lubin's object. thm:fixedlocus · ♡
(no step here)3. On the curve, the block \(\class{gk-Nblk}{N}\) is free of rank one over \(\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O}=\class{gk-Wring}{W}[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\), with \(F=(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}-1)\cdot\text{unit}\): the crystalline form of \(\pi x+x^q\). lem:F2 · prop:wild at \(k=1\)
3 · Reciprocity. Torsion points give a rank-one Tate module, and Galois acts through \(\mathfrak o^*\).4. Not used.

Two links click. First, in \([\pi](x)=\pi x+x^q\), multiplying by the uniformizer is Frobenius \(x\mapsto x^q\) up to higher terms; \(\class{gk-L}{L}\) is totally ramified, so \(q=p\), and \(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}-1\) is a uniformizer. So \(F=(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}-1)\cdot\text{unit}\) on the block is Lubin's shape, inside the crystal of the curve: the prequel's one identity, at every \(k\). Second, Lubin went from supersingular elliptic curves to formal groups; \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) goes back, from \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) to the curve \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\).

A · Big automorphisms\(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) has order \(p^k\) and acts on \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) as a root of unity \(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}\)used in lem:lift (part (b) of thm:fixedlocus)
A · Big endomorphismsThe dot is the element \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\in\mathbb Z_p[\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}]\subset\mathcal O_D\), so \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}=\mathbb Z_p[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\) acts on \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\): a formal \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\)-module of height oneused in lem:Omodule (part (c) of thm:fixedlocus)
B · No room to deformthe fixed points of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) on \(\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\) carry the Lubin-Tate module of \(\class{gk-L}{L}\)used in thm:fixedlocus, via Drinfeld, Prop. 4.2

Example (height \(2\)). Let \(p\ge7\) be a prime with \(p\equiv3\) mod \(4\), and let \(E\) be the elliptic curve \(y^2=x^3+x\) over \(\overline{\mathbb F}_p\). Its automorphism group is \(\mathbb Z/4\), generated by \(i\colon(x,y)\mapsto(-x,\sqrt{-1}\,y)\). Since \(p\equiv3\) mod \(4\), the curve \(E\) is supersingular, so its formal group has height \(2\), and \(\mathrm{End}(E)\otimes\mathbb Q\) is the definite quaternion algebra over \(\mathbb Q\) ramified at \(p\) and \(\infty\), with \(\mathrm{End}(E)\otimes\mathbb Z_p\) the maximal order \(\class{gk-OD}{\mathcal O_D}\) of the division algebra of invariant \(1/2\) over \(\mathbb Q_p\). The automorphism \(i\) generates the field \(\mathbb Q(i)\) inside this quaternion algebra.

The automorphism \(i\) alone does not force the quaternions, since for \(p\equiv1\) mod \(4\) the same equation is ordinary, its formal group has height \(1\), and \(\mathrm{End}(E)\otimes\mathbb Q=\mathbb Q(i)\) is commutative: the quaternions come from the height. At \(p=3\) this curve is our first case. It is isomorphic over \(\overline{\mathbb F}_3\) to the prequel's curve \(y^3-y=x^2\), its automorphism group is \(C_3\rtimes C_4\) of order \(12\), which is \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) at \((p,k)=(3,1)\) with \(\class{gk-h}{h}=2\), and the element \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) of order \(3\) generates \(\mathbb Z_3[\zeta_3]\) inside \(\class{gk-OD}{\mathcal O_D}\).

  • A. \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) has finite order, so \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) generates a field inside the division algebra: \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\) acts on \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\), a formal \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\)-module of height one. Standard (division algebras). In this paper: lem:lift, lem:Omodule · ♡
    Supporting results
  • A, on the curve. The block is free of rank one over \(\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O}\), with Frobenius equal to \((\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}-1)\) times a unit. This is my one identity from the prequel, \(\class{gk-unif}{\pi}^{\class{gk-h}{h}}=p\cdot(\text{unit})\), read as: Frobenius is \((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)\) times a unit. Mine: prequel Proposition 9.1 (prop:wild) at \(k=1\); lem:F2 at every \(k\) · Proved in the prequel \(k=1\) · ♡ every \(k\)
  • B. The fixed locus of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) on \(\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\) is \(\mathrm{Spf}\,\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O}\), and the universal \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-fixed deformation is a Lubin-Tate formal \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\)-module: no room to deform. The formal module that appears is the one Lubin and Tate constructed: the fixed points of the symmetry are exactly Lubin and Tate's formal group. the module: Lubin and Tate 1965 · thm:fixedlocus (Theorem (the fixed locus)), uses Drinfeld's deformation theory of formal \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\)-modules · complete modulo one citation (Drinfeld, Prop. 4.2); existence and uniqueness are both proved · ♡ · the uniqueness argument is the classical canonical-lifting one, so not new
    • Main theorems
    Remark after thm:fixedlocus (the universal \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-fixed deformation is the base change to \(\mathcal O\) of a Lubin-Tate formal \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\)-module): no proof written yet.
  • C. Galois acts on the torsion through \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}^\times\): the reciprocity map. Lubin and Tate 1965 · not used in this paper

For future work

Question 3Can the curve alone, without the functor, see which deformations keep the block?

Let \(p\) be odd and \(k\ge2\). Let \(\class{gk-theta}{\theta}\) be the character by which the center \(\class{gk-Gc}{G}\) acts, let \(H^1(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\mathcal O)_{\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}}\) be the part of \(H^1(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\mathcal O)\) on which \(\class{gk-Gc}{G}\) acts by \(\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}\), and let \(\bar F\) be Frobenius on it. Let \(\class{gk-N0}{\bar N_0}\) be the image in \(H^1(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\mathcal O)_{\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}}\) of the block \(\class{gk-Nblk}{N}\), reduced mod \(p\).

Conjecture. \(\class{gk-N0}{\bar N_0}=\bar F^{\,p^{k-1}-1}H^1(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\mathcal O)_{\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}}\).

So Frobenius on the curve alone, with no functor, would find the block. The conjecture holds at \((p,k)=(3,2)\), and it is confirmed by computation at \((3,3)\).

Let \(\class{gk-B0}{B_0}\cong\class{gk-WkFp}{W_k(\mathbb F_p)}\) be the branch orbit, let \(\class{gk-theta}{\theta}\) be the character by which the center \(\class{gk-Gc}{G}\) acts, and let \(\class{gk-Ptheta}{P(\theta)}=\kappa[\class{gk-B0}{B_0}]\otimes\class{gk-theta}{\theta}\) be the functions on the branch orbit, twisted by \(\class{gk-theta}{\theta}\), with radical \(\class{gk-rad}{\mathrm{rad}}\).

Theorem (Connective Tissue). The tangent space of Lubin-Tate space, as a \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\)-module, is the functions on the branch orbit modulo the gauge: \(\class{gk-Ptheta}{P(\theta)}/\class{gk-rad}{\mathrm{rad}}^{h-1}\class{gk-Ptheta}{P(\theta)}\). This module is uniserial (a single chain), and a single chain is fixed by its top and its length. From \(k=1\) to every \(k\), only the gauge \(\class{gk-rad}{\mathrm{rad}}^{h-1}\class{gk-Ptheta}{P(\theta)}\) grows: from the affine functions \(a+bz\) on \(\mathbb F_p\) to the difference polynomials of degree at most \(p^{k-1}\) on \(\class{gk-WkFp}{W_k(\mathbb F_p)}\).

The Lubin-Tate half of the Connective Tissue theorem follows from Cartier or Grothendieck-Messing theory, and is prop:cotangent, dualized. What is new is that a moduli of curves carries the same module, with \(\class{gk-WkFp}{W_k(\mathbb F_p)}\) as its branch orbit. The proof is not in this version.

Question 4Does \(\pi_{-2}E_{\class{gk-h}{h}}\) have a generator whose trace is zero?

Let \(g=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^{p^{k-1}}\), so that \(g\) generates the subgroup of order \(p\) of \(\langle\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\rangle\), and let \(\mathrm{Tr}=1+g+\dots+g^{p-1}\). Let \(R=\pi_0E_{\class{gk-h}{h}}\), with maximal ideal \(\mathfrak m\), and let \(L=\pi_{-2}E_{\class{gk-h}{h}}\), a free \(R\)-module of rank one. Let \(J_g\) be the ideal of \(R\) generated by all \(gr-r\). For an integer \(j\), we say that \(L^{\otimes j}\) has a trace-zero generator if it has a generator \(u\) over \(R\) with \(\mathrm{Tr}(u)=0\). We write (TZ) for the statement that \(L\) has a trace-zero generator. Given (TZ), we get our own proof of the HHR formula (conj:action) for all of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\).

Lemma. Let \(p\) be odd and let \(j\) be prime to \(p\). Then \(L^{\otimes j}\) has a trace-zero generator if and only if \(L^{\otimes j}/p\) has a generator \(\bar u\) over \(R/p\) with \((g-1)^{p-1}\bar u=0\).

In progress proof written in our notes, being checked

In characteristic \(p\) we have \(\mathrm{Tr}=(g-1)^{p-1}\), so one direction is reduction modulo \(p\). For the other, we lift \(\bar u\) to a generator \(u\) of \(L^{\otimes j}\), so that \(\mathrm{Tr}(u)=pw\) for some \(w\in L^{\otimes j}\). Since \(\mathrm{Tr}(u)\) is \(g\)-invariant and \(L^{\otimes j}\) has no \(p\)-torsion, \(w\) is \(g\)-invariant. On the fixed locus of \(g\), the element \(g\) acts on \(L^{\otimes j}/J_gL^{\otimes j}\) by a primitive \(p\)-th root of unity raised to the power \(j\), and this is not \(1\) because \(j\) is prime to \(p\). So \(L^{\otimes j}/J_gL^{\otimes j}\) has no nonzero invariants, and \(w\) lies in \(J_gL^{\otimes j}\subseteq\mathfrak m L^{\otimes j}\). Hence \(u-w\) is still a generator, and \(\mathrm{Tr}(u-w)=pw-pw=0\).

The argument uses the fixed locus twice: the fixed locus of \(g\) exists only over \(W[\zeta_p]\), and \(g\) acts there by a nontrivial root of unity. So nothing over \(W\) is left to prove, and (TZ) becomes a question in characteristic \(p\): does \(L/p\) have a generator killed by \((g-1)^{p-1}\)? In characteristic \(p\) the surgeries, the pole filtration and the fibers of Main Theorem 2 are all equivariant, which is where we hope to find this generator.

(TZ) itself is open for \(k\ge2\). By machine, in characteristic \(p\) at \((p,k)=(3,2)\), the lines \(L\) and \(L^{\otimes -2}\) have such a generator to order \(10\), and \(L^{\otimes -1}\) does not from order \(3\). At height \(2\), to order \(13\), the power \(L^{\otimes 3}\) also passes, which is the excluded case \(j\equiv0\) modulo \(p\): there the norm is an invariant generator. So the hypothesis on \(j\) is needed.

Open question (TZ) for \(k\ge2\) · Computed to order \(10\) at \((3,2)\)

After the main theorems

Where the curves sit: a Shimura variety

The Shimura variety of Behrens and Hopkins does not contain our curves (Remark (Shimura varieties) in section 1). Our curves do have a home among Shimura varieties, and the block-preserving condition becomes a Schubert condition inside it. Write \(S=\frac{p^k-1}{p-1}\) and \(g_Y=\frac{p(p^{k-1}-1)(p^k-1)}{2(p+1)}\), the genus of \(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k}\).

  1. For \(1\le j\le p-2\), the \(\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-j}\)-eigenspace of \(H^0(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\Omega^1)\) consists of the forms \(z^{-j}\eta\), with \(\eta\) a differential on \(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k}\) that is regular away from \(\class{gk-Qinf}{Q_\infty}\) and has \(\mathrm{ord}_{\class{gk-Qinf}{Q_\infty}}\eta\ge-(jS+1)\). So its dimension is \(f(\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-j})=g_Y+jS\), and \(f(1)=g_Y\). These add up to the genus of \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) from thm:curve: at \((p,k)=(3,2)\), \(6+10=16\).
  2. Torelli sends the deformations of \((\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\class{gk-Gc}{G})\) into \(\mathrm{Sh}(\mu_{p-1},f)\), the PEL Shimura variety for \(\mathbb Q[\mu_{p-1}]\) with signature \(f\): a Siegel factor of genus \(g_Y\) for the trivial character (the Jacobian of \(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k}\)), a unitary factor of signature \((g_Y+jS,\ g_Y+(p-1-j)S)\) for each pair \(\{\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{j},\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-j}\}\), and a symplectic factor for \(j=(p-1)/2\). At \((3,2)\) it is \(\mathcal A_6\) times the symplectic factor of the \(10\)-dimensional Prym part, of total dimension \(21+55=76\), while the deformations of \((Y_2,B)\) form \(25\) dimensions and Lubin-Tate space has \(5\).
  3. The block is \(\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}\)-isotypic (thm:norm (d)). Since \(p\) splits completely in \(\mathbb Q(\zeta_{p-1})\), the eigenpiece \(\mathbb G=\mathrm{Jac}\,\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}[p^\infty]_{\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}}\) deforms along every deformation of \((\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\class{gk-Gc}{G})\). It has height \(\frac{p^{2k}-1}{p+1}\), dimension \(d=g_Y+S\) and codimension \(c=g_Y+(p-2)S\); counting degrees of \(L\)-functions over the characters of \(\langle\sigma\rangle\) gives the same height. At \(k=1\) the height is \(p-1\) and \(\mathbb G=\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\) (the prequel). For \(k\ge2\), \(\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\) is a quotient of height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\) inside a much bigger \(\mathbb G\): at \((3,2)\), \(6\) inside \(20\).
  4. Unwinding the definition of \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\), \[\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}=\mathrm{Def}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\class{gk-Gc}{G})\times_{\mathrm{Def}(\mathbb G)}\mathrm{Def}(\mathbb G\twoheadrightarrow \class{gk-HX}{H_X}),\] the Torelli germ cut by the locus where the quotient \(\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\) lifts. On crystals the Dieudonne module \(N\) of \(\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\) stays constant, and the condition is that \(\mathrm{Fil}^1\cap N\) stays a line. A Schubert condition on a Grassmannian asks a moving subspace to meet a fixed subspace in at least a given dimension (for example, lines in \(\mathbb P^3\) meeting a fixed line); its solutions form a Schubert locus. Under the crystalline period map, where \(\mathrm{Fil}^1\) moves in a Grassmannian and \(N\) is fixed, our condition is the Schubert condition \(\{\mathrm{Fil}:\dim(\mathrm{Fil}\cap N)\ge1\}\), of codimension \(c-(\class{gk-h}{h}-1)\) in \(\mathrm{Def}(\mathbb G)\): at \((3,2)\), \(50\) inside \(55\). The functor \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) remembers the line \(\mathrm{Fil}^1\cap N\) in \(\mathbb P(N)=\mathbb P^{\class{gk-h}{h}-1}\), which agrees with \(\mathrm{GH}\circ\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}=P\) after inverting \(p\). For \(k\ge2\) the condition is not automatic: the Newton polygon of \(\mathbb G\) breaks at \((\class{gk-h}{h},1)\), while the Hodge polygon is still at height \(0\) there because \(c\ge\class{gk-h}{h}\), so \(\mathbb G\) is not Hodge-Newton decomposable. At \(k=1\) it is, trivially, which is why the quotient is a summand there.
  5. \(\mathrm{Sh}(\mu_{p-1},f)\) is the smallest Shimura variety of PEL type containing the deformations, since only \(\class{gk-Gc}{G}\) survives deformation. Whether a smaller special subvariety contains the germ \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\) is open; the Schubert condition is \(p\)-adic and does not come from endomorphisms, so we expect none.

So for \(k\ge2\), Lubin-Tate space is no longer a formal factor of a Shimura variety: it is the target of a Schubert locus inside one, and that is our fibered model.

Computed (1) to (4), checked at \((3,2)\) · Open question (5)

\(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G},\ \class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G'}\): formal groups over \(A\). In thm:fixedlocus and its lemmas, \((\mathcal G,\iota)\) is a deformation of \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) over \(A\), and \(\tilde{\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}}\) is the lift of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) to \(\mathcal G\).
In the proof of lem:onestep only: \(\class{gk-sigmalift}{\beta}\in I\,\text{Ш}'\) is a lift of the class \(\mathrm{ob}(X')\), written \(\tilde e=f+q+\class{gk-sigmalift}{\beta}\).
In lem:GHcomposite: \(\class{gk-tauGH}{\tau}_{G,x}\), \(\class{gk-tauGH}{\tau}_{H,x}\) are the comparison isomorphisms (quasi-isogenies read on Dieudonné modules) at the point \(x\). Not the group element \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\).
\(P\): the Hodge line of the block (not called a period map). \(P\) sends a deformation of the curve (over \(\mathcal O_C\), \(p\) inverted) to the Hodge line \(\mathrm{Fil}\cap N\) of the block of that deformation, a line in the constant \(h\)-dimensional space, so a point of \(\mathbb P^{h-1}\). Lemma (lem:GHcomposite): after inverting \(p\), on the rigid generic fiber, \(P\) equals \(\class{gk-GH}{\mathrm{GH}}\) composed after \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\); over Artinian rings \(\class{gk-GH}{\mathrm{GH}}\) is not defined. \(P\) and \(\class{gk-GH}{\mathrm{GH}}\) are different maps with different sources.
\(\bar N_0\): the image of the block mod \(p\) in \(H^1(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\mathcal O)_{\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}}\), of dimension \(\class{gk-h}{h}-1\).
\(\pi=\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}-1\): a uniformizer of \(\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\); \(\pi^{\class{gk-h}{h}}=p\cdot(\text{unit})\). At \(k=1\): \(\pi^{p-1}=-p\cdot(\text{unit})\), the prequel's one identity.
\(F\): Frobenius on the crystalline cohomology of the curve (semilinear).
\(N\): the block in crystalline cohomology, the part of slope \(1/\class{gk-h}{h}\) of \(H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k})\) (in the \(\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}\) part). Of rank \(\class{gk-h}{h}\), with \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) acting with characteristic polynomial \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}\) (Lemma Dw, Dwork's theorem).
\(T^{\mathrm{bl}}\): the tangent space of \(\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\). By thm:horsmooth(i), \(T^{\mathrm{bl}}=\class{gk-Lmap}{\text{Д}}^{-1}(\class{gk-N0}{\bar N_0})\).
\(L\colon \class{gk-Mk}{M_k}\to H^1(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\mathcal O)_{\class{gk-theta}{\theta}^{-1}}\), \(v\mapsto \class{gk-KS}{\mathrm{KS}}(v)\cup\class{gk-omegaH}{\omega_H}\): the displacement map. It is onto (lem:cleanbaby).
\(\Phi_{p^k}(x)=\dfrac{x^{p^k}-1}{x^{p^{k-1}}-1}\): the cyclotomic polynomial of degree \(\class{gk-h}{h}\). Not the functor \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\).
\(f\): one relation with \(f(0)\in p\class{gk-Wring}{W}^\times\), which can be chosen \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\)-invariant; \(f,\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}f,\dots,\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^nf\) (\(n=p^{k-1}\)) is a regular sequence generating all the relations. On the fixed locus, \(f(t,\dots,t)\) is an Eisenstein polynomial times a unit.
\(\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_W(\class{gk-rhoW}{\rho_W})=\class{gk-Wring}{W}[[x_0,\dots,x_{p^k-1}]]\): the completed symmetric algebra, a power series ring in \(p^k\) variables permuted by \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\).
\(\rho_W=\mathrm{Ind}_{\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle}^{\class{gk-Gp}{G'}}\tilde\chi\): the representation of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) induced from the Teichmüller lift \(\tilde\chi\) of the conjugation character \(\chi\) (\(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\class{gk-tau}{\tau}^{-1}=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^a\), \(\chi(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})=a\bmod p\)). A free \(\class{gk-Wring}{W}\)-module of rank \(p^k\), with basis \(x_i=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^ie\).
\(\mathrm{Def}(X_1,G)\): deformations of the prequel's curve \(\class{gk-X1}{X_1}\) together with the action of the center \(\class{gk-Gc}{G}\), over Artinian local \(\class{gk-Wring}{W}\)-algebras. Pro-represented by \(\class{gk-Wring}{W}[[t_1,\dots,t_{p-2}]]\): the configurations of the \(p+1\) branch points on \(\mathbb P^1\), modulo moving three.
\(X_1\colon y^p-y=z^{p-1}\): the curve of the prequel (\(k=1\)), of genus \((p-1)(p-2)/2\), the unique curve of least genus with a faithful action of \(\mathbb Z/p\rtimes\mathbb Z/(p-1)^2\) (Tiny and Unique, Theorem A). At \(p=3\) it is the supersingular elliptic curve \(y^3-y=z^2\).
\(X_k\colon\ \class{gk-wp}{\wp}(y_0,\dots,y_{k-1})=(z^{p-1},0,\dots,0)\), with \(\class{gk-wp}{\wp}=F-1\) on Witt vectors of length \(k\). At \(k\ge2\) the group \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) determines a connected family of curves of least genus (thm:curve); \(X_k\) is the simplest member of that family. \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) adds \((1,0,\dots,0)\); \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\colon z\mapsto\zeta z\). At \(k=1\) it is the prequel's curve \(y^p-y=z^{p-1}\).
\(\mathbb A^1\): the affine line over \(\overline{\mathbb F}_p\), with coordinate \(z\).
\([a]=(a,0,\dots,0)\): the Teichmüller representative of \(a\) in \(\class{gk-Wk}{W_k}\).
\(\wp=F-1\colon\class{gk-Wk}{W_k}\to\class{gk-Wk}{W_k}\), with \(F\) raising every coordinate to the \(p\)-th power. The map \(\wp\) is étale and onto, with kernel \(\class{gk-WkFp}{W_k(\mathbb F_p)}\cong\mathbb Z/p^k\): a \(\mathbb Z/p^k\)-torsor, the universal one in characteristic \(p\) (Artin-Schreier-Witt). At \(k=1\), \(\wp\) is \(y\mapsto y^p-y\).
\(W_k\): the scheme of \(p\)-typical Witt vectors quotiented by the \(k\)-th power of Verschiebung, over \(\mathbb F_p\); a point over a ring \(R\) is a vector \((a_0,\dots,a_{k-1})\) of elements of \(R\), added and multiplied with Witt's carry polynomials. At \(k=1\), \(W_1\) is the affine line.

Definition (geometric model; Modeling Group Actions on Stacks, §Geometric Modelling). Given prestacks \(\class{gk-M}{\mathcal M}\) and \(\class{gk-N}{\mathcal N}\), a natural transformation \(\class{gk-F}{\mathcal F}\colon\mathcal M\to\mathcal N\), and an object \(\class{gk-Xgen}{X}\in\mathcal M(\kappa)\), \(\class{gk-F}{\mathcal F}\) induces a homomorphism \(\class{gk-Fstar}{\mathcal F_*}\colon\mathrm{Aut}_\kappa(\class{gk-Xgen}{X})\to\mathrm{Aut}_\kappa(\class{gk-F}{\mathcal F}(\class{gk-Xgen}{X}))\) and a functor \[\class{gk-Ftil}{\widetilde{\mathcal F}}\colon\class{gk-Dstar}{\mathrm{Def}_\class{gk-Xgen}{X}^{\star,\mathcal M}}\to\class{gk-Dstar}{\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal F(\class{gk-Xgen}{X})}^{\star,\class{gk-N}{\mathcal N}}},\qquad(\mathfrak X,\iota)\mapsto(\class{gk-F}{\mathcal F}(\mathfrak X),\class{gk-F}{\mathcal F}(\iota)),\] Let \(\class{gk-Ggen}{G}\subseteq\mathrm{Aut}_\kappa(\class{gk-Xgen}{X})\). We say \(\class{gk-Ftil}{\widetilde{\mathcal F}}\) is \(\class{gk-Ggen}{G}\)-equivariant if \(\class{gk-Fstar}{\mathcal F_*}\) is injective on \(\class{gk-Ggen}{G}\); then \(\class{gk-Ftil}{\widetilde{\mathcal F}}\) intertwines the two actions of \(\class{gk-Ggen}{G}\) automatically, \(\class{gk-Ftil}{\widetilde{\mathcal F}}(g\cdot\xi)=\class{gk-Fstar}{\mathcal F_*}(g)\cdot\class{gk-Ftil}{\widetilde{\mathcal F}}(\xi)\). If \(\class{gk-Ftil}{\widetilde{\mathcal F}}\) is \(\class{gk-Ggen}{G}\)-equivariant and an equivalence, the tuple \[\big(\class{gk-Xgen}{X}\in\mathcal M(\kappa),\ \class{gk-Ggen}{G}\subseteq\mathrm{Aut}_\kappa(\class{gk-Xgen}{X}),\ \class{gk-Ftil}{\widetilde{\mathcal F}}\colon\class{gk-Dstar}{\mathrm{Def}_\class{gk-Xgen}{X}^{\star,\mathcal M}}\to\class{gk-Dstar}{\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal F(\class{gk-Xgen}{X})}^{\star,\class{gk-N}{\mathcal N}}}\big)\] is a geometric model for the action of \(\class{gk-Ggen}{G}\) on \(\class{gk-Dstar}{\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal F(\class{gk-Xgen}{X})}^{\star,\class{gk-N}{\mathcal N}}}\).

For Lubin-Tate: \(\class{gk-N}{\mathcal N}\) is formal groups, \(\class{gk-F}{\mathcal F}(\class{gk-Xgen}{X})=H_h\), and \(\class{gk-Dstar}{\mathrm{Def}^\star_{H_h}}=\mathrm{Spf}\,\pi_0E_h\).

\(G\subseteq\mathrm{Aut}_\kappa(Y)\): a group of automorphisms of the target object, part of the data. In this paper: \(\class{gk-Gp}{G\'}\subseteq\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\), the maximal finite subgroup. (Elsewhere on this page, \(G\) alone is the center of \(G\'\).)
\(X\in\mathcal M(\kappa)\), with \(\mathcal F(X)\cong Y\) and \(g\mapsto g_X\): a lift of \(G\), the object being deformed. In this paper: \(X=(X_k,\ \mathrm{Jac}\,X_k[p^\infty]\twoheadrightarrow H_X)\), the curve with its block.
The prestack \(\mathcal N\) of one-dimensional formal groups (connected \(p\)-divisible groups) of height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\). Objects over \(R\): such groups \(\mathcal H/R\). Morphisms: isomorphisms. The object is \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\in\mathcal N(\kappa)\).
\(\mathcal F_*\colon\mathrm{Aut}_\kappa(X)\to\mathrm{Aut}_\kappa(\class{gk-F}{\mathcal F}(X))\): the homomorphism a natural transformation \(\class{gk-F}{\mathcal F}\) induces on automorphisms. Here an automorphism \((\varphi,\psi)\) of \(X=(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\twoheadrightarrow \class{gk-HX}{H_X})\) goes to \(\psi\in\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-HX}{H_X})\cong\class{gk-OD}{\mathcal O_D}^\times\); on \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\) it is \(\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\). A lift asks \(\mathcal F_*(g_X)=g\) for every \(g\in G\).
\(\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cd}}\): the prestack of curves with a divisor. Objects over \(R\): \((\mathcal Y,\mathcal B)\), \(\mathcal Y/R\) flat proper, \(\mathcal B\) a relative Cartier divisor. Morphisms: isomorphisms carrying \(\mathcal B\) to \(\mathcal B'\).
\(\class{gk-W}{W}=W(\overline{\mathbb F}_p)\): the Witt vectors of \(\overline{\mathbb F}_p\), the ring of integers of the maximal unramified extension of \(\mathbb Q_p\), completed.
For a prestack \(\class{gk-M}{\mathcal M}\) and an object \(X\in\class{gk-M}{\mathcal M}(\kappa)\): \(\mathrm{Def}^{\star,\class{gk-M}{\mathcal M}}_X\), the deformations of \(X\) in \(\class{gk-M}{\mathcal M}\). Over Artinian local \(\class{gk-W}{W}\)-algebras \(R\) with residue field \(\kappa\): objects \((\mathfrak X,\class{gk-iota}{\iota})\) with \(\mathfrak X\in\class{gk-M}{\mathcal M}(R)\) and \(\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\colon\mathfrak X\otimes_R\kappa\xrightarrow{\sim}X\); morphisms the isomorphisms in \(\class{gk-M}{\mathcal M}(R)\) compatible with \(\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\) (star-isomorphisms).
\(\widetilde{\mathcal F}\colon\mathrm{Def}^{\star,\class{gk-M}{\mathcal M}}_X\to\mathrm{Def}^{\star,\mathcal N}_{\class{gk-F}{\mathcal F}(X)}\): the functor that \(\class{gk-F}{\mathcal F}\) induces on deformations, \((\mathfrak X,\class{gk-iota}{\iota})\mapsto(\class{gk-F}{\mathcal F}(\mathfrak X),\class{gk-F}{\mathcal F}(\class{gk-iota}{\iota}))\). Here it is \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\colon\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\to\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\): keep the formal group, forget the curve.
\(G'=\langle\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\rangle\rtimes\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\rangle\cong\mathbb Z/p^k\rtimes\mathbb Z/(p-1)^2\), \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\class{gk-tau}{\tau}^{-1}=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^{\chi(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})}\), \(\chi\) of order \(p-1\): the maximal finite subgroup of the Morava stabilizer group containing an element of order \(p^k\). Unique up to conjugacy.
The center \(G=\langle\class{gk-tau}{\tau}^{p-1}\rangle\) of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\), of order \(p-1\), prime to \(p\).
\(\bar G=\class{gk-Gp}{G'}/\class{gk-Gc}{G}\), the group modulo its center; it acts on the quotient curve \(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k}\).
The Morava stabilizer group \(\mathbb G_h=\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})=\class{gk-OD}{\mathcal O_D}^\times\).
\(Y_k=\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}/\class{gk-Gc}{G}\), the quotient by the center: \(\wp(y)=(s,0,\dots,0)\), \(s=z^{p-1}\).
\(\mathrm{Def}(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k},B)=\mathrm{Def}^{\star,\class{gk-Mcd}{\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cd}}}}_{(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k},B)}\): deformations of the object \((\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k},B)\) in the prestack \(\class{gk-Mcd}{\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cd}}}\) of curves with a divisor, over Artinian local \(\class{gk-W}{W}\)-algebras \(R\) with residue field \(\kappa\). Objects: \((\mathcal Y,\mathcal B,\alpha)\), \(\mathcal Y/R\) flat proper, \(\mathcal B\) a relative Cartier divisor, \(\alpha\colon(\mathcal Y,\mathcal B)\otimes \kappa\xrightarrow{\sim}(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k},B)\). Morphisms: isomorphisms carrying \(\mathcal B\) to \(\mathcal B'\), compatible with \(\alpha\). No automorphisms, so a set.
\(\mathrm{Def}(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k},\class{gk-Gc}{G})=\mathrm{Def}^{\star,\mathcal M_G}_{\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}}\): deformations of the object \(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) in the prestack \(\mathcal M_G\) of smooth proper curves with a \(\class{gk-Gc}{G}\)-action, over Artinian local \(\class{gk-W}{W}\)-algebras with residue field \(\kappa\). Objects: \((\mathcal X,\class{gk-iota}{\iota})\), \(\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\colon\mathcal X\otimes \kappa\xrightarrow{\sim}\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\) \(\class{gk-Gc}{G}\)-equivariant. Morphisms: \(\class{gk-Gc}{G}\)-equivariant isomorphisms compatible with \(\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\).
The branch orbit \(B_0=\{s=0\}\cong \class{gk-WkFp}{W_k(\mathbb F_p)}\): \(p^k\) points, permuted simply transitively by \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\).
\(Q_\infty\): the point fixed by all of \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\). \(B=\class{gk-B0}{B_0}\cup\{Q_\infty\}\).
\(B=\class{gk-B0}{B_0}\cup\{\class{gk-Qinf}{Q_\infty}\}\): the branch orbit together with the fixed point.
\(\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}=\mathrm{Def}^{\star,\class{gk-M}{\mathcal M}}_X\): deformations of the object \(X=(\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}\twoheadrightarrow \class{gk-HX}{H_X})\) in the prestack \(\class{gk-M}{\mathcal M}\) of curves with a quotient of height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\). Forgetting the quotient makes it the subfunctor of \(\class{gk-DefYB}{\mathrm{Def}(Y_k,B)}\) of deformations along which \(\mathrm{Jac}\,\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}[p^\infty]\twoheadrightarrow \class{gk-HX}{H_X}\) lifts (uniquely). A closed formal substack, stable under \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\).
The functor \(\text{Б}=\class{gk-Ftil}{\widetilde{\mathcal F}}\colon\class{gk-Defbl}{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{bl}}}\to\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\). On objects \(X_R\mapsto(\class{gk-HR}{H_R},\ \class{gk-HR}{H_R}\otimes \kappa=\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\cong \class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\); on morphisms, the induced isomorphism of lifted quotients.
Lubin-Tate space \(\mathrm{Def}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})=\mathrm{Def}^{\star,\mathcal N}_{\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}}\): deformations of the object \(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\) in the prestack \(\mathcal N\) of one-dimensional formal groups of height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\), over Artinian local \(\class{gk-W}{W}\)-algebras with residue field \(\kappa\). Objects: \((\mathcal H,\class{gk-iota}{\iota})\), \(\class{gk-iota}{\iota}\colon\mathcal H\otimes \kappa\xrightarrow{\sim}\class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\). Morphisms: star-isomorphisms. Pro-represented by \(W[[u_1,\dots,u_{h-1}]]=\pi_0\class{gk-E}{E_h}\).
\(H_h\): a one-dimensional formal group of height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\) over \(\overline{\mathbb F}_p\).
\(H_X\): the formal group cut out of \(\mathrm{Jac}\,\class{gk-Xk}{X_k}[p^\infty]\) by the block; \(H_X\cong \class{gk-Hh}{H_h}\).
\(H_R\): the lift of \(\class{gk-HX}{H_X}\) along a block-preserving deformation over \(R\); unique by Grothendieck-Messing.
The ghosts \(\mathfrak g=\ker\big(d\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\big)\): the tangent space of the fiber of \(\class{gk-Phi}{\text{Б}}\) over the base point. The fiber is the deformations of the curve whose formal group stays constant: the curve moves, the formal group of the curve does not. The fiber is connected and formally smooth (thm:horsmooth), so Lubin-Tate space is the base of this fibration (not a quotient by a group action).
\(\sigma\): the generator of order \(p^k\) in \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\). On the curve it adds \((1,0,\dots,0)\) in Witt coordinates.
\(\tau\): the generator of order \((p-1)^2\) in \(\class{gk-Gp}{G'}\); on the curve \(z\mapsto\zeta z\).
\(\theta\): a primitive \((p-1)\)-th root of unity, a generator of \(\mathbb F_p^\times\). The generator \(\tau\) of order \((p-1)^2\) scales the coordinate \(x\) by \(\zeta\), a primitive \((p-1)^2\)-th root of unity, and \(\theta=\zeta^{p-1}\); so the generator \(\tau^{p-1}\) of the center \(G\) sends \(x\) to \(\theta x\) and fixes each \(\tilde y_i\). \(G\)-weight: an eigenvector \(v\) of \(G\) has \(G\)-weight \(\theta^m\) if \(\tau^{p-1}\cdot v=\theta^m v\); the \(\theta^m\)-isotypic part is the span of the eigenvectors of weight \(\theta^m\). Only \(m\) mod \(p-1\) matters. Example: \(x^a\tilde y^{\underline b}\,dx\) has \(G\)-weight \(\theta^{a+1}\). \(\theta\) also names the character through which \(\bar\tau\) scales.
\(M_k=H^1(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k},T(-B))\): the tangent space of \(\class{gk-DefYB}{\mathrm{Def}(Y_k,B)}\).
\(P(\class{gk-theta}{\theta})=\mathrm{Ind}_{\langle\bar{\class{gk-tau}{\tau}}\rangle}^{\class{gk-Gbar}{\bar G}}\class{gk-theta}{\theta}\cong\mathrm{Fun}(\class{gk-WkFp}{W_k(\mathbb F_p)},\kappa)\otimes\class{gk-theta}{\theta}\): the functions on the branch orbit, twisted by \(\class{gk-theta}{\theta}\). Dimension \(p^k\).
\(W_k(\mathbb F_p)\cong\mathbb Z/p^k\): Witt vectors of length \(k\) over \(\mathbb F_p\); the branch orbit, with \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) adding \(1\) and \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\) scaling by Teichmüller.
\(\omega_H\): the Hodge line of the block, the line \(\bar N\cap\mathrm{Fil}^1\).
\(\mathrm{KS}(v)\): the Kodaira-Spencer class of a tangent vector \(v\), in \(H^1(\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k},T(-B))\).
\(h=\phi(p^k)=p^{k-1}(p-1)\): the height.
\(U=\class{gk-Yk}{Y_k}\smallsetminus \class{gk-B0}{B_0}\): the affine curve away from the branch orbit; it contains \(\class{gk-Qinf}{Q_\infty}\).
\(\mathcal O_D\): the maximal order of the division algebra \(D\) of invariant \(1/\class{gk-h}{h}\) over \(\mathbb Q_p\); \(\mathcal O_D^\times=\mathrm{Aut}(\class{gk-Hh}{H_h})\).
\(\iota=\class{gk-Fstar}{\mathcal F_*}|_{\class{gk-Gp}{G'}}\colon \class{gk-Gp}{G'}\to\mathrm{Aut}_\kappa(\class{gk-HX}{H_X})\cong\class{gk-OD}{\mathcal O_D}^\times\): an automorphism \((\varphi,\psi)\) of \(X\) goes to \(\psi\), its action on the block.
\(\mathrm{rad}\): the radical of a \(\kappa\class{gk-Gbar}{\bar G}\)-module. \(\mathrm{rad}^{h-1}\class{gk-Ptheta}{P(\theta)}\) is the gauge.
\(\chi_\omega\): the twist identifying the Frobenius module with the Lubin-Tate module (Theorem E(iii)).
\(\mathfrak S_k\): the pole-order stack, surgeries of order \(\class{gk-r}{r}\) modulo lower order and the gauge. First order only; its tangent is \(\class{gk-Ptheta}{P(\theta)}/\class{gk-rad}{\mathrm{rad}}^{h-1}\class{gk-Ptheta}{P(\theta)}\).
\(E_h\): Morava \(E\)-theory; \(\pi_0E_h\) is the Lubin-Tate ring.
\(\mathcal M\): a prestack, a functor from \(\class{gk-W}{W}\)-algebras to groupoids. In this paper: \(\mathcal M(R)\) has objects \((\mathcal X,q)\), a smooth proper curve with \(\class{gk-Gc}{G}\)-action and a quotient \(q\colon\mathrm{Jac}(\mathcal X)[p^\infty]\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal H\), one-dimensional of height \(\class{gk-h}{h}\); morphisms \((\varphi,\psi)\) with \(q'\circ\varphi_*=\psi\circ q\).
\(\mathcal F\colon\class{gk-M}{\mathcal M}\to\mathcal N\): a natural transformation of prestacks. In this paper: \((\mathcal X,q\colon\mathrm{Jac}(\mathcal X)[p^\infty]\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal H)\mapsto\mathcal H\), \((\varphi,\psi)\mapsto\psi\): forget the curve, keep the formal group.
\(\mathrm{Fil}\): the Hodge filtration of the crystal, a line (or subspace) inside the isocrystal; moving it is what deforming the formal group means.
\(\mathrm{GH}\): the Gross-Hopkins period map, an etale map from the rigid generic fibre of \(\class{gk-LT}{\mathrm{Def}(H_h)}\) to \(\mathbb P^{h-1}\).
\(R_C=R/\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)R\bigr)\), where \(R=\pi_0\class{gk-E}{E_h}\) is the Lubin-Tate ring and \(\bigl((\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1)R\bigr)\) is the ideal generated by all \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}r-r\). It represents the deformations fixed by \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\), so \(\mathrm{Spf}\,R_C\) is the fixed locus.
\(\zeta_{p^k}\): a primitive \(p^k\)-th root of unity.
\(\mathcal O=\class{gk-Wring}{W}[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]=\class{gk-OL}{\mathcal O_L}\otimes_{\mathbb Z_p}\class{gk-Wring}{W}\): a complete discrete valuation ring with uniformizer \(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}-1\) and residue field \(\kappa\); the ring of integers of the completed maximal unramified extension of \(\class{gk-L}{L}\).
\(\mathcal O_L=\mathbb Z_p[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\): the ring of integers of \(\class{gk-L}{L}\).
\(L=\mathbb Q_p(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}})\): the cyclotomic field, totally ramified of degree \(\class{gk-h}{h}\) over \(\mathbb Q_p\).
\(C=\langle\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\rangle\cong\mathbb Z/p^k\): the cyclic group generated by \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\).
\(\mathfrak m=(p,u_1,\dots,u_{h-1})\): the maximal ideal of the Lubin-Tate ring \(R=\pi_0\class{gk-E}{E_h}\); \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\) is its cotangent space, of dimension \(\class{gk-h}{h}\).
\(x\): the top of the single chain \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\), chosen as a \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\)-eigenvector; on the fixed locus it becomes a uniformizer of \(\class{gk-Wring}{W}[\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}]\).
\(n=p^{k-1}\), so that \(p^k=\class{gk-h}{h}+n\).
\(\chi\): the conjugation character, \(\class{gk-tau}{\tau}\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\class{gk-tau}{\tau}^{-1}=\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}^{a}\) with \(\chi(\class{gk-tau}{\tau})=a\bmod p\); \(\tilde\chi\) is its Teichmüller lift. Not the twist \(\chi_\omega\).
\(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}-1\): nilpotent on \(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\), since \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) has \(p\)-power order.
\(c\): the leading coefficient of the lift of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) to a fixed deformation, \(\sigma_{\mathcal G}(X)=cX+\cdots\); a root of \(\class{gk-cyc}{\Phi_{p^k}}\) reducing to \(1\).
\(\sigma_{\mathcal G}\): the unique lift of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) to an automorphism of a \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\)-fixed deformation \(\class{gk-calG}{\mathcal G}\) (lem:lift).
\(K=I/\mathfrak m_SI\): the relations read on cotangent spaces; isomorphic to \(\mathrm{rad}^{h-1}(\rho_\kappa)\), the gauge, one chain of length \(n+1\).
\(\nu\colon\class{gk-Odf}{\mathcal O}\to\class{gk-RC}{R_C}\): the unique isomorphism of \(\class{gk-Wring}{W}\)-algebras sending \(\class{gk-zeta}{\zeta_{p^k}}\) to the scalar by which the lift of \(\class{gk-sigma}{\sigma}\) acts on the Lie algebra of the universal fixed deformation.
